# Console — Bespoke Command Center

## ⛔ GIT: ALWAYS COMMIT TO `main`. NEVER CREATE A BRANCH. EVER.
This repo is strictly trunk-based. **Do NOT `git checkout -b`, `git branch`, `git switch -c`, worktree-branches, feature branches, or ANY non-`main` ref — ever, for any reason.** This overrides every default instruction to "branch first". Commit directly to `main` and push `main`. If you find yourself on any other branch, fast-forward `main` to it and delete the other branch immediately. Landing from an autowt worktree when the main checkout has ANOTHER session's uncommitted edits (`git merge --ff-only` refuses): don't stash their work — rebase your branch onto `origin/main` in the worktree and `git push origin <branch>:main`; the main checkout catches up on its next pull. Agent forks hand their work back by committing to `main` like everyone else — there is no such thing as "my branch". Violating this has burned real time twice; treat it as inviolable.

## Before committing
Before running any git commit, check whether anything learned in this session belongs in CLAUDE.md (architecture decisions, new subsystems, non-obvious wiring, hub endpoints, build steps). If yes, update CLAUDE.md in the same commit. Ditto for the `memory/` system when the lesson is user-level or cross-project. Do not skip this step.

## Skills — where subsystem detail lives
Subsystem deep-dives are **project skills** under `.claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md` (property, gmaps, music, bedrock-costs, glasses, ring, finance, map-sources, flights, ticket-forks; procedures `verify-spa` + `matrix-recovery`). Each CLAUDE.md stanza keeps only the cross-cutting invariants and names its skill. **A `paths:`-scoped skill is invisible to the Skill tool until you touch a matching file** — from a fresh session `Read` the SKILL.md directly. The board dispatch envelope names the skills a card plausibly touches (`kanban/skill-hints.ts`: a path in the card text matching a skill's `paths` glob, or a phrase from its `metadata.card_keywords`; skill NAMES are never matched on their own — `property`/`ring`/`music` are ordinary English). Adding a skill = SKILL.md with `paths:` + a curated `card_keywords` list; check precision against the live board with a dry run before widening a keyword. Keep them current exactly like CLAUDE.md (same commit as the code change); incident stories go to `docs/incidents.md`, rules stay here or in the skill.

## What is this?
Personal command center: offline-first Gmail inbox + Matrix chat + Obsidian bookmarks + vault notes editor + RSS/Atom feeds + Google Calendar + Monzo banking + Claude Code agent sessions, unified under inbox-zero. No labels, no folders — just fast triage.

## Architecture
- **PWA** — installable standalone, works in any browser including mobile
- **Android APK** (`android/`) — fully-native Kotlin + Jetpack Compose app, boundary at the hub API, offline-first (Room + outbox + WorkManager; WhatsApp model), optional Android launcher. **Everything mobile-specific lives in `android/CLAUDE.md`** (architecture map, release workflow, live-device debugging via `?target=apk`, the bug classes that have bitten us) — it is auto-loaded when you touch `android/`, so no cwd change is needed and one session can land a hub+APK change together. Cross-cutting rules for hub/SPA work: (1) **APK changes batch, hub/SPA changes deploy immediately** — an APK release is cut only when Yousef says "ship"/"release"; (2) **every new agent-protocol message, hub route or `SessionInfo` field needs an APK twin** (`android/.../data/agents/AgentsRepository.kt` for agents, the matching `data/<domain>/` repo otherwise) — file it in `android/BACKLOG.md` "Open" if you don't build it; (3) keep ephemeral broadcasts (`session_todos`, `boards` events) idempotent and stamp `absIndex` on logged agent messages BEFORE broadcasting (the APK upserts by index).
- **Public SPA + public hub via Caddy.** `con.amar.io` resolves to the home WAN IP (Namecheap DDNS — see Al's `ddns-update.md` workflow), Caddy on :443 with a Let's Encrypt cert serves: `/hub/*` → `https://127.0.0.1:9877` (Caddy strips `/hub`, sets `X-Forwarded-Host/Proto/-Prefix`); `/public/*` → `https://127.0.0.1:9877` (prefix preserved, never auth-gated); legacy `/cron.ics` rewrites to `/public/cron.ics`; legacy `/apk/*` rewrites to `/public/apk/*` (for stuck pre-v21 installs); catch-all → `http://127.0.0.1:5173` (Vite). Hub binds `127.0.0.1:9877` — Caddy is the only external ingress; emergency override is `--host 0.0.0.0`. The global Caddy block forces `protocols h1` on `:443` because HTTP/2 → HTTP/1.1 WebSocket bridging was unreliable for fresh upgrades (chrome timed out on new wss opens). **The hub sets `keepAliveTimeout = 75s` / `headersTimeout = 76s`** (`index.ts`): it must outlive Caddy's idle window so Caddy always closes first, and headersTimeout must exceed keepAliveTimeout or Node kills the next request on a reused socket. SPA's `getHubUrl()` returns `${window.location.origin}/hub`. **Different origin = different IndexedDB** — one-time cross-origin migration UI in `AccountModal` ("Import from another origin"), powered by `src/migration.ts` (hidden iframe + postMessage pump). See `memory/project_public_funnel_arch.md` and `memory/reference_caddy_funnel_setup.md`.
- **Hub auth — cookie + bearer.** Single allow-list (`hubAllowedEmails` in `~/.config/console/auth.json`, default `['yousefamar@gmail.com']`). Browser path: SPA hits `${origin}/hub/auth/google/start?return=…`, hub does Google OAuth, callback sets `console_session=<id>; HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Lax; Path=/hub`. Sessions stored in `auth.json` `hubSessions[]`, 30-day TTL. **APK Custom Tab → WebView cookie handoff** via one-time token: hub callback redirects to `console://auth/done?ott=<ott>`; the APK navigates the WebView to `/hub/auth/claim?ott=…` which validates the OTT (single-use, 2-min TTL) and issues a fresh `Set-Cookie` in the WebView's jar. Non-browser path: named bearer tokens (`hubTokens[]`, hashed sha256, individually revocable) sent as `Authorization: Bearer <…>`. Scopes: `cli`, `al`, `apk`, `ring`, `voice`, `webhook`, `other`. CLI + Al read plaintext from `~/.config/console/local-tokens.json` (mode 0600) — auto-minted on hub first boot, separate per scope. APK gets paired via SPA "Pair this APK" → `POST /hub/auth/tokens` → `window.ConsoleNative.setHubToken(…)` → `HubTokenStore.kt` (EncryptedSharedPreferences). PushService attaches the bearer via OkHttp `Request.Builder().header("Authorization", …)` and posts a "needs re-pair" notification on close-4401/4403 or HTTP 401/403 during handshake. **Enforcement** gated by `CONSOLE_AUTH_ENABLED=1`; **break-glass** is `CONSOLE_AUTH_DISABLED=1` (forces allow even if enabled). Both env vars set in pm2's environment. The loopback bypass requires NO `X-Forwarded-*` headers (so Caddy-proxied requests aren't silently treated as same-host). Mutating session-authed requests also pass a `SameSite=Lax`-belt-and-braces `Origin`/`Referer` check (`isCsrfSafe`). Confused-deputy guard: requests carrying BOTH cookie and bearer are rejected. **Pre-auth render gate**: `src/GatedBoot.tsx` is the SPA's only entry point — it probes `/hub/auth/session` first and renders LITERALLY nothing until authenticated; the rest of the app (`App`, sync-bus, stores, panes) is lazy-imported behind the gate so an unauthenticated visitor can't even see pane scaffolding via DOM inspection.
- **Public canvas surface — NO tokens (Yousef's rule).** Each tab/island can be flipped public via `con dashboard canvas {tab,} publish <slug>` (or the Share button on the Home canvas card → `src/components/home/CanvasShareMenu.tsx`) → adds the slug to `~/.config/console/canvas-public.json` (`CanvasPublicRegistry` in `server/src/canvas-public.ts`; sibling of `canvas/` so `con dashboard canvas reset` does NOT silently unpublish — only explicit `unpublish` does). Public URL is the plain slug: `https://con.amar.io/public/<slug>/` (short form, tab-first-then-island; `/public/canvas/<tab|island>/<slug>/` is the explicit fallback for a slug published as both kinds; `canvas`/`apk`/`cron.ics` are reserved slugs) — publish means public, unpublish 404s it. Routes in `server/src/routes/public.ts`: trailing-slash 301, asset path-traversal guard (`path.resolve` ⊂ `tabsDir/<slug>/`), `no-store`/`nosniff`/`no-referrer` HTML headers. Islands are inline-HTML only (no asset paths); tabs serve files from their dir.
- **Unpublished canvas is auth-gated — `/canvas/*` is NOT in the auth exemption list.** `GET /canvas/*` requires a principal; besides session/bearer/loopback, a dedicated read-only cookie `console_canvas` (`HttpOnly; SameSite=None; Secure; Path=/hub/canvas`, value = the hub-session id) authenticates it. Why a second cookie: the SPA canvas iframe is `sandbox="allow-scripts"` (opaque origin), and a TAB canvas's nested tab-frames fetch relative assets FROM that opaque origin — cross-site, so the `SameSite=Lax` session cookie is withheld there; `SameSite=None` is sent. Minted beside the session cookie at the OAuth callback + `/auth/claim`, refreshed on every `GET /auth/session` probe (GatedBoot hits it each load, so existing logins gain it without re-login), cleared on logout. GET-only + path-scoped, so no CSRF surface. Published `/public/canvas/<kind>/<slug>/` shares are a separate always-open path, unaffected. The native APK's Home canvas WebView holds no cookies — it attaches the apk bearer per-request via `shouldInterceptRequest` (HomeScreen.kt). Tests: `server/src/__tests__/canvas-auth.test.ts`.
- **Offline-first** — all mutations (email, chat, calendar) write to IndexedDB first, sync via queue
- **Hub server** — Node.js backend (`server/`) proxies all API calls (Gmail, Calendar, Matrix, Monzo), manages OAuth tokens, hosts agent sessions. Internal HTTPS via any `*.crt`/`*.key` pair in `~/.config/console/` (Caddy terminates the public TLS in front). Binds `127.0.0.1:9877` — all external traffic goes through Caddy.
- **Sub-app isolation** — Layout subscribes only to `activePane`. Each tab component is `React.memo`'d. A chat state change never re-renders the email pane.
- **Pre-rendered panes** — all panes mounted with `display:none`, toggled on selection. `EmailFrame` renders a height-preserving placeholder when `!visible` — live hidden email iframe documents fire recurring ~500 ms browser-internal long tasks that lag typing in OTHER panes (`memory/project_emailframe_iframe_perf.md`).
- **Hub WS heartbeat** — `src/sync-bus.ts` runs a 15 s ping (server already responds at `server/src/sync-bus.ts:95`). 30 s without inbound traffic → `forceReconnect()`. Catches the APK / mobile-Safari "WS half-dead while backgrounded" bug at the source — no visibility-event dependence. See `memory/project_apk_ws_heartbeat.md`.
- **Hub restart from the SPA** — Settings → Hub → Restart (`HubSection` in `AccountModal.tsx`) → `POST /restart` (`server/src/routes/hub.ts`, behind the auth wall): replies 202, then self-SIGTERMs once the reply has flushed — the same graceful `shutdown()` a `pm2 restart` takes, and pm2 autorestart brings the hub back (no pm2-on-PATH dependency). `/health` carries `startedAt` + `pid`; the SPA polls until `startedAt` changes (≤30 s) so the old process answering mid-shutdown never reads as "back". Like every hub change, the route itself only exists after the NEXT restart — a 404 from the button means restart once via `con hub restart` first.

## Tech Stack
React 19, TypeScript, Vite, Zustand, Dexie.js (IndexedDB), Tiptap 3 (email compose), CodeMirror 6 + vim (notes), Tailwind CSS 4, Lucide React, Day.js, Vitest, ws (WebSocket), @matrix-org/matrix-sdk-crypto-wasm (E2EE)

## Project Structure
```
src/
  main.tsx, App.tsx, hub.ts, notifications.ts, debug-agent.ts
  db/          — Dexie schema + offline sync queue
  gmail/       — Auth (hub-based), API, sync, types
  matrix/      — Auth, API, crypto (E2EE), sync, key-backup
  calendar/    — API (hub-proxied), accounts, types, sync (offline queue)
  store/       — Zustand stores: inbox, chat, calendar, bookmarks, notes, feeds, money, agent, ui
  hooks/       — useKeybindings, useSync, useMediaQuery, useSwipeActions
  components/  — All UI components (Layout, *Tab, *View, *List, etc.)

  utils/       — Email, date, HTML helpers
server/
  src/
    index.ts         — HTTP/HTTPS + WebSocket server, route dispatch
    session.ts       — Claude CLI subprocess manager
    auth-store.ts    — OAuth token manager (~/.config/console/auth.json)

    routes/          — auth, mail, calendar, matrix, monzo, bookmarks, feeds, notes, agents, debug
cli/               — `con` CLI tool (81 commands, self-describing)
android/           — native Kotlin+Compose APK; OWN CHARTER in android/CLAUDE.md (auto-loaded when you
                     touch anything under android/ — read it first). BACKLOG.md = work ledger + release log,
                     FEATURES.md = SPA parity inventory. Never restructure/describe it here; keep it there.
```

## Debugging (PRIMARY METHOD)

**Use the embedded debug agent, NOT browser DevTools or Chrome MCP tools.**

The app has a built-in debug agent (`src/debug-agent.ts`) that streams all console output, network requests, and errors to the hub in real-time. Claude Code observes via HTTP endpoints:

```bash
# See recent events (console logs, network requests, errors)
curl -sk https://localhost:9877/debug/log?n=30

# Filter for errors
curl -sk https://localhost:9877/debug/log?n=100 | jq '.[] | select(.cat=="error")'

# Filter for failed network requests
curl -sk https://localhost:9877/debug/log?n=100 | jq '.[] | select(.cat=="net" and .status >= 400)'

# Execute JS in the browser (replaces Chrome MCP javascript_tool)
# ALWAYS pass ?target=chrome — with the APK also connected, an untargeted eval
# can hit its native debug agent, which errors "unknown command" on any JS
# (looks like a syntax problem, is a routing problem).
curl -sk -X POST 'https://localhost:9877/debug/eval?target=chrome' -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"code":"document.title"}'

# Get Zustand store state
curl -sk https://localhost:9877/debug/state

# Check if debug agent is connected
curl -sk https://localhost:9877/debug/status
```

The debug agent:
- Captures from page load (imported first in `main.tsx`)
- Survives navigation (WebSocket auto-reconnects)
- Works across all tabs/devices (all stream to same hub)
- Logs to `~/.config/console/debug.log` (NDJSON, auto-rotated at 5K lines)
- `window.__console` (dev-only) exposes Zustand stores + Dexie db for browser console access
- **Eval-probe trap**: `import('/src/store/x.ts')` from `/debug/eval` can resolve a DIFFERENT module instance than the app's after HMR churn (empty-looking stores that aren't). Probe via `window.__console.stores` or the DOM, never a fresh dynamic import
- **Isolated SPA verification from a worktree (never Yousef's live tab)**: `npx vite --config vite.verify.config.ts --port 5174` + Playwright against the live hub. The full recipe and every known gotcha (auth-probe shim, setState injection, lazy panes, recurring writers, headless dictation, screenshots) is the **`verify-spa` skill** (`/verify-spa`, user-invocable) — load it before live-verifying UI.

## Key Patterns

### SPA reads a new hub field → optional-guard it, always
Vite HMR ships the SPA reader immediately; the hub writer only ships at the next restart. In that window the live hub serves payloads WITHOUT the new field, and an unguarded access (`d.tags.includes(…)`) crashes the whole React tree, not just one component ("screen goes dark"). Rule: any new field on a hub payload consumed by the SPA gets `?? []` / `?.` at the point of use, no exceptions.

### Prefs: a FAILED load must never look like a loaded one
`src/prefs.ts` is the hub-backed pref cache (`GET/PUT /config`); `isPrefsLoaded()` is the guard every automatic writer uses to avoid persisting a default over the user's real value. `initPrefs()` resolves only on a SUCCESSFUL load, retries with backoff (2/5/15/30/60 s), leaves `isPrefsLoaded()` false until one lands, and grafts newer local writes over a late-arriving hub copy (`localWrites`, the `auth-store` graft lesson). `prefsReady()` therefore never resolves while the hub is unreachable — callers race their own timeout and must not write on that path. Symptom of a regression: *"my settings keep resetting, and then the WRONG ones persist"* — for ANY pref.
- **Corollary for any pref derived from in-memory state**: a Zustand store is created at import time, BEFORE the pref cache is populated (App hydrates it a few ticks later), so a write derived from the store's own state in that window persists a set missing everything. `registerOverlaySource` seeds from `persisted ∪ in-memory` so a register can only ever ADD. **But never DECIDE against an unloaded cache** (^tame-ibis, 2026-09-08): with prefs pending, an empty `overlaySeen` made every overlay look first-seen, the store added it to the in-memory set, and App hydration + `fetchCalendars` unioned that guess over the saved pref — a hidden Meetup came back on every slow boot/restart. Overlay visibility is now settled only after `prefsReady()` (`settleOverlayVisibility`), hydration replaces with `saved ∪ first-seen`, and no path unions the in-memory set over the saved one.
- **Component state backed by a pref = `usePref(key, fallback)`** (`src/prefs.ts`), never `useState(() => getPref(…))`: panes are pre-rendered before `initPrefs()` resolves, so the initialiser reads the empty cache and the saved value never shows (the Inbox day-rail collapse silently reset on every reload until ^blue-bass). The hook re-reads once `prefsReady()` lands and follows later `setPref`s of the key.

### Auth
- **Hub-managed OAuth** — all tokens stored server-side in `~/.config/console/auth.json`. Frontend bootstraps via `GET /auth/token`. Sign-in opens hub's `/auth/google/start` in a popup.
- **Multi-account** — hub stores multiple Google accounts. Calendar sidebar can add accounts via OAuth flow. **Adding an ADDITIONAL account must pass `?add=1`** to `/auth/google/start` — that switches `prompt` from `consent` to `select_account consent`. Without it Google silently re-consents whichever account the browser is already signed into, so "Add calendar account" just re-links the existing account and the new one never appears. The plain sign-in path deliberately stays `consent` (no chooser).
- **Token refresh** — hub proactively refreshes tokens 5min before expiry. Frontend re-fetches from hub on 401.

### Offline Sync Queue
- **Shared queue** — `src/db/sync-queue.ts` provides `enqueue()`, `markDone()`, `markFailed()` for all services
- **Action types** — email (`archive`, `send`, etc.), chat (`chatSend`, `chatMarkRead`), calendar (`calCreate`, `calUpdate`, `calDelete`, `calRsvp`, `calReminder`, `calLocation`)
- **Pattern** — optimistic IDB write → `set()` for instant UI → `enqueue()` → background processor flushes within 500ms
- **Retry** — 3 retries before marking `failed`. Queue survives page reload.
- **Undo** — delete actions show 5-second undo toast. Undo removes from queue + restores IDB.

### Email
- Optimistic archive/snooze with undo. Auto-archive on reply. Pre-rendered iframes. CID inline images.
- Snooze is local-only (Gmail has no snooze API). Conflict detection before sending queued replies.
- Send-as aliases fetched during sync. Calendar invites parsed from `text/calendar`.
- **Gmail labels: an id with no name is DROPPED, never shown raw.** The id→name map (`src/gmail/labels.ts`) refreshes on boot and self-heals when an incremental sync stores a thread wearing an id the map can't name; Mail rows and Inbox items both render through `MailLabels`. Before ^zany-fox (7 Oct 2026) only `fullSync()` wrote the map — i.e. a hard reset or an expired historyId — so every label made since the last reset showed as `Label_38` for the life of the browser profile. General rule: **a derived lookup table must refresh on the incremental path, not just the full one.** The APK reads its map on every thread-list change for the same reason (`MailScreens.kt`).

### Chat (Matrix)
- **Hub-owned room snapshot.** The canonical view of every joined room (name, isUnread, unreadCount, avatar, memberCount, tags, snoozedUntil, lastMessage*) lives server-side in `server/src/matrix/chat-rooms-store.ts` (a `SnapshotStore` wrapper from `server/src/snapshot-store.ts`). Every `/sync` delta computes the row via `server/src/matrix/room-state.ts` (port of the SPA's old `processJoinedRoom` logic) and the store broadcasts on the `chat-rooms` service. Browser-side `src/matrix/chat-rooms-subscribe.ts` mirrors snapshot + deltas straight into `db.chatRooms`. Replaces per-device derivation — "mark read on PC → phone sees it" works without local divergence. RPCs registered under `chat-rooms`: `snapshot`, `markRead`, `markUnread`, `snooze`. **Manual unread is durable** (`RoomState.manualUnread`): "mark unread" has no homeserver concept, so the sticky flag forces `isUnread` true across recomputes and is cleared only by an explicit `markRead` (`setRoomRead`) — never by a sync reporting `notification_count=0` (a post-restart catch-up sync touches enough rooms to wipe every marker otherwise). **"Someone said something" = `isConversationEvent()` in `room-state.ts` (`m.room.message` OR `m.sticker`), never a bare `m.room.message` check** — the preview/unread/SLA loop and sync's push broadcast both filtered on the message type alone, so a bridged sticker left the thread read and silent (^neat-heron); `previewBody()` supplies "Sticker" for the two-thirds of real stickers with an empty `body`. **An optimistic Dexie write whose RPC fails must not stand**: the SPA's snapshot reconcile is seq-based, so a local row can diverge from the hub with seqs equal and "the hub will reconcile on the next snapshot" is FALSE (Baba's draft vanished for a day, 2026-09-16). Page load now does a FULL reconcile (`reconcileFromHub({full:true})`), and every `chat.ts` optimistic RPC (`markUnread`/`snooze`/`unsnooze`/`setDraft`) calls `healAfterFailedRpc()` on failure — never leave a bare `catch {}` on an optimistic write. **Timestamps in `computeRoomState` (^rare-deer)**: a bridged message carries WhatsApp's ORIGINAL send time, which can predate the portal room's own state events by seconds — so the first real message in a room ALWAYS wins the preview (`hadPreview = !!existing.lastMessageSender`), the unread divider is never seeded from a state-event time, and state events dedupe by (type, state_key) (a join echoed in `state` and `timeline` inflated DMs to memberCount 3). A new contact's first message otherwise arrives read with a blank row. `SWEEP_VERSION` in `index.ts` bumps whenever a room-state rule changes so the boot sweep re-derives every room. **Mutes (^tall-ant)**: a `room`-kind push rule mutes when its actions carry no `notify` — `mutedRoomsFromRules` in `sync.ts` accepts BOTH the legacy `dont_notify` (what Console writes) and the spec's current empty `actions: []` (what Beeper's clients/bridges write; the phone-side mute of Rayyan's DM was invisible for months because only the former was parsed), and the boot seed pushes the set into the snapshot (`setMutedRoomIds`) rather than waiting for the next `m.push_rules` change. **A muted room's `notification_count` is 0 by construction and is NOT a read signal**: `computeRoomState` ignores `unread_notifications` for rooms in `ctx.mutedRoomIds` and takes "read elsewhere" from MY OWN `m.read`/`m.read.private` receipt at-or-after the newest message (Beeper double-puppets a phone read as one) — the old explicit-zero path flipped every new message in a muted DM to read within the second (the sender's own ghost receipt was the next delta), so unmuting revealed nothing. Un-muted rooms keep the count-based rule unchanged. The tick captures `pushRulesChanged` BEFORE clearing the flag — the `setMutedRoomIds` block used to re-read the already-cleared field and was dead code, so an unmute never reached the snapshot until that room's next delta. **The HTTP room-state routes go through `matrixSync`**: `POST /matrix/rooms/:id/read|unread|snooze` call `markRead`/`markUnread`/`snooze` (the same methods as the SPA's `chat-rooms.*` RPCs) — `unread` and `snooze` were stubs answering `{ok:true}` without touching anything, so `con chat mark-unread` / `snooze` "succeeded" for months and did nothing, and `read` sent a raw receipt without the optimistic snapshot flip. `snooze` takes `{until}` = ISO | `+30m|+2h|+1d` | epoch ms | `none` (`parseSnoozeUntil`); an unknown room is a 404 (`requireRoom`), never a silent no-op. `GET /matrix/rooms` (`con chat rooms`) still lists from a cold homeserver `/sync` but takes `isUnread`/`unreadCount`/`isMuted` from the snapshot (`matrixSync.roomSnapshot`) where it knows the room — the raw count is zero forever for muted rooms and blind to mark-unread. Tests: `matrix-mute-sync.test.ts`, `matrix-push-rules.test.ts`.
- **Per-room drafts are hub-owned (`RoomState.draft` + `draftUpdatedAt`, ^bold-lynx)**, not composer-local: the composer is ONE shared textarea across rooms, so the text lives on the room row like `snoozedUntil` — `ChatRoomsStore.setRoomDraft` (empty clears; `computeRoomState` carries both fields through every recompute; `setRoomRead` leaves them alone — reading is not discarding), RPC `chat-rooms.setDraft {roomId, text}` (SPA) and HTTP `GET|PUT|DELETE /matrix/rooms/:id/draft` + `GET /matrix/drafts` (CLI `con chat draft <room> [--body|--file|--stdin] [--clear]`, `con chat drafts`) — **the agent seam: an agent writes a reply for Yousef WITHOUT sending, he reviews it in the chat and sends or discards**. **A drafted room is live** (Chat list + Inbox `roomIsLive`, Chat badge, Kotlin `InboxLogic.roomIsLive`): listed whatever its read/mute/low-priority state until the draft is sent or cleared — only snooze hides it; the row's preview becomes amber `Draft: …`, the Inbox row shows the draft as its body + a `draft` chip and ranks by the draft edit time, and `e` there DISCARDS the draft (5 s undo) + marks read (`discardDraftAndMarkRead`) — a bare mark-read would leave the row live and it would pop straight back. Composer (`ChatComposeInput.tsx`): `useRoomDraft` (Dexie `liveQuery` on the room row, stamped with its roomId so a switch never hydrates the new room with the old text; re-renders only when the string changes), **the draft is written ONLY on textarea blur** (`setRoomDraft` — optimistic Dexie + RPC; also room switch, pagehide, unmount, send/empty clears) — **never while typing** (^cool-ibis: a 400 ms typing debounce made the room list re-sort/re-preview on every pause and a hub delta computed before the write landed echoed the PREVIOUS text back; Yousef: "only set the draft on blur"), so the Send button `preventDefault`s mousedown to keep focus (a click would otherwise write-then-clear); edit mode never persists and restores the draft on exit; remote changes (agent/another device) replace the textarea only when nothing unsaved is typed, a first read landing after typing began keeps the typed text, and a remote value arriving within 1.5 s of our own write is ignored (the stale-echo case above). `flushDraft` compares against the Dexie mirror, not its own last push, so a push the hub dropped is retried on the next flush. Android: the Inbox gate + entry body read `draft` from `rawJson` (no schema change); the composer twin is `data/chat/ChatDraftSync.kt` (same rules, pure + tested) wired in `ChatRoomScreen` — see `android/BACKLOG.md`. Tests: `server/src/__tests__/chat-drafts.test.ts`, the draft cases in `room-state.test.ts`, `src/__tests__/inbox-route.test.ts`, `InboxLogicTest.kt`.
- **Editing a sent message from the CLI: `con chat edit <room> <event-id> --body "…"`** (^wavy-kite, 2026-09-20 — the stale Baba greeting was sent before AL swapped the draft and nothing could fix it) → `POST /matrix/rooms/:id/edit {eventId, body, html?, waitMs?}` → `MatrixSync.editMessage`: fetches the target and refuses anything not sent by us or not a message, then sends the same `m.replace` shape as the SPA's `editMessage` (outer `body: " * new"`, `m.new_content`, `m.relates_to`) through `sendRoomEvent` (encryption auto-detected). **On a bridged room (`RoomState.networkIcon` set) it waits up to `waitMs` (10 s) for the bridge's `com.beeper.message_send_status` referencing the edit** — `tick()` feeds every decrypted timeline event to `noteBridgeSendStatus`, PENDING is ignored, statuses arriving before the PUT returns are buffered while a send is armed — and returns it as `bridge: {status, reason, error, network}`. The WhatsApp bridge only forwards edits inside WhatsApp's ~15-min window; after that it answers `FAIL_RETRIABLE` with `reason: com.beeper.unsupported_event`, `error: "the message is too old to be edited"` (measured 2026-09-20 on a 3-day-old message — NOT `FAIL_PERMANENT`/`m.too_old`, and it never becomes deliverable however often it retries, so treat status ≠ SUCCESS as final); the CLI turns any non-SUCCESS verdict into exit `BRIDGE_REJECTED` spelling out that the Matrix edit landed but the recipient still sees the original. A successful edit's verdict arrives in ~2 s. `con chat messages` (TTY) prints one line per event, event id first, local time, oldest at the top, so ids can be pasted into `edit`/`react`. Tests: `server/src/__tests__/matrix-edit-message.test.ts`.
- Direct REST API (no matrix-js-sdk). E2EE via OlmMachine WASM. Long-polling sync.
- Local echo with `~timestamp.random` IDs. Bridge detection (WhatsApp, LinkedIn, Slack).
- Pre-rendered room views. Pinned favourites grid. Edit/delete diffs via `diffWords`.
- **Send failures surface in the UI**: Beeper bridges emit `com.beeper.message_send_status` events referencing the original encrypted event. Browser-side `src/matrix/sync.ts` flips `sendFailed` on the local echo and fires a notification. SUCCESS status clears any prior fail marker. Root cause of FAIL_RETRIABLE is almost always the hub device not being cross-signed — run `POST /matrix/keys/restore-cross-signing` (see Matrix recovery workflow below).
- **Force-rotate Megolm**: `matrix.rotateRoomKey({roomId})` RPC (→ `HubMatrixCrypto.invalidateRoomKey`) throws away the outbound session so the next send re-keys from scratch. Useful when a bridge gets into a wedged state. Note: `invalidateGroupSession` only touches the in-memory session, so we pre-load by calling `shareRoomKey(roomId, [], …)` first — otherwise after a hub restart the persisted stale session stays live.
- **Resume semantics (gap recovery)**: the hub runs one /sync loop and broadcasts `matrix.delta` over SyncBus — fire-and-forget, no per-client buffer. Reconciliation uses Matrix's **native** resumable-sync primitive: the client persists `next_batch` from every ingested delta (in `meta` table under `matrix:lastBatch`), and hands it back to the hub via `matrix.resume({since})` RPC on any wake signal. Hub calls `/sync?since=<token>&timeout=0`, decrypts, returns the delta directly (point-to-point, not broadcast). Trigger points all converge on a single debounced `reconcile()` in `src/hooks/useSync.ts`: (1) `hubBus.onConnect`, (2) `document.visibilitychange → visible` (critical for APK — the WebView pauses while backgrounded so WS stays "up" from JS's perspective and onConnect never fires), (3) `window.online`. `bulkPut` on event_id makes re-ingestion idempotent, so racing live-delta + resume is safe. If the homeserver rejects the `since` (expired), hub falls back to initial sync and flags `isInitial` on the response. `processJoinedRoom` gates `lastMessageBody`/`lastMessageTime` behind `advancesPreview` (newest-by-timestamp > existing) so out-of-order resume ingestion can't roll a room preview back to an older message. **A resume batch can carry an event AND its relation in one pass** — any relation handler (`m.replace` edits, reactions) must patch the in-flight `messages` array first and only fall back to `db.chatMessages.update` (which silently no-ops on a not-yet-persisted row; the `bulkPut` at the end then stores the pre-relation state).
- **Limited-timeline backfill**: Matrix `/sync` caps each room's timeline at ~10 events and sets `limited: true` + `prev_batch` when more happened during the gap. The APK WebView gets paused while backgrounded — on resume, its `since` token is far behind and sync returns many truncated rooms, so without backfill the local cursor jumps past real messages. Hub-side `backfillLimitedTimelines()` in `server/src/matrix/sync.ts` walks `/rooms/{id}/messages?from=prev_batch&dir=b` (limit 100) for each limited room, reverses to forward order, decrypts, and **prepends** the gap events before broadcasting the delta. Runs on both `resume()` (skipped on `isInitial`, since the full initial sync has no gaps) and `tick()` (live loop). Idempotent via `bulkPut(event_id)` client-side.
- **Redaction tombstones (bridges)**: WhatsApp / Signal delete events come through as `m.room.encrypted` with `content: {}` (the original ciphertext stripped) plus `unsigned.redacted_because.{sender,type:"m.room.redaction"}`. Without special handling they render as "🔒 Encrypted message" placeholders. Fix lives in `src/matrix/sync.ts`: `eventToMessage` returns `null` for tombstones, and `processJoinedRoom` has a pre-loop guard that flips any existing row's `isDeleted=true` + `deletedBy=<redactor>` when a tombstone for it arrives. Detect via `event.type === 'm.room.encrypted' && typeof event.content?.ciphertext !== 'string'`. **Explicit `m.room.redaction` events (Slack over Beeper) are a SEPARATE path with a pre-v11 shape**: hungryserv puts the target ONLY at the event's top level (`.redacts`), content is just `{reason}` — so the hub's field-by-field delta re-shape (`decryptJoinedRoomsWithCounts`) must preserve `redacts` on `MatrixEventLike` or every client loses the target. Client extraction is centralised in `src/matrix/redaction.ts` `redactionTargetId()` (content-first, top-level fallback); both client redaction handlers patch in-flight batch rows before `db.update`. When adding fields to the delta re-shape: it's an explicit allow-list — a top-level Matrix field not listed there is dropped for all clients.
- **Soft-delete-only guarantee (message archive)**: Console NEVER hard-deletes chat content — deletes by any party only ever mark rows `isDeleted`; original text + media stay recoverable. `server/src/matrix/message-archive.ts` (`MessageArchive`, `~/.config/console/chat-archive/`): append-only NDJSON per room (`events/<sha1(roomId)>.ndjson`, dedup-by-first-record so the pre-redaction copy wins) + `media/<sha1(eventId)>.bin` decrypted-attachment rescue. Wired in `MatrixSync.decryptJoinedRoomsWithCounts` (live sync) and `paginate` (history): every decrypted message-bearing event is archived BEFORE downstream processing; observing a redaction (explicit `m.room.redaction` or encrypted tombstone) triggers `recordRedaction` → downloads + AES-CTR-decrypts the attachment to disk (blobs outlive event redaction on Beeper). The archive class has deliberately NO delete/prune API. Surface: `chat-rooms.archivedEvent {roomId,eventId}` RPC (pre-redaction copy) + `GET /matrix/archive/media/<sha1>.bin`. SPA `DeletedMessageBody` in `ChatMessageBubble.tsx` renders deleted messages by falling back to the archive (struck-through original text, recovered image inline). Limit: content the hub never saw (deleted before Console ever synced the room) is unrecoverable — the homeserver strips it.
- **@-mention autocomplete** (`src/components/ChatComposeInput.tsx`). `@<query>` only opens the picker when the `@` is at start-of-input or after whitespace (so emails like `alice@example.com` don't trigger). Surviving `@DisplayName` tokens at send time become matrix.to anchor tags in `formatted_body` and userIds in an `m.mentions` block per MSC3952 — Element / Cinny / bridges treat the message as an intentional ping. Deleting the `@token` before send removes the userId from the m.mentions block. Room-members cache lives in `src/matrix/room-members.ts` (module-level, primed via `/matrix/rooms/:id/info`).
- **Hub Megolm backup upload (auto)**: on every boot, `server/src/index.ts` calls `hubMatrixCrypto.activateBackupUpload(homeserver, token)` which GETs `/room_keys/version`, extracts the public key, and calls `enableBackupV1(pub, ver)` — encryption-only, no recovery key needed. From that point on every new Megolm session the hub receives via to-device is auto-uploaded through `processOutgoingRequests` → `/room_keys/keys`, so a later hub re-init can restore everything. A one-shot `backupPendingRoomKeys()` sweep runs after activation to upload any sessions imported from the local M0 backup or received while backup was inactive. Boot log line: `[hub-crypto] backup upload activated (version N)` followed by `[hub-crypto] backed up N pending room keys` if any were outstanding.

### Calendar
- **Hub-proxied** — all API calls go through hub (`/cal/*`), which handles tokens server-side
- **Which calendars are Yousef's = `server/src/cal/visibility.ts`, the ONE rule for every hub fan-out over "all calendars"** (^lime-lark, 2026-09-22). Every linked Google account is his, but each account's calendarList also carries calendars other people shared with him — as a Workspace admin he holds `accessRole: owner` on colleagues' primaries (`sam@artanis.ai`, `olly@artanis.ai`), unticked in Google Calendar. **Google OMITS `selected` when false**, so `selected !== false` treats every unticked calendar as visible — that gate leaked Sam's private meetup into three late-check alerts. `isVisibleCalendar` = `selected === true && !hidden && !deleted`; `visibleCalendars(entries)` dedupes across accounts keeping the most-access copy (primary, then owner>writer>reader>freeBusyReader — the same `bestApiAccount` rule as the SPA store, so the stamped `accountEmail` is the token the APK may write back through); `isOwnCalendar` (primary / his address / an owner-role `@group.calendar.google.com` — never a person's address that isn't his); `attendsEvent` (invited under one of his addresses and not declined, else own calendar with no `self` decline — `attendees[].self` marks the CALENDAR owner, so on Sam's calendar it means Sam). Consumers: `listVisibleCalendars`/`listAllEvents` in `routes/calendar.ts` (= `GET /cal/events` without `calendarId`, the APK's reconcile fan-out; events carry `calendarId`, `accountEmail`, `accessRole`), late-check via `listAllEvents(…, { attending: true })`, `cal/sync.ts` reminders. `GET /cal/calendars` stays UNFILTERED — the SPA sidebar needs the toggles and filters client-side (`src/store/calendar.ts`). Tests `__tests__/cal-visibility.test.ts` carry the live 13-entry list shape + the Sam event. **Client ports of `isVisibleCalendar` — keep the three in sync (^odd-bat)**: SPA `src/calendar/google-visibility.ts` `isShownInGoogle` (the store's list gate), Android `data/cal/CalVisibility.kt` `isSelectedCalendar` (reconcile keeps only those rows in `cal_list` and never caches another calendar's events; `eventTitle` renders a detail-stripped event on a `reader`/`freeBusyReader` calendar as "Busy", the way Google does).
- **Offline-first** — all mutations (create, update, delete, RSVP, reminder, location) are optimistic with sync queue
- **Temp IDs** — created events use `~timestamp.random` IDs, replaced with real Google IDs after queue processes
- **An optimistic calendar row is the ONLY copy of what the user typed until it reaches Google — every path that deletes calendar rows must exempt it.** `fetchEvents`'s stale-reap exempts queue rows in `pending`/`processing`/`failed`/`conflict` AND any `~`-prefixed temp id (`isTempEventId` — the guard that survives a reload); only calendars whose fetch FULFILLED are `reapable` (a rejected `allSettled` fetch is not "Google deleted everything"); a permanently failed `calCreate`/`calLocation` rolls back and toasts (`notifyFailure`); `CalendarEventForm` keeps the form open on error ("Retry"); Ctrl-refresh preserves `~`-id rows; `createEvent` passes `tempCompoundKey` as the hub `DedupStore` `clientToken`. Note a deleted Google event loses its `summary`, so `updatedMin` is the only usable forensic sweep (`/cal/events` forwards no `showDeleted`). Known gap: `markDone` deletes queue rows (no audit trail) and `db.queue` has no `eventCompoundKey` index. Memory: `project_calendar_optimistic_loss.md`.
- **A calendar reachable through several accounts resolves to ONE row** (`fetchCalendars` dedup in `src/store/calendar.ts`). `accountEmail` = the account it displays under; **`apiAccountEmail` = whose token to actually call with**, picked by `ACCESS_RANK {owner:3,writer:2,reader:1,freeBusyReader:0}` with an override when the calendar id equals an account's email (that account is the owner). Every mutation path uses `apiAccountEmail`. **GOTCHA: `accessRole` must be copied from that winning account's row too** — it describes what the token being used can do. A calendar shared read-only into one account but OWNED by another connected account must report the owner's role, or `isOwner` in `CalendarEventPopover` and the `writableCalIds` drag gate stay shut despite the hub holding an owner token.
  - **The stale-reap keys on `calendarId` ALONE, never `accountEmail:calendarId`.** `apiAccountEmail` promotion re-keys every row in that calendar (`fetchEvents` stores rows under the *fetching* account), so account-qualified reaping leaves the old rows permanently unreapable and every event renders twice. The rejected-fetch protection is per-calendar, so dropping the account qualifier loses nothing.
  - **Losing a Google account silently un-promotes its calendars back to `reader`; the only visible symptom is that they vanish from the new-event picker** (`CalendarEventForm` renders `calendars.filter(accessRole === 'owner' || 'writer')`) — everything else keeps working, so it reads as "I can't create events on that calendar any more". `refreshGoogleToken` never removes an account: `AuthStore.removeGoogleAccount` is the sole mutator, reached from `POST /auth/logout/google` (**removes ALL accounts when the body has no `account` field** — what the SPA `signOut()` and the APK Settings sign-out send) and `DELETE /cal/accounts/:email`; all three log the caller (store: stack-trace slice; routes: UA). The APK calendar sheet's per-account Delete sits behind an AlertDialog spelling out the un-promotion consequence.
  - **Never construct an `AuthStore` outside `index.ts`** — `save()` writes the WHOLE in-memory config (last-writer-wins, no merge), so a second process holding one reverts every change the hub has made since it booted; a script needing tokens calls the hub over HTTP. Defences in `auth-store.ts`: the Google/Monzo/Spotify `scheduleRefresh` timers are `.unref()`'d (merely constructing a store can't pin a process's event loop); `save()` is atomic (`.tmp` + `renameSync`); and when the on-disk mtime is newer than our last write it **grafts newer disk keys before overwriting** (`graftNewerKeys`, `__tests__/auth-graft.test.ts` — additive-only, never overwrites a held key, leaves a present-but-`undefined` intentional clear alone; caveat: a live orphan can still bounce a removed key back). **Diagnosis when `auth.json` keeps reverting**: `ps -eo pid,lstart,etime,args | grep tsx` — any `scripts/*.ts` with a multi-day `etime` is the clobberer; `fuser`/`lsof` on `auth.json` is useless (`writeFileSync` holds no fd). Memory: `project_authstore_orphan_clobber.md`.
- **Private per-event links (`con cal link|unlink|links|linked`, ^gray-bat).** Google has NO user-facing private-notes field — description, attachments and the Meet notes doc are shared with every guest. The one private channel is `extendedProperties.private`: key/values stored on YOUR copy of the event, never propagated to other attendees, never rendered by any Google UI (limits: key ≤44 chars, value ≤1024 — silently truncated — 300 props / 32 KB per event). `server/src/calendar-links.ts` (pure) lays links out as `console.link.<i>` + a constant marker `console.links=1`; routes `GET|POST|DELETE /cal/events/:id/links` (matched BEFORE the bare `/cal/events/:id` regexes), `GET /cal/linked` (server-side `events.list?privateExtendedProperty=console.links=1` across writable calendars — the filter is exact `key=value`, which is why the marker carries no count), and `GET /cal/events/:id` returns a derived `links[]`. Proven live 2026-09-08 on a throwaway event: a PATCH with `key: null` DELETES the private key while omitted keys persist, so every rewrite sends the whole set with vacated keys nulled. CLI stores an existing relative path as absolute (an unambiguous pointer); URLs/vault-relative/plain text go in verbatim. A link is a string — the vault note or file behind it is not touched. **SPA**: `src/calendar/links.ts` is the client port (same key grammar — keep in sync); `toDbEvent` derives `linksJson` from the fetched event's `extendedProperties`, `fromDbEvent` exposes `CalendarEvent.links`; the event popover's `LinksBlock` lists them (vault note → opens in Spaces Docs via `openVaultFile()` + inline peek of the first 40 lines from `/notes/file/`; image → bridge thumbnail + lightbox; pdf/video → bridge in a new tab; URL → new tab; anything else → copy path), `+`/`×` call `linkEvent`/`unlinkEvent` (optimistic Dexie row, DIRECT hub call — not the outbox — revert + `showAlert` on failure). `/notes/vault-path` tells the SPA the absolute vault dir so absolute paths under it classify as vault notes. CLI `con cal links --cat` prints local text-file links. Android: `data/cal/EventLinks.kt` is the Kotlin port (same key grammar — a third copy to keep in sync), detail-sheet block in `EventDetailSheet.kt`. Tests: `server …/calendar-links.test.ts`, `src/__tests__/calendar-links.test.ts`.
- Custom week/day grid. Drag-to-create/move/resize. Recurring event dialog. Event merging across calendars.
- **Declined events render struck-through + faded (`opacity-45` + `line-through`), NOT hidden** (Yousef's call: he wants to see them and *delete* them to hide — never auto-hidden). The `declined` flag (self attendee `responseStatus === 'declined'`) lives on the positioned-event shapes in `CalendarGrid.tsx` (timed events + AllDayBar) and `CalendarMonth.tsx` (pills + spans); merged duplicates are declined only if EVERY copy is declined. The hub reminder ticker (`server/src/cal/sync.ts` `cacheReminder`) still skips declined events so they don't ping.
- **Recurring delete has GCal's three scopes** (`DeleteScopeDialog` in `CalendarEventPopover.tsx`, shown when the event has a `recurringEventId`): *this event* = plain per-instance `calDelete`; *this and following* / *all* = optimistic bulk-remove of the series' local instance rows + ONE queued `calDeleteFollowing` action keyed on the **master** id. The processor (`src/calendar/sync.ts`) fetches the master and either PATCHes a truncated recurrence (`truncateRecurrence` in `src/calendar/recurrence.ts`, unit-tested — UNTIL stamped just before the cut instance, COUNT dropped, UNTIL's value type must match DTSTART's date vs date-time) or deletes the master outright (all-scope, or a cut at/before the first instance). Instances arrive via `singleEvents:true` so the master exists only on Google — `recurrence` lines are fetched at process time, never stored.
- **Read-only overlay sources** (`useCalendarStore.registerOverlaySource(id, info, events)` / `unregisterOverlaySource(id)`): a generic seam for showing non-Google, non-persisted events in the grid. The `CalendarInfo` must carry `synthetic: true` (skipped by `fetchEvents` so there's no bogus Google fetch for a fake account) + `accessRole: 'reader'` (gates the grid drag/resize + popover edit/delete — all keyed on accessRole, never on a specific id). Events are merged in `loadEventsFromDb` (same range+visibility filter as Dexie events), live only in memory (`overlaySources`), and the source's `id` must be unique per event (no Dexie compoundKey). First registration defaults the source visible (`OVERLAY_SEEN_PREF`). **GOTCHA: `OVERLAY_SEEN_PREF` and `VISIBLE_CAL_IDS_PREF` must be written ATOMICALLY** — both gated on `isPrefsLoaded()`. Marking seen without persisting visibility left the overlay seen-but-absent-from-visibleIds → treated as user-hidden → its sidebar toggle silently vanished on every subsequent boot. Visibility for a SEEN overlay is exactly what the saved set says (absent = toggled off, survives reloads/restarts); the decision is deferred to `prefsReady()` when a source registers before `/config` lands (`settleOverlayVisibility`), and `fetchCalendars`/App hydration only ever add FIRST-SEEN overlays on top of the saved set — never the in-memory set (^tame-ibis). All synthetic infos group under ONE "Overlays" bucket in `CalendarSidebar` (keyed `' overlays'`, not per fake accountEmail). Consumers: **Meetup** (`src/meetup/calendar-overlay.ts` — `meetupEventToCalendarEvent` + `wireMeetupCalendarOverlay()` subscribing to `useMapStore.events`) and **Eventbrite** (below).
- **Eventbrite organiser overlay.** Feed: `GET /v3/organizers/{id}/events/?status=live&order_by=start_asc&expand=venue` with Yousef's PERSONAL OAuth token (`page_size` is rejected with ARGUMENTS_ERROR — paginate via `pagination.continuation`). Hub `server/src/eventbrite.ts` `EventbriteStore` (config `~/.config/console/eventbrite.json` 0600 = `{token, organizers:[{id,name}]}` — token never in the repo and never in a response; 6h TTL cache; `parseEventbriteRef` accepts an organiser URL, ANY of the organiser's event URLs (resolved via `/events/{id}/?expand=organizer`), or a bare id) + `routes/eventbrite.ts` (`GET /eventbrite/{status,events}`, `POST /eventbrite/organizers {ref}`, `DELETE /eventbrite/organizers/:id`, `POST /eventbrite/token`). SPA `src/eventbrite/calendar-overlay.ts` (wired in GatedBoot beside Meetup) registers the read-only synthetic `eventbrite` calendar (#f05537) with the events' REAL end times; boot retry backoff + reconnect + 6h. CLI `con cal eventbrite {status|events [--force]|follow <url|id>|unfollow <id>|token <t>}`. Calendar-only (no Map pins). Android: `CalOverlays.eventbriteEventRow` beside Meetup.
- **Meetup client mirror is snapshot-authoritative** (`src/meetup/subscribe.ts` `fetchSnapshot`): rows absent from the hub snapshot are DELETED from Dexie + the map store (loaded `detail` preserved on survivors) — merge-only mirroring kept hub-pruned events alive client-side forever. The hub store is append-only with expiry-only pruning, so a location cleanup = edit `meetup-events.json` + hub restart, and the authoritative snapshot propagates it.

### Bookmarks
- 977+ `.md` files in Obsidian vault. Hub parses + caches. Browse mode + Tinder-style triage mode.

### Notes
- CodeMirror 6 with vim mode. Obsidian-style live markdown preview. FSA or hub adapter.
- **Saves are conflict-guarded + serialized**. Every open tab carries `baseMtime` (disk mtime at read); `saveFile` sends it, the hub's `writeConditional` (or FSA `lastModified` compare) 409s when disk is newer, and the SPA shows Overwrite/Keep-editing instead of clobbering — "Keep editing" re-arms `savedContent`/`baseMtime` against the disk copy with the buffer untouched. Two gotchas: (1) **Ctrl+S fires TWO save paths** (window keybinding in `useKeybindings` + CM6's own `Mod-s` keymap) — `saveFile` serializes per-path via a module-level `savesInFlight` promise queue (re-reads state inside the lock; trailing save no-ops), or the second save falsely conflicts against the first's fresh mtime; (2) `reconcileWithDisk` (`disk-review.ts`, which remote-open now calls) must re-arm `baseMtime` too or the save after a remote-open falsely conflicts. Recovery when a clobber DOES land: local Syncthing has no versioning for `yam-brain`, but the VPS side does — `yousefamar.com:~/sync/brain/.stversions/` keeps timestamped copies.
- Quick Switcher (fzf fuzzy + MiniSearch full-text). Multi-file tabs. No auto-save.
- **Unsaved buffers survive a refresh** (draft mirror in `src/store/notes.ts`): a module-scope store subscriber mirrors every dirty buffer to localStorage `console:notes:draft:<path>` (400 ms debounce + synchronous flush on `pagehide`/`visibilitychange:hidden` — the debounce window must never hold the only copy), dropped the moment the buffer is clean or its tab closes (force-closing a dirty tab = explicit discard). `openFile` restores a draft over the disk copy **keeping the draft's ORIGINAL `baseMtime`**, so if the disk moved while the draft slept the next save trips the existing conflict dialog instead of clobbering either side; a draft whose content matches disk is stale and dropped. localStorage, not Dexie — the pagehide flush must be synchronous. 1 MB per-file cap (quota safety; the in-memory buffer is unaffected).
- **Live-buffer mirror — the writing-assistant seam**: the same draft-flush also POSTs the ACTIVE buffer (dirty or clean — "what am I looking at" is the context) to `POST /notes/live` `{path, content, cursorLine, selection}` (single in-memory hub slot in `server/src/routes/notes.ts`, `getLiveBuffer`/`setLiveBuffer`, 15-min staleness; deliberately NOT persisted — the vault + localStorage drafts are the durable layers). Agents PULL it via `GET /notes/live` / `con notes live` when asked to help with a draft — zero tokens between asks, unsaved keystrokes included; no open file clears the slot (the subscriber arms the flush on EVERY openFiles/activeFilePath change for exactly this). Discoverability lives in the VAULT's CLAUDE.md (`~/sync/brain/CLAUDE.md` "Live buffer" section), NOT in composer injection — Yousef's rule: only the Curator should know, via its CLAUDE.md; never decorate every message. The consumer is the vault **Curator**: a single shared session (agentKey `curator`, cwd `~/sync/brain`, bound to ALL areas — one accumulating writing companion, fork-on-demand for heavy research threads, never per-draft forks) minted via `con agent create --name/--project/--areas` (`--name` = asAgent, the others = space binding). In the Spaces rail the Curator is HOISTED: one Feather row above the Areas section (`CURATOR_AGENT_KEY` in `SpacesTab.tsx`; skipped by the badge/alert loop so it never badges its 7 areas; click selects the session, switching to one of its areas first if needed). The skip covers the whole Curator LINEAGE: forks inherit the all-areas binding, so the loop walks `parentClaudeSessionId` to the root — agentKey-matching alone misses chat-forks, which carry their own key; alerted/working forks render as indented GitBranch rows under the Curator row.
- **AI-edit inline review** — an agent Edit/Write to a vault file that's OPEN in the doc editor flips the buffer into VSCode-style review: word-level diff, per-chunk ✓/✗ (`@codemirror/merge` `unifiedMergeView`, `allowInlineDiffs`), violet banner with pending count + Accept all/Reject all (`ReviewBanner.tsx`). **The primitive is "disk moved under an open buffer", not "an agent used Edit"** — `src/notes/disk-review.ts` `reconcileWithDisk(path, {force?})` (wired in GatedBoot): read disk, then pure `decideReconcile(file, fresh, {force, onScreen})` → `unchanged` (buffer already holds disk; re-arm savedContent/baseMtime) | `local-only` (disk still equals `savedContent` — only the user's unsaved typing differs, i.e. our own save echoing back; NEVER a review) | `review` | `replace` (force — OR the buffer is clean and **no live editor shows it**: `onScreen = editorViewPath === path`, false on another pane / Spaces Board view / another file active, since editors mount only for the visible file. A review there would ask him to adjudicate a change he never saw (^ripe-ram); a dirty off-screen buffer still reviews — the edits are the base — and an already-open review keeps its base and spans later edits). Review BASE = the user's buffer at that moment (dirty edits included — ✗ restores exactly what they had), buffer = the disk copy, `savedContent`/`baseMtime` track disk so a normal save persists the verdict. It awaits `pendingSave(path)` before reading (and re-reads if a save started mid-read) so a write of our own buffer can't read back as external. **Four triggers, all converging on it**: (a) hub `AgentContext.onToolDiff` (off the existing `tool_diff`) → `vaultRelative()` (`server/src/agents/vault-edit.ts`) → SyncBus `notes.agent_edit {path, sessionId}` — instant, Edit/Write tools only; (b) **`notes.file_changed {path, mtime}` from the BoardWatcher poll** (`onFileChanged`, every changed vault `.md` each 10 s tick — that poll is the vault's only change feed, so it doubles as the signal for Bash / `con notes write` / heredoc / Syncthing-from-another-device writes, none of which emit a tool_diff); (c) **`hubBus.onConnect` sweeps every open file** (`reconcileAllOpen`) — SyncBus broadcasts are fire-and-forget and never replayed, so a change announced during a WS gap is only ever caught here; (d) **`:e`** — reconcile, never a blind reload, so it's safe on a dirty buffer too (the disk change shows as a review with your typing as a rejectable chunk); **`:e!`** is the blind reload and drops any open review. Remote-open (`con notes open` on an already-open file) also reconciles, so an agent that just wrote a file and opens it for Yousef shows him the diff. Load-bearing details: (1) `remainingChunks()` returns **null when the merge field isn't configured** — treating null as 0 would auto-exit a review whose compartment reconfigure hasn't landed; auto-exit fires on `mayResolveChunks(update) && === 0` — NOT bare `docChanged`: CM6's `acceptChunk` rewrites only the merge view's ORIGINAL doc (`userEvent:"accept"`, docChanged false), so a review finished by accepting the last chunk never doc-changes and would stick at "0 pending changes"; rejects/typing edit the buffer and always doc-change. Related: the base theme absolutely positions `.cm-chunkButtons` OVER the chunk's first line — `reviewTheme` forces static flow so the ✓/✗ row sits above the text; (2) the compartment reconfigure happens in a `[reviewBase]` effect AND in `mount()` — the editor mounts on a deferred rAF/50ms race, so an effect alone can hit a null view and never re-fire; (3) dispatching inside an updateListener is illegal — the auto-exit defers via setTimeout; (4) the base mirrors to localStorage `console:notes:review:<path>` (1 MB cap) and re-arms on `openFile`, so a reload mid-review keeps the pending ✗-restore copy; closing the tab = explicit walk-away (agent copy stands), same contract as force-closing dirty — but the Spaces **single-buffer auto-close spares files under review** (the tree marks them with a violet dot, `marks` in `NotesFileBrowser`: amber dirty > violet review > blue draft); (5) **accepting a chunk advances the store base** (`updateReviewBase` ← `currentOriginal(state)` on `acceptedChunk(update)`): `acceptChunk` rewrites only the merge view's in-memory original, and a remount (pane/tab switch) reconfigures from the STORE base; the `[reviewBase]` effect skips the reconfigure when the view's original already equals the base. A review in flight ignores later `beginReview`s for the same path — rapid successive changes fold into one pass against the ORIGINAL base. Files not open in the editor get NO review (opening later reads the post-edit disk copy normally). **Vim ex bang gotcha**: codemirror-vim parses `^\w+` as the command name, so `:q!`/`:e!` arrive as `q`/`e` with `argString === '!'` — a `defineEx('q!')` is unreachable; read the bang from `params.argString`.
- **View mode** (`useNotesStore.viewMode`, persisted via hub pref `notesViewMode`): `tree` (default `NotesFileBrowser` sidebar) | `circles` (`src/components/notes/CirclesView.tsx`). Toggle via the `Circle` icon in the tree toolbar / `FolderTree` icon in the circles toolbar.
- **Circles view** (`src/components/notes/CirclesView.tsx`) — canvas circle-pack of `useNotesStore.files` weighted by `VaultFile.size` (`d3-hierarchy`; one `<canvas>`, rAF + dirty flag, all imperative; d3-zoom owns pan/wheel/pinch and its `filter` rejects gestures over files so they never race drag-to-rename; long-press 400 ms lifts a file to move, a quick tap opens). Machete-style level-of-detail: parents render as opaque covers that fade past `coverFadeThreshold(W, H) = min(W,H) * 0.4`, and `isAncestorChainOpen` gates both label rendering and hit-testing so labels can't leak through covers. Pure helpers + tests: `circles-view-helpers.ts`.
- **Live table preview** (`src/notes/table-preview.ts`, wired in `NotesEditorCore` beside `livePreview()` — covers Notes tab AND Spaces Docs, same component): GFM tables block-replace into rendered `<table>` widgets; cursor/selection in-or-adjacent reveals the raw markdown, clicking a cell puts the cursor at that cell's text. **Block decorations must come from a StateField, not a ViewPlugin** (CM6 restriction — the `frontmatterField` precedent); tables are scanned by line-walk with fence tracking (`scanTables`, pure + unit-tested in `src/__tests__/table-preview.test.ts`), not the async syntax tree. **GOTCHA: a block-replace acts like a fold — vertical cursor motion (j/k, arrows) skips it, so "cursor inside" can never happen**; the reveal window must extend one line above/below the table or the widget is permanently stuck rendered. Cell content renders inline md via DOM building (never innerHTML). Note: the SPA's vitest env is `node` — no jsdom, so widget/DOM code is untestable here; keep the scanner pure.
- **Wiki-links** (`src/notes/wiki-target.ts`, pure + unit-tested): the picker (`NotesLinkPicker`, opens on `[[`) searches filenames AND content (MiniSearch hits appended, FileSearch icon), and mints targets via `wikiTarget()` — basename when unique vault-wide, full path otherwise (a bare `[[index]]` among many index.mds resolves arbitrarily). Inserts are always `[[target|display]]` with the display segment pre-selected (type to rename; two backspaces → bare link). **GOTCHA: any inline widget's click handler must bind `mousedown`, not `click`** — click's own mousedown moves the CM cursor onto the line, the live-preview reveal destroys the widget mid-gesture, and the `click` event never fires. Click resolution: `resolveWikiTarget` (exact path → basename fallback), and on the Spaces pane it selects the owning space first (path project → frontmatter `project:` → first tag naming a known area → `~vault`) because the Docs editor is scope-gated. The Spaces `/` switcher ranks files by `max(mtime, openedAt)` — `useNotesStore.openedAt` is stamped on every `openFile` so merely-read files rank.
- **Active-line highlight suppression during selection**: the default `highlightActiveLine` paints a line background that sits above CM6's selection layer, washing the selection out. Replaced with a custom `ViewPlugin` in `NotesEditorCore.tsx` that only adds the `.cm-activeLine` decoration when `state.selection.main.empty`, so any non-empty selection (visual mode, mouse drag) shows cleanly.
- **Tab nav** — `Ctrl+H` (prev) / `Ctrl+L` (next), wired as a capture-phase keydown listener on `window` in `useKeybindings.ts`. Capture phase is critical: it runs before CodeMirror's keymap (which would otherwise consume Ctrl+H as cursor-left in vim normal / `deleteCharBackward` in insert) AND lets `preventDefault()` stop Brave/Chromium's `Ctrl+H = history` accelerator (it's "unreserved" per Chromium's accelerator table — `Ctrl+T/N/W` are the reserved ones that can't be intercepted). Works in installed PWA + APK.
- **Blog writing mode** (third `viewMode: 'blog'`): `src/components/notes/BlogView.tsx` sidebar (New post, drafts age-coloured, projects with expandable devlogs, recent published with ↗ permalinks). When the active file is a draft (`log/drafts/` for unhomed drafts, `projects/<slug>/log/drafts/` for project drafts — a draft sits BESIDE where it publishes, publish = move up one level; legacy `scratch/blog-drafts/` still recognised; `isDraftPath` in `src/utils/frontmatter.ts` / `server/src/blog.ts`, kept in sync incl. Android `Frontmatter.kt`. Eleventy safety: drafts are `public: false` AND the post/permalink regexes only match direct children of `log/`) or a published post ("writing files"): `WriteMetaBar` (title/tags/project structured editing — round-trips through the CM6 buffer via `src/utils/frontmatter.ts`, a client port of the server's parse/stamp, NEVER a server-side patch since the vault may be FSA-local) + `WriteActionBar` (Photo/Camera with canvas downscale to 2000px JPEG → `pasteImage`, Mic via `src/hooks/useDictation.ts`, Format via `POST /blog/format`, Publish/View-live morphing). Permalink formula: `log/<name>.md` → `https://yousefamar.com/memo/log/<name>/` (`permalinkForLogPath`).
  - **Every frontmatter WRITE goes through `yamlScalar()`.** All three ports hand-roll YAML (hub `server/src/blog.ts` `createDraft`/`createProject`/`stampFrontmatter` → `publishDraft`/`setProjectStatus`; SPA `src/utils/frontmatter.ts` `stampFrontmatter` → `WriteMetaBar`; Kotlin `Frontmatter.kt` `stamp`), and a bare plain scalar with `: `, a leading `- `, a bare `2026`/`true`, or ` #` is a hard YAML parse error or a silent coercion that breaks the Eleventy build for that post. `yamlScalar` double-quotes (the vault convention) only when needed — the mapping test is `:` + **whitespace**, so `date: 2026-06-08 10:00:00` sees no churn. Its inverse `unquoteYamlScalar` strips only a **MATCHED** quote pair. Parse rules: bool coercion applies to UNQUOTED values only (`title: "true"` is the string); a quoted `tags:` scalar is ONE tag, commas and all. `server/src/bookmarks.ts` uses the real `yaml` lib. **Tests assert against `parseYaml()`, never our own forgiving parser** — Eleventy is the consumer that actually breaks.
- **Dictation hook** (`src/hooks/useDictation.ts`): extracted from AgentPromptInput — browser SpeechRecognition with fallback to hub `/stt` WS; APK goes straight to hub (WebView SR hangs silently). Commits text via `onText(chunk, verbatim)` per chunk; dedups VAD finals against streamed deltas. AgentPromptInput + WriteActionBar consume it.
  - **The two sources chunk at different granularities, and that decides who owns the word boundary** (`src/utils/dictation-text.ts` `dictationSeparator`, unit-tested). Hub `/stt` streams raw OpenAI **token** deltas — a token carries its own leading space when it *starts* a word and none when it *continues* one (`' structur'` + `'ally'`) — so it must be inserted **verbatim**. Browser SR emits whole finalized utterances with **no** leading space, so those DO need one inserted or they glue (`"worldhow"`). Hence the `verbatim` flag on `onText` rather than one rule for both.
- **`POST /blog/format`**: dictation formatter — runs `claude -p` one-shot via the CLI (no ANTHROPIC_API_KEY needed, rides the subscription, model = CLI default). Strict prompt: punctuation/paragraphs/artefacts only, wording untouched. `GET /blog/posts?limit=N` lists recent published posts (title/date/project/tags).
- **Publish waits for Syncthing propagation before rebuilding** (`server/src/syncthing.ts`). The vault reaches the VPS via **Syncthing, not git push**, so `publishDraft`/`republishPost` call `waitForVaultSync(join(store.vaultPath, path))` BEFORE `triggerRebuild()`: it force-scans the path (`POST /rest/db/scan`, bypassing the fsWatcher debounce) then polls `/rest/db/completion` per remote until `needItems/needBytes/needDeletes` all 0 (60s bounded wait, 1s cadence). **Remotes = the folder's `devices` minus the local `myID`** (`/rest/system/status`); folder resolved by **deepest-path match** so the vault's `yam-brain` folder wins over the ancestor `sync` folder. **Creds read at RUNTIME from `~/.local/state/syncthing/config.xml`** (`<apikey>` + `<gui><address>`, mode 0600) — NEVER hardcoded; env overrides `SYNCTHING_API_KEY`/`SYNCTHING_URL`. **Degrades gracefully**: Syncthing unreachable/unconfigured → `available:false` and the rebuild fires anyway (no worse than before). `PublishResult` gained `synced`/`syncTimedOut`/`syncWaitedMs`; the SPA `publish`/`republish` timeouts were bumped to 90s (60s wait + 15s rebuild headroom). UI: `LiveStatusChip`'s mount probe bails if status is already `'building'`, and `NotesEditor.handlePublish` sets `'building'` BEFORE `openFile` mounts the chip.
- **`/rebuild` only QUEUES; "published" means "build scheduled"**. The blog server debounces 3 s then runs a FULL-SITE Eleventy build (~25 s on the VPS) and serialises builds — `{"success":true,"queued":true}` comes back before any of that. So the post goes live ~30 s after the click, or never if a build is wedged. Diagnose a slow publish on the VPS, not in Console: `ssh yousefamar.com 'ps -eo pid,etime,args | grep [b]in/eleventy'` (runs as root under pm2 `brain`; `sudo -n kill <pid>` unwedges it — the server treats a killed build as failed and re-runs pending rebuilds). **Stale published posts** — the sidebar twin of drafts: `src/blog/stale.ts` `stalePostCandidates(files, siteBuiltAt)` marks a post stale when its vault mtime is newer than the site's last build; `siteBuiltAt` is the blog server's OWN build clock — `/blog/site-status` proxies its `/rebuild/status` and `buildClock()` takes the last SUCCESSFUL build's `startedAt` (when Eleventy read the vault; `finishedAt` would call a file saved mid-build live) — held on `useBlogStore` (`refreshSiteBuiltAt`, `siteBuildError`, `stalePosts`, `recomputeStalePosts`), wired in `GatedBoot` via `src/blog/stale-subscribe.ts` (boot + hub reconnect + every 2 min + after `waitForBuild` sees a new build; recompute debounced on notes-store `files` changes). **A page's Last-Modified is NOT a build clock** (^jade-yak, 5 Oct 2026): Eleventy honours the vault's `.gitignore`, so a pattern there can drop a template from the build while Caddy keeps serving the file it last wrote — `root/*/` froze `https://yousefamar.com/memo/`, the page this used to probe, on 3 Oct, and every post published afterwards read as stale forever. It survives only as a fallback for a blog server with no build on record (and probes `/memo/log/`, which a publish does regenerate). A FAILED build keeps the old clock and fills `siteBuildError` with its `[11ty]` lines: the chip goes red `build failed` and the publish toasts name it, instead of a bare "stale" that looks like a forgotten publish. Frontmatter is fetched only for stale hits (project = path for `projects/<slug>/log/`, else `project:`; tags → areas). SpacesTab rows them `level: 'stale'` (yellow dot, rank between dirty and draft) under the project and each tagged area, counts them in the rail's FileText badge ("N not live"), and the devlog strips show a yellow `stale` chip in the date's place (`unsaved` amber still wins). Unknown build time (probe failed) → nothing is marked stale, by design.
- **Remote-open (`con notes open <path>[#Heading] [--create] [--anchor "<H>"]`)** — any agent/terminal can make the *running* SPA switch to the Notes pane and open a file, focused. Hub route `POST /notes/open` is a pure **relay**: it holds no UI state, so it broadcasts SyncBus service **`notes`**, op **`open_file`** `{path, anchor?}` and reports `409 NO_CLIENT` when `syncBus.subscriberCount('notes') === 0` — **never a silent success**. A missing path is a **404** (`--create` opts in, seeding `# <title>` plus `## <anchor>` when anchored, so an anchored create has a real scroll target); note the file IS created before the client check, so `created:true` can come back *with* a 409. Notes routes have no RouteContext carrying the bus, so `index.ts` injects a small `NoteOpenBridge` `{broadcast, clientCount}` literal at the call site (the `handleMicRoutes` `compose:` precedent). **GOTCHAs**: (1) `/notes/open` must be matched FIRST in `handleNoteRoutes` or the `/notes/file/` prefix matchers shadow it; (2) the receiver `src/notes/open-subscribe.ts` is wired at **boot in `GatedBoot`, NOT in NotesTab** — the vault adapter is only created on NotesTab mount and `openFile` early-returns `if (!adapter)`, so it calls `reconnectVault()` first; (3) the CM6 `editorView` isn't in the store until a frame or two after the pane switch, so it polls (`setTimeout`, 3 s cap — **never rAF**, see below). Anchor matching is case-insensitive against any heading level; unmatched → cursor stays at the top rather than guessing. Pure helpers `splitAnchor` (splits on the LAST `#`) / `findHeadingLine` unit-tested in `server/src/__tests__/notes-open.test.ts` + `src/__tests__/notes-open.test.ts`.
- **Remote-open into an ALREADY-OPEN tab — three rules**: (1) `requestAnimationFrame` does not fire in a backgrounded OR occluded tab (`document.visibilityState` still reads `"visible"` when merely covered), so `NotesEditorCore.mount()` races rAF against `setTimeout(…, 50)` with a `mounted` latch (the rAF must stay — it stops vim's `BlockCursorPlugin` calling `coordsAt` pre-layout) and the content-flush debounce is `setTimeout` too; never gate mount/flush/poll logic on rAF alone (`memory/project_raf_occluded_tab.md`). (2) The store's `editorView` slot is global — `setEditorView(view, path)` also stores `editorViewPath` and `waitForEditorView(path)` requires the match, or a mid-switch anchor dispatch scrolls the previous file. (3) An already-open tab holds the content read when it opened, and the caller has usually just written the file — `reconcileWithDisk()` re-reads and dispatches into the live view (setting `content` AND `savedContent`), opening an inline REVIEW rather than replacing when the copies differ (dirty buffer included — the user's text is the review base).
- **Open tabs are hub-backed, not localStorage** (`notesOpenTabs` pref; `persistTabs`/`loadPersistedTabs`/`restoreTabs` in `src/store/notes.ts`). The open-tab set is work-in-progress *context* — losing it is real loss — so the hub pref is authoritative and localStorage is only an offline mirror, read when the hub is unreachable at boot. Four load-bearing guards: (1) `restoreTabs()` awaits **`prefsReady()`** (raced against a 4 s timeout for tests / an unreachable hub) — reading the pref cache before `initPrefs()` resolves yields the empty default, and the next `persistTabs()` would write that emptiness over the real value; (2) `persistTabs` **no-ops the hub write while `!isPrefsLoaded()`** for the same reason (the local mirror still updates, and the next write after prefs land syncs); (3) a module-level `restoring` latch suppresses the per-`openFile` writes during a restore, so a reload landing mid-restore can't truncate the pref to however many tabs had re-opened — one write at the end, which also drops paths that no longer exist; (4) **`reconnectVault()`'s hub-adapter branch needs its own `restoreTabs()` call** — it doesn't go through `loadVaultFiles()`, so before this a hub-adapter boot came up with zero tabs regardless of what was persisted.
- **Mobile vim**: off by default (`console:notes:mobileVim` localStorage); auto-enables on hardware-keyboard heuristic (real Escape / Ctrl-chord keydown — soft keyboards emit keyCode 229/'Unidentified'); manual toggle = tappable "vim" chip in the editor status bar.
- **Live pen pages** (Neo smartpen → Notes): the APK forwards each BLE event as `pen_frame{kind:'dot',hex}` over `/push`; `server/src/pen-hub.ts` decodes via the pure `server/src/pen/page-codec.ts` and `server/src/pen/page-assembler.ts` assembles strokes into per-Ncode-page SVGs at `scratch/pen/<note>/page-<page>.svg` (the lossless `PenPageDoc` rides in `<metadata><penpage>{json}</penpage></metadata>`), broadcasting SyncBus service `pen` (`page_open`/`stroke_delta`/`stroke_end`/`page_saved`). SPA `PenPageRenderer.tsx` draws the page + overlays live strokes; `NotesEditor` mounts it when `isPenPagePath(activeFilePath)`. **GOTCHA**: both vault walkers (`server/src/notes.ts`, `src/notes/vault-adapter.ts`) list `.md` only and were widened to `.svg` **scoped to `scratch/pen/`**. **Live streaming is opt-in** (`con pen stream on|off`, persisted in `pen-auth.json`, default off): the pen emits NO live dots until the hub registers **AddUsingNotes** (`0x11`, payload `ffff`) post-auth — sent on connect when enabled, alongside remembered-PIN auto-unlock (forgotten on any failed attempt so a wrong PIN can't burn the retry counter; seeded PIN `1551`; never brute-force). Rendering details (page viewBox, pressure-weighted ribbon strokes) + future work: `docs/neo-pen-plan.md`.

### Feeds
- Server-side RSS/Atom fetching. Offline reading via IndexedDB. OPML import/export.
- Optional full-text extraction via Readability. 15-min refresh interval.
- **Hidden folders** (`HIDDEN_FOLDERS` in the pure `src/feeds/hidden-folders.ts` — pure so node-env tests can import it without Dexie; case-insensitive): a folder whose feeds are excluded from the aggregated **"All"** list AND from the `totalUnread` badge, surfacing only when that folder/feed is opened directly. For high-volume/low-signal sources that would otherwise drown the main list. **Two edits are required and it's easy to ship half of it**: the filter in `loadItemsFromDb`'s `feedIds === null` branch (the "All" query — the per-feed and per-folder branches must stay unfiltered or direct navigation breaks) and the `total++` guard in `computeUnreadCounts` (per-feed `counts[]` still increment, so the folder shows its own count). The unified Inbox pane's Feed column also suppresses these (see Inbox section — `hiddenFolder` flag + `feedMode`).
- **`FeedSubscription.viaProxy`** — per-feed opt-in to fetch through a Cloudflare Worker instead of directly, for sources that **region-gate their RSS** (some 302 to an interstitial that then 404s; cookies, `old.` subdomains, and spoofed `CF-IPCountry` headers all fail because the gate is applied at the edge by requester geography, not by request content). The Worker is hostname-allow-listed + `x-proxy-secret`-authenticated; `REDDIT_PROXY_URL`/`REDDIT_PROXY_SECRET` live in `server/.env` (uncommitted) — absent config just fails those feeds rather than breaking the hub.
  - **`[placement] region = "aws:us-east-1"` in `wrangler.toml`, NOT `mode = "smart"`.** Smart placement optimizes *backend* latency and leaves execution at the PoP nearest the requester, so the outbound fetch still egresses locally and the gate still applies. Verify with the **`cf-placement` response header** — `local-LHR` means it didn't move, `remote-IAD` means it did. This distinction cost a debugging cycle.
  - **`rss-parser.parseURL` cannot be proxied** — it fetches internally. Proxied feeds must `fetch` the text themselves and use `parseString`.
- **Reddit comments** (`FeedStore.fetchRedditComments` → `GET /feeds/reddit-comments?permalink=`, rendered by `RedditComments` in `FeedItemView.tsx` for any Reddit item, mirroring the HN thread UI): Reddit serves a thread as Atom at **`<permalink>.rss`** — entry[0] is the OP (`t3_*`), the rest are comments (`t1_*`). **Flat, with no depth/parent field**, unlike HN's Firebase tree, so it renders as a flat list — don't try to reconstruct nesting from it. `?sort=top` is honoured server-side. **No upvote count is obtainable anonymously**: RSS/Atom omits `score`/`ups` entirely and the JSON API that carries them 403s without login, so ordering is the only signal available. Direct fetch first, proxy only as fallback.

### Money (Monzo + financial planner)
- Hub proxies Monzo API. Single-use refresh tokens. Server-side transaction cache (`monzo-transactions.json`, never deleted — Monzo's 90-day API wall is one-time pain at first sync).
- SCA required after OAuth. Webhook for real-time transaction notifications.
- **Sub-tabs in `MoneyTab.tsx`** (sub-tab state in `src/store/finance.ts`, persisted to localStorage `console:money:subtab`): `Cashflow | Net worth | Budgets | Scenarios | Categories | Transactions`. Default landing = Cashflow (runway-first); Transactions is the original 3-pane forensic view.
- **Data-source reality check**: only Monzo is auto-synced. Lloyds/Revolut/S&S ISAs/GIAs/held-by-others are all "manual" accounts — log a balance entry whenever you check (Net worth tab → expand the row → add entry), runway is computed from those.
