It fires when, within `OUTAGE_WINDOW_MS` (10 min), a model has ≥ `OUTAGE_MIN_FAILURES` (3) failures spanning ≥ `OUTAGE_MIN_SPAN_MS` (2 min — a simultaneous burst from ten sessions is a blip; the third auto-resume wave, and a lone session's third retry, both land at ~4 min) and more than twice as many failures as successes since the streak's oldest failure; the session then calls `signalModelFailure('upstream outage: …')` and the ORDINARY `model_failure` handler in `routes/agents.ts` does the rest (un-pin if pinned, else `reportFailure` → `restartAllSessionsForModel`'s in-place `set_model` fast path — no context lost). `OUTAGE_ADVANCE_COOLDOWN_MS` (10 min) between fires is the cascade brake: if the model we just moved to is failing too, the problem isn't the model — hold there (auto-resume keeps nudging) rather than walk the chain down to haiku in 20 min. A streak held by the cooldown is kept, not reset, so it fires the moment the cooldown ends. The pending auto-resume nudge notices the model changed under it (`failedModel` captured at schedule time) and tells the agent "X was unavailable upstream; this session now runs on Y" instead of the generic text. `setModelLive` resets `modelFailureSignaled` (only the respawn path did before, so an in-place-moved session could never report the new model dying). Nothing moves the fleet BACK when the outage ends — that stays manual (or a probe-guarded cron, AL's `reference_model_outage.md`). Tests: `transient-errors.test.ts` (tracker) + `session.test.ts` "upstream-outage fallback" (fake fleet over the stream-json wire).
- **Auth backend switch** (`server/src/auth-backend.ts`) — a distinct lever from the model picker above: Yousef alternates between his **Claude Max subscription** (fixed cost, hits 5h/weekly session limits under fleet load) and **Amazon Bedrock** (pay-per-token, no session limits, needs AWS creds). Each `claude` CLI subprocess resolves its backend from `~/.claude/settings.json`'s `env` block **at its own spawn time** (confirmed: the hub's own pm2 env carries zero Bedrock/AWS keys) — so switching means (1) rewriting that file's env to the target preset (`BACKEND_PRESETS.first_party` = `{}`; `.bedrock` = `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_BEDROCK=1` + `AWS_PROFILE=bedrock-amar` + `AWS_REGION=us-east-1` + the ARN/`us.anthropic.*` vars) and (2) swapping the model chain to that backend's id format (same bare-vs-`us.anthropic.`-prefixed split as above — the same id 400s on the other backend). `MANAGED_ENV_KEYS` strips any stray managed key not in the target preset, so switching is a clean swap, not an accumulation. **The managed env is also re-baked at every boot** (`syncBackendSettings()` after Bedrock profile discovery, no-op when nothing differs). **Critical**: a backend switch MUST force every live session to fully respawn (`forceRestartAllSessionsForBackend`, never the `set_model` in-place fast path) — an already-running process already has its OLD backend's env baked in from its own startup; only a fresh `claude` invocation re-reads settings.json. Hibernated sessions need no action (they resolve the new backend/model fresh at wake). A per-session model pin (`modelOverride`) doesn't auto-translate across backends — `applyBackendSwitch` logs a warning listing any pin that looks like it belongs to the other backend (prefix heuristic) rather than silently letting it 400. Surface: `con agent backend [get|set <first_party|bedrock>|history|usage]`, HTTP `GET/POST /agents/backend`, SPA segmented toggle above the model picker (`SpacesFleetMenu`); `model_state.backend` carries the active backend on every push.
- **Max first, Bedrock as backup — automatic failover** (`server/src/backend-failover.ts` + `subscription-usage.ts`, ^warm-heron 2026-10-02: AWS credits running out, Max 20x reactivated). A first-party `claude` emits `rate_limit_event {rate_limit_info: {status: allowed|allowed_warning|rejected, resetsAt (epoch SECONDS), rateLimitType: five_hour|seven_day|…}}` on the stream-json wire whenever its unified rate-limit headers change (CLI 2.1.280 schema; NEVER emitted on Bedrock — no such headers). `Session.handleClaudeMessage` surfaces it as a `'rate_limit'` event (plus a text fallback: a synthetic API error matching `isUsageLimitError` in `transient-errors.ts` — "usage limit", "hit/reached your … limit", "out of usage credits" — emits a synthetic `rejected`; a plain 429 is NOT that). `routes/agents.ts` forwards to `BackendFailover.onRateLimit`: on `rejected` while `preferred === 'first_party'` it calls `applyBackendSwitch(ctx,'bedrock')` (the ordinary env-rewrite + chain-swap + fleet respawn), persists the open episode to `~/.config/console/backend-failover.json`, emits `console.backend.failover`, and arms a timer for `resetsAt + 60 s` (no resetsAt → `DEFAULT_HOLD_MS` 5 h; a resetsAt > 8 d is treated as unknown) that switches back and emits `console.backend.restored`. A second rejection while spilled (5 h tripped, then weekly) only extends the hold. **`preferred` is the HUMAN's standing choice**: `con agent backend set` / the SPA toggle go through `applyUserBackendChoice` → `setPreferred`, which closes any open spill (`closedBy: 'manual'`) and disarms the return — a manual Bedrock is never undone by the timer, and the failover is inert while preferred is bedrock. First boot with no state file adopts whatever settings.json says as preferred. `boot()` runs after the session restore: a persisted episode is re-armed, or returned immediately if its reset passed while the hub was down. **Mid-turn respawns now nudge**: `restartForModelChange` on a `running` session queues `MODEL_RESTART_NUDGE` and `doModelRespawn` flushes the queue after the silent resume — before this, every backend switch silently killed every in-flight turn (the session that tripped the limit already had its transient-resume nudge). **The subscription-usage ledger** polls `https://api.anthropic.com/api/oauth/usage` (what the CLI's `/usage` reads) with the OAuth access token from `~/.claude/.credentials.json` every 10 min (30 min while 401 — "run `claude auth login`"; the hub never refreshes the token, the `claude` processes do), records every window object carrying `utilization` (0–100; `five_hour`, `seven_day`, `seven_day_opus`, …) into `~/.config/console/subscription-usage.json` (14 d cap). `GET /agents/backend` now returns `preferred`, `failover {active, lastWarning, history}` and `usage {latest, last7d peak/mean/≥90 %, authError}`; `con agent backend` renders them, `history` lists the spills, `usage` the window table — that is the "how many subscriptions do I need" readout. **Max model chain leads with `claude-opus-5-5`, and Fable is deliberately NOT in it** (auth-backend.ts is authoritative; this line said "Fable first, Yousef 3 Oct" until 8 Oct 2026 — the belief it describes is what cost $3.2k). Fable on a subscription has its own small allowance and then "requires usage credits", i.e. exactly the pay-per-use spend the chain exists to avoid: cfbdc7b6 put it at the head, the credits were gone in three days, and the `seven_day_overage_included` rejection it then threw read as PLAN-wide exhaustion and spilled the whole fleet to Bedrock for five days while opus-5-5 and haiku answered fine throughout. Worse, `applyBackendSwitch` reseeds the chain FROM the preset, so hand-fixing the live chain and switching back re-tripped it in 3 s. Pin Fable per session (`con agent model pin`) if a card warrants it; never return it to the preset without buying credits deliberately. The Bedrock chain keeps Opus 5 first for the same reason. **A preset chain changed in code reaches a running hub only via the boot reconcile** (ed2e1163, ^shy-kite): `ModelConfig.presetChain` records which preset the saved chain was seeded from; at boot an UNEDITED older preset is replaced by the current one (and the active model follows only if it was that preset's old default), a hand-edited chain is never touched — log `[model] chain updated to the current preset`. Why: the chain lives in `agent-model.json` and was only reseeded on a backend switch, so cfbdc7b6 (Fable first) sat inert, and setting it by hand (`con agent model chain …`, 3 Oct 17:09) did not survive either — the 3 Oct failover returned under old code and saved the no-Fable chain back ("Why is Fable gone from my dropdown again?"). A hand-set chain is not a deploy; verify the dropdown after the next backend switch. **Per-model windows step down, never spill** (cfbdc7b6): a `rateLimitType` of `seven_day_<family>` (`modelFamilyOf` — `seven_day_opus`, a Fable one; NOT `seven_day_overage_included`) means only that family's allowance is out, so `onRateLimit` calls `deps.stepDownFrom(family)` — the hub model moves to the first chain entry outside that family on the SAME subscription (`setModel` + the in-place `restartAllSessionsForModel`), a `ModelHold` is persisted and a timer steps back up at `resetsAt + 60 s` via `deps.restoreModel` — skipped if a human picked another model meanwhile. A per-model limit hit while the hub model is NOT in that family (a pinned session tripped it) is recorded and changes nothing. Every hold lands in `modelHistory` (the per-model half of the usage ledger; `con agent backend` prints `model: from → to … back at` and `model limits: seven_day_x ×N`); events `console.backend.model_stepdown|model_restored`. Plan-wide windows (`five_hour`, `seven_day`, overage) still spill to Bedrock. Login itself is interactive-only (`claude auth login`, browser OAuth) — the hub cannot do it; `claude auth status` with `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_BEDROCK` unset tells you whether the Max login is live. Tests: `__tests__/backend-failover.test.ts`, `__tests__/subscription-usage.test.ts`.
- **More than one Claude Max login** (`server/src/max-logins.ts`, 8 Oct 2026). Why: at 00:13 that morning the plan's WEEKLY window hit 100 % three days before its reset — a genuine `seven_day` rejection (the ledger's own sample said 100 % and a real-login probe was refused, unlike the `seven_day_overage_included` credit wall which carries the SAME `resetsAt`) — and parked the fleet on pay-per-token Bedrock for 74 h, $6–12k modelled. A second subscription is ~$200/mo, so the lever is another login, not more failover tuning. **A login IS a config dir**, never a credentials file: every `claude` process refreshes its own OAuth token and rewrites `.credentials.json` in place, so each login must own the file it rewrites — copying credentials is what rotated the refresh token and killed the whole fleet on 7 Oct. `~/.claude-logins/<name>/` therefore holds only that login's `.credentials.json`, and **everything else is shared back to the canonical `~/.claude`**: DIRECTORIES by symlink (`SHARED_DIRS`; `projects` above all — transcripts, the recall index and auto-memory live under it, so without the link a rotation would orphan every session's history and break `--resume`), FILES by copy (`SHARED_FILES` = settings.json, settings.local.json, .mcp.json) because the CLI rewrites files atomically and a rename REPLACES a symlink instead of writing through it. `ensureAll()` re-links at every boot, repairing anything the CLI replaced; a real directory a login already wrote into is never clobbered. `writeBackendSettings` fans settings.json out to every registered dir, so a rotation can never land a login on the wrong backend. **The failover gains a rung**: a plan-wide rejection still spills to Bedrock FIRST and only then calls `deps.rotateLogin` — spill-then-rotate deliberately, so the fleet is never pointed at a login that has not been proven, and the candidate is probed via `checkMaxLogin({configDir})` against its own REAL dir. On success the episode closes `closedBy: 'rotated'` and the backend goes back to first_party (one respawn), emitting `console.backend.login_rotated`; the spent login carries an `exhaustedUntil` mark so it is not rotated back onto. **Inert until configured**: with no registry file the active dir IS `~/.claude` and `rotateLogin` returns null, so every path behaves exactly as it did before — which is what makes it safe to have on main before the second subscription exists. Surface: `con agent login [list|add <name> [--dir]|use <name> [--force]|check <name>|remove <name>]`, HTTP `GET/POST /agents/logins`. `add` only provisions the dir — the OAuth flow is interactive, so `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR=<dir> claude auth login` must be run by hand. Tests: `__tests__/max-logins.test.ts` plus the rotation block in `__tests__/backend-failover.test.ts`.
- **`messageLog` rolling cap** (`server/src/session.ts` `MAX_LOG_SIZE = 500`). Older entries `shift()` off; `logOffset` tracks the absolute index of `messageLog[0]`. Client paginates with absolute indices via `session.messageLogLength` / `session.messageLogOffset` getters — `get_older_messages` in `server/src/index.ts` translates `beforeIndex - offset` into the in-memory window. Past the boundary, `hasMore: false`. Full history still on disk in Claude CLI's `~/.claude/projects/<encoded-cwd>/<claudeSessionId>.jsonl` (decode via `decodeClaudePath` in `server/src/projects.ts`). (`memory/project_hub_session_log_cap.md`.) **Unread survives restart**: the unread dot is `hasUnread = messageLogLength > lastReadIndex` (read state persisted per `claudeSessionId` in `agent-read-state.json`). The in-memory `messageLog` starts empty on a restart, so `messageLogLength` reported 0 while `lastReadIndex` persisted. Fix: the manifest persists `messageLogLength` (saved on each `result`/turn-end) and the restore path seeds `logOffset` from it (`SessionOptions.restoreMessageLogLength`), so the count stays on the same absolute scale as `lastReadIndex`.
- **SPA message appends are rAF-batched** (`src/store/agent.ts` `bufferMessage`/`drainMessageBuffer`, mirroring `bufferDelta`/`drainDeltaBuffer`). The hub replays `REPLAY_LIMIT = 50` messages **per session** on every WS (re)connect, so with a ~48-session fleet a reconnect is ~1,400 appends — one `setState` each would clone `messagesBySession` and notify every subscriber app-wide, and the burst lands exactly when the phone foregrounds (the heartbeat watchdog reconnects), i.e. exactly when Yousef starts typing. Three guards make the ≤1-frame delay safe: per-session FIFO order is preserved, `dropBufferedMessages(sessionId)` is called from the `/clear` + reload-history paths so a late flush can't resurrect cleared rows, and the `sessions_list` id-remap renames buffered keys so restart-era appends can't land under a dead session id. No caller reads `messagesBySession` synchronously after `addMessage`; store tests must `await flush()` after `receiveMessage`.
- **Queued messages — "send when the WHOLE turn is done"** (`Session.queuedMessage` + `queueMessage`/`setQueuedMessage`/`flushQueuedMessage`/`flushIfIdle`). Distinct from **steering**: writing to the CLI's stdin mid-turn lands at its next *tool boundary*, so anything held client-side and released on a timer would interrupt the turn. The queue therefore lives **hub-side**, which also makes it survive a page reload, the phone backgrounding, and a hub restart mid-turn (`ManifestEntry.queuedMessage`, restored via `SessionOptions`). **ONE buffer that grows** (Yousef's call, not a FIFO): a second queue appends blank-line-separated and the whole thing arrives as a single prompt. **Four flush points, all needed**: (1) `handleResultMessage` — the normal turn end; (2) the **interrupt-resume branch of the `exit` handler**, which emits a *synthetic* `result` via `emitHub` and so bypasses `handleResultMessage` entirely (without this an interrupt strands the queued prompt until some later turn ends); (3) inside `queueMessage` via `flushIfIdle()` — queueing on an idle session is just sending; (4) the `index.ts` restore loop for a queue restored onto an *idle* session (`setTimeout(…, 1_000)`, matching the nudge delay). A `wasRunning` session needs none of these — its restart nudge re-runs the turn and flush (1) fires at its end. **Do NOT flush from the constructor** — it races the "hub was restarted, continue" nudge and converts the queued prompt back into steering. `flushQueuedMessage` clears the slot **before** `sendMessage` (which flips status to `running`) or a re-entrant flush double-sends; it logs the prompt so the transcript shows it. `kill()` drops the queue. The `session_queued` broadcast bypasses `emitHub` (which logs everything but `status`/`tool_input_delta`) via a direct `this.emit('hub_message', …)`, exactly like `scanForAttention` — it's ephemeral state, not transcript, and is deliberately absent from `LoggableHubMessage`; the authoritative value rides `SessionInfo.queuedMessage`. Hibernation needs no special case (`sendMessage` is already hibernation-aware, so a flush wakes the session). Surface: protocol `queue_message`/`set_queued_message` → `session_queued`; store `queueMessage`/`setQueuedMessage`; UI in `AgentPromptInput.tsx` — **Ctrl/Cmd+Enter** queues (falls through to a plain send with no active session; **Shift+Enter is now the only newline**), a `Clock` chip above the composer shows the pending text with click-to-edit (pulls it back into the textarea and clears the queue, so re-queueing replaces rather than doubles) and an X to cancel, and while running the button cluster is `[Square (interrupt), Send]` where the Send **tap steers now** and **long-press (500 ms) queues** — that's the mobile trigger (`longPressFired` swallows the click that follows a fired press).
- **Idle hibernation** (`Session.hibernate()` + sweep in `index.ts`, 5-min cadence). Every live `claude` subprocess holds ~250MB RSS even idle. Idle sessions get their subprocess SIGKILLed — **threshold is TTL-aware since 2026-09-21 (^lime-kiwi): 65 min for a 1h-cache process, 30 min for 5m** — a `--resume` respawn rebuilds the prompt with fresh bytes (gitStatus/date) and so ALWAYS rewrites the cache even while the old entry is live (measured ~80% cold), so a 1h process must outlive its own cache for pokes inside the hour to land warm (~2% cold) (`hibernating` flag → exit handler flips `hibernated`, status stays `idle`, messageLog/unread/claudeSessionId intact — NO `session_ended`); the next `sendMessage` transparently re-spawns with `--resume` (a message racing the SIGKILL is queued in `pendingWakeMessage` and delivered after wake). **Restore-into-hibernation**: the hub-restart restore loop passes `hibernateOnStart` for idle sessions (`!wasRunning`, Al exempt) so a restart spawns ~zero subprocesses instead of thundering 40+. Guards: `canHibernate()` blocks on running / pending approval (`approvalPending` — killing then would orphan the CLI's control_request) / model-restart in flight; `kill()` supersedes hibernation; model changes SKIP hibernated sessions (`restartForModelChange` no-op — the new model/pin resolves at wake since `spawn()` reads `modelOverride ?? resolveAgentModel()` fresh). `SessionInfo.hibernated` → moon glyph in the session list. Al never hibernates (always-on front door). **Pruned-transcript wake**: the CLI's retention can delete an old session's JSONL from `~/.claude/projects/` — `--resume` then prints `No conversation found with session ID: …` on stderr and exits(1) pre-init, which the exit handler misread as a model failure. stderr now sets `resumeTargetMissing`, and the exit handler respawns FRESH (no `--resume`; hub-side log/name/key kept; the CLI mints a new claudeSessionId at init) and re-delivers the waking message via `lastStdinPrompt` (captured in `sendMessage` — the original write went to the dying process). The user sees one error line explaining the model lost its old context.
- **The CLI's tool-result clearing ("microcompact") does NOT exist for hub sessions** — it is the `context_hint` beta, REPL + first-party only, server-triggered overflow avoidance, not a cost knob; no env/setting enables it, and the only `context_management` edit the CLI itself sends is `clear_thinking_20251015 keep:all` (thinking models; cost-neutral) — never `clear_tool_uses`. **The hub's context proxy does that on the wire** (next bullet).
- **Context proxy (`server/src/agents/context-proxy.ts` + `sigv4.ts`, ^plum-fawn 2026-10-01/02) — a loopback HTTP proxy between opted-in `claude` processes and bedrock-runtime; `log` mode is the fleet's only wire-level view of what a request is made of, `clear` mode is a MEASURED NEGATIVE RESULT — do not enable it fleet-wide.** Sessions listed in `~/.config/console/context-proxy.json` (`{enabled, sessions: {"<name>|<hubId>|*": "log"|"clear"}, policy: {keep, clearInputs, excludeTools, enterAbove, coldAfterMs}}`, re-read on change) are spawned with `ANTHROPIC_BEDROCK_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:<port>` + `CLAUDE_CODE_SKIP_BEDROCK_AUTH=1` (the CLI's gateway mode — unsigned requests) + `ANTHROPIC_CUSTOM_HEADERS` carrying `x-console-session`/`x-console-spawn`; the proxy re-signs with hand-rolled SigV4 from the settings.json `AWS_PROFILE`, forwards (`/inference-profiles*` to the control-plane host), and appends every request to `~/.config/console/context-proxy.jsonl` — prompt composition in chars (system/tools/results/inputs/text, stale share beyond `keep`), the trigger decision, usage + `applied_edits` parsed from Bedrock's eventstream in flight. **Anything not listed never touches it**; the proxy only starts when `enabled` and the backend is Bedrock. Readout: `scripts/spend/proxy-ledger.py` (stock counterfactual per request). `clear` mode appends `{type: clear_tool_uses_20250919, keep, clear_tool_inputs, trigger}` to the CLI's edits (+ the `context-management-2025-06-27` beta in `anthropic_beta`) and a 4th `cache_control` on the tool_use keep+8 exchanges back. **What the API does (measured, `scripts/spend/context-probe.ts`, haiku/sonnet/Fable 5.1): (1) original ≤ trigger → no edit; (2) else a CACHED cleared set with retained ≤ trigger MAY be reused (warm); (3) else clear all-but-keep or the minimum to get under the trigger — a COLD write of the retained prompt (with the CLI's breakpoints: everything after the ~28k system prefix; with our mid breakpoint: only the tail).** A fixed trigger below the uncleared floor (>100k in every fleet session) hits rule 3 every call. `decideTrigger` steers (`force-first`/`force-cold` → 1, `hold` → last original − 5 %, `refresh`/`enter` → 1, `none`/`backoff` → 50M), BUT rule 2 is unreliable at fleet scale: live on Fable 5.1 at 420k, 4/6 holds reused the set; synthetic on sonnet at 370k, 1/6 and 1/5 (2 s and 30 s gaps alike — not latency, not thinking; the one hit reuses the set from TWO calls back). Each miss rewrites ~130–270k ($2.6–5.4 at 1h rates) against a warm stock read of ~$0.10; the 8-request live canary cost $21.31 vs $15.80 stock (−35 %). On Fable 5.1 pricing (reads $0.25 vs 1h writes $20/MTok = 80×) nothing that rewrites per call can win, and a free cold clear followed by unreliable holds loses too. Server-side editing is still the only history edit Fable 5.1's preserved-thinking check tolerates — a proxy rewriting `tool_result` bytes itself would not be. Sim ceiling if holds were reliable (`scripts/spend/clear-sim.py`, 7d): stale results = 37 % of input spend; cold-timed clearing −30 %, with inputs −46 %. Valve: ≥ 34 % cold holds over the last 10 → `backoff` (no edits until the next cold moment).
- **Prompt-cache TTL is chosen per spawn** (`server/src/agents/cache-ttl.ts`). Claude Code reads `CLAUDE_CODE_PROMPT_CACHE_TTL` (`5m` | `1h`) once at process start; unset = 5m on Bedrock (its own doc string), so every hub session ran a 5-minute cache and a quarter-plus of cold-rewrite spend was the cache lapsing mid-work. Verified 2026-09-08 that Bedrock honours the env var alone: `usage.cache_creation.ephemeral_1h_input_tokens = 37728, ephemeral_5m = 0` on the real Fable 5.1 profile with it set, the reverse without; `ENABLE_PROMPT_CACHING_1H_BEDROCK` is NOT needed. Policy (`resolveCacheTtl`, in order; **since 2026-09-21 ^lime-kiwi every spawn with history gets 1h** — repriced Fable 5.1 reads at $0.25/MTok flip the old ^odd-toad "all-1h costs more" conclusion, wakes cluster (44% get another poke within the hour), and per-session simulation on 7d of traces puts all-1h + the 65-min hibernation threshold ~$1.4k/wk ahead of the old mix): **pin** (`SessionOptions.cacheTtl`, persisted in the manifest — `forkRoleSessionForTicket` pins every ticket-fork `1h` for life, wakes included; **Al is pinned 1h at his creation sites** in `al/al-session.ts` + forced in the restore loop, else a boot spawn reads as fresh and the always-on front door runs days on a 5m cache) → **hibernation wake = 1h** → **unfinished turn = 1h** (`resumeMidTurn` from the restore loop's `wasRunning`, or live `midTurn`) → **fresh instance = 5m** (mostly single-turn listener forks that never see a second request) → **any respawn with history = 1h** (`cache.ttlRecentMinutes` prefs, default 30, now only picks the `recent` vs `idle` log label). Note `status` is `'running'` on every fresh instance and says nothing at spawn time — never derive the TTL from it. Every spawn logs `[cache] <name>: prompt-cache TTL 1h (mid-turn)`; `SessionInfo.cacheTtl`/`cacheTtlReason` show the current process's (absent while hibernated). **Measurement**: `CacheTtlLedger` (`~/.config/console/cache-ttl-ledger.json`, atomic, local-day buckets) counts the CLI's own `usage.cache_creation.ephemeral_{1h,5m}_input_tokens` + reads + spawns per class from every `result`; `GET /dashboard/costs` ships it as `cacheTtl` (optional — older hub → absent) and the Costs card renders one "Cache writes 1h X (N%) · 5m Y · reads Z · spawns" line. Cost Explorer can't split the two write classes, the CLI can. Subagent TTL (`CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_PROMPT_CACHE_TTL`) is deliberately left unset (5m — subagents are short bursts).
- **`--effort` is chosen per spawn KIND** (`server/src/agents/effort.ts`, decided in `Session.spawn()` beside the TTL — the flag is read once per process). Until 2026-10-01 (^busy-elk) one value served every spawn (pm2 `CLAUDE_EFFORT=xhigh` → 20,538 of 20,584 requests in the 22–28 Sep week ran xhigh; output+thinking ≈ 9% of the modelled bill, per-kind policy sized at $150–300/wk in `research/cost-review-2026-09-30.md`). `resolveEffort({kind, pin, policy})`: **pin** (card `#effort/<level>`, `SessionOptions.effort`, manifest-persisted) → **`cache.effort` pref** (`{ default, fork, cronFork, listenerFork, chatFork }`, partial OK, live — no restart) → **code defaults** `default: xhigh` (generals, standing sessions, Al + his WhatsApp/voice forks, SPA-seeded forks — anything Yousef types into), `fork | cronFork | listenerFork | chatFork: high` (ticket-forks, cron/listener `--fork` wakes, `con agent chat`). `SessionOptions.spawnKind` is set at the fork factories (`forkRoleSessionForTicket`, `forkSessionForWake`, the seedless `fork_session` branch) and persisted so a restored fork keeps its kind. **The pm2 `CLAUDE_EFFORT` env is no longer read** — the defaults live in code. Every spawn logs `[effort] <name>: --effort high (fork, default)`; `SessionInfo.spawnKind/effort/effortReason` show the current process's. A fork Yousef later types into stays at its kind's effort for that process's life — re-dispatch with `#effort/xhigh` if it matters. Board tag: `#effort/<low|medium|high|xhigh|max>` (`#effort:<level>` accepted on read, written back as `/`; unknown levels stay plain text), `con spaces board <p> effort "^id" <level|none>`, ignored with a log line on `#nofork`/direct wakes like `#model`.
- **Autocompact window is chosen per spawn KIND** (`server/src/agents/compact-window.ts`, 7 Oct 2026, console-treasurer; Yousef chose "400k, forks only"). `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW` is set beside the TTL: `fork | cronFork | listenerFork` → 400k; `default` (generals, AL, his conversation forks) and `chatFork` (`con agent chat`, which inherits a transcript on purpose) get no env, i.e. the CLI default of 1M. Pref `cache.autoCompactWindow` (`{ <kind>: tokens | 0 }`, 0 = CLI default) applies live at each process's next spawn. CLI 2.1.292 clamps the value to [100k, 1M] (`r4e`/`Pit` in the bundle) and compacts a little BELOW it (100k → compacted at 67–77k, ~40–50 s per compaction). The `[1m]` argv hint does nothing on 2.1.280+. Never go below ~350k fleet-wide: an Astera session STARTS at ~168k. Sizing: vault `projects/console/research/treasury/levers-2026-10-07.md` (400k for everything ≈ −15% modelled $). `SessionInfo.compactWindow` + a `[compact]` log line per spawn.
  - **Measured over the first night live** (8 Oct 2026, Bedrock $/request, 6–7 Oct vs 8 Oct): astera $0.237 → $0.155 (**−35%**), console $0.217 → $0.130 (**−40%**) — twice the modelled saving, and the cache-read share rose (57 → 68%), which is the mechanism. **But `~/proj/code/console/android` went $0.292 → $0.943 (+223%)**, which exposed two things the sizing got wrong:
    - **The effective ceiling is ~0.55x the number you set, not ~1.0x.** With 400k, 29 observed compactions fired at **168–244k of total request context**; sessions with no env compacted at 938k–1044k (~1.0x of the CLI's 1M). The CLI's rule is not pinned down — budget 0.55x and verify, don't assume a 400k cap buys 400k.
    - **Fixed overhead comes off that first, and it is big.** console/android loads **114k tokens** of CLAUDE.md chain + auto-memory (90k of it THIS file) plus ~29k of system prompt, and the bundle is **re-attached after every compaction**. Each cycle was 5–9k of summary + 114k of instructions + ~75k for ONE turn of work = 223k → compact again. Three forks: 27 compactions in 70 min, $62, 195k written cold per cycle against 28k read, no progress.
  - **So a thrashing cwd loses the cap by itself** (`isThrashing` + `liftCompactWindow`): `THRASH_COMPACTIONS` (3) compactions within `THRASH_WINDOW_MS` (10 min) in one process → the cwd is added to an in-memory lift set and every later spawn there gets the CLI default, reason `thrash`, logged as `[compact] <name>: THRASHING …`. Keyed by **cwd, not session**, because the cause is the shared instruction bundle, so one fork's lesson protects the rest; in memory only, so a hub restart re-learns it for the price of 3 compactions. It beats an explicit pref too — a cap that thrashes costs several times what it saves. The treasury guard also alerts on `COMPACTION THRASH` (`~/exec/treasury/guard.py`, counting `compact_boundary` in the transcript tail it already reads).
  - **The real fix is this file's size.** 371 KB ≈ 90k tokens, charged to every Console and android request on both backends, and it grows ~3 KB/day. A diet needs Yousef's go (the Astera equivalent is card ^jade-bee); until then, keep additions here tight and prefer the per-area CLAUDE.md.
- **Background-process detection.** `Session.getInfo()` includes `backgroundProcessCount` — child PIDs of the `claude` subprocess (from `ps -eo pid,ppid` via `server/src/process-tree.ts`). Session rows show a Terminal-icon badge per row when > 0.
- **`@amar` attention mechanism.** Any agent session that emits the literal `@amar` in its **assistant output** flags itself for Yousef's attention — a sticky red marker in the sidebar (red left-rail + `AlertCircle` badge), a red dot on the Spaces/Inbox pane tabs, and a push notification (pane `agents`). The intent: agents ping Yousef without `con agent send`-ing into his live timeline. Wiring:
  - Detection is a pure module `server/src/attention.ts` (`mentionsAmar` uses `/\B@amar\b/i` so `name@amar.io` / `@amaranth` don't match; `extractAttentionSnippet` centres a ~140-char excerpt). Tested in `server/src/__tests__/attention.test.ts`.
  - **A pending AskUserQuestion/ExitPlanMode raises the same red marker** (`flagAttention(snippet, wantPush:false)` from the `can_use_tool` branch — the question text/plan label as snippet). `wantPush:false` + a `notify:false` hint on the `session_attention` message because `approval_required` already fires its own phone push and desktop notification — the marker sticks, nothing double-notifies. Answering clears it: `approveTool`/`denyTool` go through `resolveUserApproval`, which calls `clearAttention()` only when the requestId matches `pendingApprovalRequest` (auto-approvals of ordinary tools never set it, so a genuine `@amar` marker survives them).
  - `Session.scanForAttention()` runs on **finalized assistant text** (`flushPendingDeltas` for streamed text + directly-emitted `text` in `emitHub`) — checking complete text, since `@`/`amar` split across deltas. User prompts are never scanned (self-ping protection). Sets `Session.needsAttention = { ts, snippet }`, emits a `session_attention` hub message.
  - **Push dedup / anti-noise** (in `Session`): no second push within 60 s; ≥5 in 10 min → marker stays but push is suppressed + a `[attention]` warn logged. The `push: boolean` on the `session_attention` message is a transport-only hint; `routes/agents.ts` strips it before broadcasting and only fires `ctx.notifyAttention` (→ `pushServer`) when true. `clearAttentionPush` cancels the phone notification on clear.
  - Persisted in the manifest (`needsAttention`), restored on hub restart via `SessionOptions.needsAttention`.
  - SPA: `SessionInfo.needsAttention` flows through `sessions_list`; the `session_attention` handler in `src/store/agent.ts` updates it + notifies (desktop notification suppressed only when that session is already the active one — the marker still sticks). **The marker is sticky until Yousef marks the session read OR replies to it** — merely *opening* it (`selectSession`) does NOT clear it, matching the "unread chats stay until marked read" rule, but answering an agent that asked for him IS the acknowledgement. **Clearing is hub-side, inside `markSessionRead` in `routes/agents.ts`** (`session.clearAttention()` + `clearAttentionPush`), which `send_message` and `mark_session_read` both call — so every client, APK included, inherits reply-clears-alert from one place and none of them need to send `clear_attention` themselves (the explicit message still exists for out-of-band callers). Clients only do the optimistic local half.
- Al (personal AI) connects on `/al` WebSocket path.
- **Hub session ids are per-process; `con agent send` fails loudly on a dead one.** `session_N_<ts>` is minted at spawn and changes on every hub restart (the claudeSessionId and agentKey are the stable keys). `send_message` now answers the SENDER: `message_sent` on delivery, `hub_error` otherwise — and because the manifest records each session's `hubId` (+ `formerHubIds`, restored into `Session.formerIds`), a pre-restart id's error names its successor ("that conversation is now session_… (CEO). Address it as ceo"; `agents/stale-id.ts`, also behind `/agents/peek`'s 404). `con agent send <hub-id|claudeSessionId|name|agentKey>` resolves via `/health` first, waits for the ack-or-error, and exits `NOT_FOUND`/`AMBIGUOUS` instead of printing `sent:true` (^zany-orca: AL's message to a stale CEO id was silently lost, 2026-09-15). A target `/health` listed as live is still reported sent if the hub stays silent 3 s (a hub predating the ack).
- **Agent-to-agent forks** (`con agent chat`). Spawns a fork of a named session, injects a message, waits for the reply, prints `conv: <claudeSessionId>` then the reply text. Follow-up turns: `con agent chat --id <conv-id> "<message>"` routes via the fork's claudeSessionId (survives hub restarts). The fork is a real hub session — visible in `con agent list`, tailable, appears in the SPA sidebar (nested under the parent with a `GitBranch` glyph). The target's MAIN session is untouched. Implementation: `cli/src/commands/agent.ts` + `cli/src/ws-client.ts` (`streamWithSends`, `injectAndCapture`).
- **A session is either there or gone — there is no lingering `ended` entry** (^warm-elk, 2026-09-13; supersedes the earlier "kill keeps it readable" call). `kill_session` and `delete_session` are the same hub action: terminate the subprocess, remove the entry from the list + manifest, broadcast. `con agent chat --end`, `con agent kill`, and the SPA's "End session" all do this; the transcript stays on disk for `con agent list --past` / `con agent read`. The dup-guard removes EVERY session sharing the `claudeSessionId` (a resume during a restart can spawn a twin). The other half: an **incidental** subprocess death (crash, OOM, killed from outside) of a session that has a `claudeSessionId` is treated like hibernation — `status: idle`, `hibernated: true`, a synthetic `result` closes the turn for waiters (plus an `error` line if it was mid-turn), and the next message `--resume`s it. `status: ended` now only ever means a session that never initialised and can't be resumed. The SPA no longer deletes a session when you open it (the old "reading an ended session acknowledges + removes it" rule is gone).
- **Hub restart reaps the previous generation's `claude` children before resuming anything** (`server/src/agents/process-reaper.ts`). pm2 restarts the tree with **SIGINT** (a claude child EXITS on it — see `midTurn` below) and the old `shutdown()` SIGTERMed children then `process.exit(0)`ed in the same tick, so anything that had not yet acted on the signal reparented to init with a dead stdio pipe and a LIVE tool loop. Three defences: (1) **boot reap** — the restore loop + AL bootstrap now run inside an async IIFE that first awaits `reapStaleProcesses()` (SIGTERM → 2 s grace → SIGKILL, each reap logged `[reaper] ended stale claude pid …`); (2) **`StaleProcessSweeper`** — 60 s timer, stale twins older than a minute get SIGTERM on first sighting and SIGKILL on the next; (3) **shutdown ends children before exiting** — `Session.terminateForShutdown()` SIGTERMs WITHOUT touching session state, `shutdown()` waits ≤1 s (`waitForExit`) then SIGKILLs survivors, then exits (pm2 `kill_timeout` is 1.6 s — keep the sequence under it). **A stale twin** = argv names `claude` AND carries the hub stdio signature `--input-format stream-json` AND ppid ≠ hub, AND either its env `CONSOLE_HUB_PID` (stamped on every spawn since this landed) names a pid that isn't us, or — for pre-marker processes — its argv names a csid we own: `--session-id <own>` (forks since 2a36c43a) or `--resume <x>` (`csidRefs` → `classifyStale`, pure, unit-tested). Yousef's interactive `claude --resume <csid>` in a terminal has no stream-json flag and is never touched; non-claude processes that merely inherited the marker (an agent's vite, zsh) are ignored. GOTCHAs: a **fork's `--resume` names its PARENT** (`--resume <parent> --fork-session [--session-id <own>]`; pre-2a36c43a forks carry no own id at all) — ownership is tested against every csid in argv and the ppid guard keeps live forks (our children) out regardless, but never assume a process's `--resume` IS the session it serves (`servedCsid`/`describeCsid` report `<parent>+fork` for the legacy shape). **Shutdown must not use `kill()`**: it sets `endedByUser` → the hub's `session.on('exit')` → `saveManifest` would persist `ended: true` for every session and the next boot would skip them all. `kill()` itself now escalates SIGTERM → SIGKILL after 5 s (`KILL_ESCALATE_MS`) so an ended session can't keep a live process either. `/proc` is read directly (Linux-only by design). Verify a restart with `ps -eo pid,ppid,args | grep -- --resume`: exactly one process per session, all with ppid = the hub.
- **Manifest** (`~/.claude/console-hub-sessions.json`, written by `server/src/manifest.ts`): the list the hub uses to re-spawn sessions at startup. Every write is **synchronous and atomic** (`writeFileSync` to a `.tmp` sibling, then `renameSync`) — no in-memory debounce buffer, so a SIGKILL or host crash can't lose pending state. Every session with a `claudeSessionId` is persisted regardless of `status` (yes, including `ended`); removal is driven only by `delete_session`, not by subprocess lifecycle. Subprocess lifecycle can therefore never prune sessions. If you ever need to manually rebuild the manifest from JSONL transcripts on disk: the hub clobbers external writes within ms of any session event, so the only reliable path is **write the new manifest then `SIGKILL` the hub in the same Node tick** (the shutdown handler also calls `saveManifest`, so SIGTERM would still clobber); pm2/tsx will respawn the hub and it'll load the new manifest cleanly. **SIGKILL the node process actually holding :9877 (`ss -tlnp`), NOT the pm2 wrapper pid** — killing the wrapper leaves the old hub serving while pm2's respawns crash-loop on EADDRINUSE, and one old-hub save wipes the edit (tell: a route the new code removed still answers 200).
- **`wasRunning` must come from `Session.midTurn`, never from `status`**. pm2 restarts the hub with **treekill: SIGINT reaches the claude CHILDREN too, and a claude child EXITS on SIGINT** (measured: `kill -INT <child>` → process gone, hub's exit handler runs with `shuttingDown` still false → `status = 'ended'`). Those child exits routinely land *before* the hub's own signal handler, so a manifest keyed on `status === 'running'` recorded every mid-turn session as not-running: they restored hibernated and silent, and **nobody got the "The hub was restarted, which interrupted you. Continue." nudge**. `midTurn` is the durable shadow of a turn: set in `sendMessage`, cleared ONLY by a real `result` or a user `interrupt()` — never by a process death, which is the entire point. `shutdown()` also calls `session.markShuttingDown()` for every session *before* saving, so a child dying during the save can't flip state either. When touching this path: a lost nudge is invisible (nothing errors, agents just sit), so assert on BOTH ends of the log — `Shutting down — saving manifest (N mid-turn: <names>)` names what the old process saved, and `Resumed + continued:` must appear for exactly those on the restore side. **The subtle leak: SIGINT mid-tool-call makes the CLI emit `result/error_during_execution is_error=true` and THEN exit 0** — that result reached `handleResultMessage` before the exit and cleared `midTurn`, so `markShuttingDown()` froze an already-cleared bit. Error results (`is_error` or `subtype` starting `error`) no longer clear `midTurn`; only a success result or `interrupt()` does. Probe recipe: spawn `claude -p --output-format stream-json --input-format stream-json --verbose --dangerously-skip-permissions --model haiku`, send a user message that runs `sleep 40` via Bash, `kill('SIGINT')` 3 s after the `tool_use`, log every stdout `type/subtype` until exit. **Anything computed at SHUTDOWN needs TWO restarts to observe**: the restart that deploys the change still runs the OLD process's shutdown, so its manifest carries the old derivation. The first real test is the restart after the deploying one.

### Sessions are the only agent entity (durable roles removed)
The durable-role system (`~/.config/console/agents/<key>.md` files, `AgentRegistry`, charter injection, org chart, park/revive, manager tree) was **torn out** — sessions are now the only entity, either **active** or **dormant** (hibernated). Where each concern went:
- **Charter** → the project's CLAUDE.md (the session's `cwd` picks it up natively — the hub injects no prose).
- **Memory** → Claude Code's own auto-memory, pinned per cwd by the hub (`~/.claude/projects/<encoded cwd>/memory/` — see Spaces). Retired role files: `~/.config/console/agents.retired-2026-08-26/`.
- **Identity** → `agentKey` stays: a plain slug on the session (minted at `create_session{asAgent}` / ticket-fork), persisted in the manifest, used for board `@key` addressing and the `CONSOLE_AGENT_KEY` env → `X-Console-Agent` actor attribution. No file behind it.
- **Space binding** → `project`/`areas` fields on Session/ManifestEntry/SessionInfo (set at create/fork; forks inherit the source's). Drives the Spaces agent panel, board default-owner resolution, and the board-path pointer injected at spawn.
- **Hierarchy** → fork lineage ONLY (`parentClaudeSessionId`). Merge-into-parent, rail indentation, and `#blocked` escalation (fork parent, else Al) all resolve through it. No manager edges.
- **What still injects at spawn** (keyed sessions, fresh spawn only): `buildBoardProtocol()` + a one-line `@key` identity + the project board path. Al keeps his bespoke `buildAlSystemPrompt`.
- **Removed surface (do not re-add)**: WS `list_agents`/`set_manager`/`get_agent_role`/`revive_agent`/`delete_role`/`create_folder`/`rename_role`, `agents_list`/`agent_role` broadcasts, HTTP `GET/POST /agents/roles`, CLI `con agent role*`/`con agent revive`, `fork_session.seedRole`, SPA `AgentOrgChart`/`AgentProfilePanel`/`AgentInfoDialog`/org undo-redo/`agentViewMode`, `reviveAgentRole`/`reloadAgentRole`/`reapForkRole`/`buildOrgPosition`/`renderOrgRoster`/`buildChildMergeRequest`, the `index.ts` role backfills. "Parked" no longer exists — a session with no live process is dormant and wakes on message; an ended session is gone.

### Board-driven delegation — vault kanban boards ARE the task store (`server/src/kanban/`)
The old `agent-tasks.json` delegation system (delegate/report RPC verbs, TasksPanel, `con agent delegate|report|tasks`) was replaced: work assignment now lives in **plain-markdown Obsidian-Kanban boards in the vault** — human- and agent-editable with normal file tools, synced by Syncthing, no parallel store, no RPC.
- **Format** (`kanban/board.ts`, pure + lossless — round-trip verified byte-identical on all four real vault boards): Obsidian Kanban files (`kanban-plugin: board` frontmatter, `## Column` headings, `- [ ] card` lines, `%% kanban:settings` footer). Card grammar extensions are TRAILING tokens the plugin renders as harmless text/tags: `@agentkey` = assignment, `^blockid` = Obsidian block-ref syntax, **stamped only by the hub at dispatch** — the id IS the dispatch marker (unstamped = not yet dispatched; deleting the id re-arms the card) — and `#blocked` = stuck-as-a-PROPERTY (card keeps its column/queue position; the old Blocked columns still parse), and `#model/<alias-or-id>` — or the bare shorthand `#haiku`/`#sonnet`/`#opus`/`#fable` (`MODEL_ALIASES`, the CLI's alias set; the serializer writes aliases back in the short form, ids behind `#model/`) — = the ticket-fork spawns pinned to that model (`modelOverride` pass-through in `forkRoleSessionForTicket`; use aliases `haiku`/`sonnet`/`opus`/`fable` — they resolve via the `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_*_MODEL` env so they stay valid on both backends; ignored with a log line on `#nofork`/direct wakes since pinning would leak onto the session's whole model). **An alias whose env key is MISSING falls back to the CLI's baked-in Bedrock default for that family — an OLD version, billed untagged.** `syncBackendSettings()` re-bakes all six keys at every boot; `taggedModelId` deliberately passes aliases through, so the env is the ONLY thing standing between an alias and the CLI default. Set via the modal's Model pill or `con spaces board <p> model "^id" <alias|none>`. `#effort/<level>` (`low|medium|high|xhigh|max`; `#effort:<level>` read-compatible) = the ticket-fork's `--effort` pin (`con spaces board <p> effort "^id" <level|none>`; forks run `high` by policy — see "`--effort` is chosen per spawn KIND"). Indented lines under a card are continuations (details) — and so is a bare NON-blank line directly under a card's own lines (^loud-pony: a dictated 2nd paragraph once landed as a raw newline inside the card line; all three parser ports — hub, SPA `src/kanban/board.ts`, Kotlin `KanbanCodec` — keep it on the card verbatim, lossless; a blank line ends the run so prose after a gap stays an interstitial). Writers never produce that shape any more: `BoardOps.add`/`edit` and the APK's `CardContent.splitHeadAndDetail` split a newline-bearing text into card + detail lines. `parseCardTokens` requires tokens to be trailing so `alice@example.com` / `2^10` mid-text never match.
- **Dispatch** (`kanban/dispatch.ts` pure + `kanban/watcher.ts` `BoardWatcher`, wired in `index.ts`): the watcher polls the vault via `NoteStore.listSince` (mtime — same primitive as mobile notes sync; NOT fs.watch, the vault is written from every direction), classifies every changed `.md` (`isKanbanBoard`), and for each **assigned + unstamped + unchecked card under an In-Progress-like column** (`DISPATCH_COLUMN_RE`: in progress/doing/active/now): stamps a `^id` into the file (readable `adjective-animal` pair from `mintBlockId`, **clash-checked against every ^id on every board — a boot pre-scan of all boards before any stamp, plus every parse thereafter, never pruned — AND every session whose key ends `-<id>-fork` or whose title is `forkTitle(id)`, ended/hibernated included** (`BoardWatcher.usedIds` + the `isIdTaken` hook, `sessionCarriesBlockId`); the ledger/wind-down/reopen are all keyed by id alone, so a reused id sends one card's approve/reopen wakes to another's fork (astera ^gray-stag/^spry-kite, 2026-09-09). `forkRoleSessionForTicket` logs `fork key CLASH` if `mintAgentKey` still had to suffix the key — a `…-fork-N` key is unresolvable by the `-<id>-fork` peel), then wakes the assignee's live session (`wakeSession`; no live session with that @key = no dispatch, stamp stays off) with a self-instructing `[BOARD TASK]` envelope naming the board file. **Backlog is "planned", not "go"** — only dispatch columns trigger. **Unassigned cards dragged into In Progress auto-assign to the project's default owner**: board frontmatter `default_owner: <agentKey>` wins; else the "* general" convention over the project's bound live sessions (`resolveDefaultOwner` in `kanban/dispatch.ts`: one → it; several → the `-general`-suffixed one; else first by key, logged as a convention pick). Unresolvable → the card stays unassigned and undispatched. **Setting the owner**: right-click a session in Spaces → "Set as project owner" (toggles to Unset; shown only for keyed sessions bound to a PROJECT; the owner's row glyph is a bot WEARING a crown (`BotCrowned` in `src/components/icons/`, a lucide-style composite; same state colour as the plain Bot — no separate amber crown) — and it marks owners **by convention too**: `src/spaces/owner.ts` `effectiveOwnerKey()` mirrors the hub's `resolveDefaultOwner` picking order (frontmatter `default_owner:` wins; else the single bound root session; else the `-general` one; else first by key — keep in sync when the server's order changes). The context menu reads Unset / "Pin as project owner (owner by convention)" / Set accordingly. Rail 1's alert rows crown the owner too, and **a project whose only alerted agent is its own root owner shows no row** (`soleOwner` in the `alertsBySlug` memo) — the badge already carries the state and clicking the project lands on the owner) → `POST /board/:project/owner {agent|null}` → `BoardOps.setDefaultOwner` → pure `setBoardDefaultOwner` (header-only mutation: replaces/inserts the line inside the fence, prepends a fence when absent). CLI `con spaces board <p> owner <key|none>`. `listSpaces` ships `defaultOwner` per project so the SPA never loads the board to know it (optional-guarded `?? null`). **Deploy-gated boards**: board frontmatter `deploy_gate: review` (parsed by `boardDeployGate` in `kanban/board.ts`, threaded through `BoardDispatch` → the envelope) flips the envelope's worktree stanza — the agent must NOT merge to main (merge = deploy on auto-deploy repos like Astera's Vercel app); it pushes its branch, notes branch + preview URL on the card at Under Review, and merges only after the card owner approves. Ungated boards keep fold-into-main (Console itself stays trunk-based). All state is in the board files: a hub restart re-derives everything by re-reading boards (stamps prevent double-fires); a card assigned DURING an outage dispatches at next boot. **CONCURRENCY CAP**: at most `boards.maxRunningForks` cards (prefs key, default `DEFAULT_MAX_RUNNING_FORKS` = 4) may have a live worker at once, counted MACHINE-WIDE across every board (`BoardWatcher.runningForks()` = stamped cards in a dispatch column, not review/done/blocked, whose `agentKey` still resolves to a live session — so a dead fork frees its slot, and Under-Review cards never hold one since their forks idle waiting on Yousef). Board frontmatter `max_forks: N` (`boardMaxForks`) overrides the default for that board's cards against the same shared total; `0` parks the board. Over the cap the card is left **UNSTAMPED and unassigned-by-us** — an ordinary board card, nothing on it explains the wait — and recorded in the watcher's in-memory `pending` map keyed by `boardPath + text` (no ^id exists yet) with the timestamp it was FIRST seen dispatchable. `drainQueue()` dispatches the backlog **oldest-first across all boards** (global FIFO = the order Yousef moved cards, not the order files happen to be polled) whenever capacity frees: every poll (which is also how "a card left In Progress" is noticed), at boot, and immediately on session end (`AgentContext.onWorkerEnded` → `session.on('exit')`). Selection is FIFO but stamping is grouped per board so each file is read/written once, and the stamp still lands BEFORE the wake (crash mid-dispatch leaves the card visibly stamped rather than silently re-firing). Stamp REPAIRS (the duplicate-fork guard) bypass the cap — they restore an id that already existed. `listSpaces` ships `queuedCount` per project (`ListSpacesOpts.queuedFor`, wired from `boardWatcher.queuedCards()`), which the SPA renders as a `queued (N)` chip on dispatch columns. **An INHERITED ticket-fork's FIRST turn is a `/compact`** (fresh-context forks — the default — have nothing to compact and take the plain wake; pref `boards.compactForksOnSpawn`, default on, live — `DEFAULT_COMPACT_FORKS_ON_SPAWN`): `wakeForkCompacted()` in `routes/agents.ts` sends `buildForkCompactPrompt()` (`/compact <focus on identity/rules/state, drop tool output + hand-back chatter>`) and `queueMessage()`s the card envelope + card images behind it, so the queue flush on the compaction's `result` delivers the card (`queuedImages` ride the flush; the manifest keeps only the text, so a restart mid-compaction drops the pictures — the board lines still name them). Why: `--fork-session` copies the source's whole transcript (~70k tokens of it at first request, fleet median) and the fork re-reads it on every one of its ~490 requests — 24 % of all cache_read spend; the CLI's own tool-result clearing never runs for hub sessions (see the Context-cost bullet under Agents). Verified over stream-json: `system/compact_boundary trigger:manual`, 40k → 1.2k conversation tokens, ~11 s, then a normal `result` (a failed compaction still yields one, so the envelope is never stranded). `#nofork` direct wakes and conversation forks (`con agent chat`, manual Fork) are NOT compacted — inheriting context is their point. The dispatch envelope carries `load: {running, cap}` and, when more than one card is being worked, tells the fork to run heavy steps (typecheck/tests/lint/build) through the repo's serialising wrapper — Console's is `scripts/heavy.sh <cmd>` / `npm run heavy -- <cmd>`, a `flock` on `/tmp/console-heavy.lock` shared by every worktree (it did not exist until 2026-09-15; two forks reported the envelope pointing at a phantom) — exists because uncapped forks saturated the single disk (iowait ~58 %, load 21, the board CLI itself timing out).
- **A card token is parsed TWICE and needs a button.** The server (`server/src/kanban/board.ts`) and the SPA port (`src/kanban/board.ts`) each carry `parseCardTokens`, the `sanitizeCardText` regex, `cardFirstLine`'s token order and the loop bound; a tag added to the server alone has no control in the card, renders as a generic hashtag, and STOPS the client's trailing-token loop so every token to its left goes unparsed there. `#forge`/`#local` and `#effort/…` shipped server + CLI only and got their pills on 9 Oct 2026 (Yousef: "It's not in the UI… We have buttons for everything else I think"). New token = both parsers + a `CardDetailModal` pill + a tile badge + a case in `src/__tests__/kanban-board-model.test.ts`, same commit.
- **Hand-back contract — Yousef reads the CARD, not the transcript, often days later.** Before moving a card to Under Review the agent (1) `note`s a concise `- ` bulleted summary of EXACTLY what it did (what changed, how verified, decisions he must make), (2) `attach`es screenshots wherever a visual check helps — REQUIRED when it worked in a worktree or touched UI (he can't easily run the worktree), (3) only then `move`s, and ends its turn with the same bullets. All wording lives in ONE place, `kanban/dispatch.ts` (`handbackStanza` in the envelope, `handbackWarning`, `buildReviewReminder`; the protocol stanza mirrors it) so the four surfaces can't drift. Enforcement is software at the moment it matters, not memory of the envelope: `BoardOps.move` into a `REVIEW_COLUMN_RE` column returns `warning` when the card has no `- ` bullets (`hasSummaryBullets`) and the CLI prints it; and **every human message reaching a session whose `@key` owns an Under-Review card gets a stdin-only `[BOARD — you own a card in Under Review]` reminder appended** (`withReviewReminder` in `routes/agents.ts`, fed by `BoardWatcher.reviewCardsFor` off the in-flight ledger; wired into `send_message` AND `injectToSession` so PTT/ring dictation count too) (feedback = move it back to In Progress FIRST). The transcript keeps Yousef's own words; `/clear` is never decorated. **`note` is multi-line safe** (`detailLines` splits on `\n` → one indented detail line each); same for `block --note`, `add/edit --detail`. **`attach`** (`POST /board/:project/attach {card, image: base64, ext?, caption?}` → `BoardOps.attach`): accepts png/jpg/gif/webp stills AND webm/mp4 clips (`ATTACH_IMAGE_EXTS`/`ATTACH_VIDEO_EXTS`, all capped at `MAX_ATTACH_BYTES` = 20 MB — the CLI refuses oversize files before base64-ing them), writes `board/card-<ts>-<blockId>.<ext>` to the vault's sibling assets dir (`CARD_ASSET_DIR`, same dir as the SPA paste-upload in `src/kanban/card-images.ts`) — the SPA's `CardDetailModal` reaches the same dir via paste OR its footer picker/camera (^snug-fox: `<input type=file>` → `prepareCardMedia` in `card-images.ts` downscales stills to 2000 px like WriteActionBar's `downscaleImage` (now shared in `src/utils/downscale-image.ts`) and the pure gate `src/kanban/card-media.ts` mirrors these same ext/20 MB caps — `PUT /notes/asset/` itself enforces nothing) and appends `![caption](board/…)` (default caption `screenshot` / `clip`). Stills render as thumbnails and ride any later dispatch as real image attachments (`cardImagePaths` = stills only); clips render as a play tile (`CardClipTile` in SpacesTab — fetches nothing until clicked, then the global lightbox plays the blob in `video` mode via `useUiStore.lightboxKind`) and, since the API takes no video, the envelope names them as `(video clip, not viewable by you: <abs path>)` instead (`assetsAbsPath` opt). `isVideoAsset`/`cardMediaPaths`/`cardVideoPaths` live in `kanban/board.ts` (server + client port). Resolves the card BEFORE writing so a bad query leaves no orphan asset. **`assets/board/`, never `assets/images/`:** the vault's `.eleventy.js` publishes `assets/` by a dir allow-list (`publicAssetDirs`) and `images/` is ON it — an unlisted dir is private by construction; never add `board` to that list, and never write agent artefacts into a listed dir. Older `images/card-*` detail lines still render (the hub serves any assets path) and stay unpublished via the site's exclusion.
- **Completion is a file edit, not an RPC — and agents never self-certify**: the agent moves its line under `## Under Review` (`REVIEW_COLUMN_RE`); only a human/manager moves it on to `## Done`. Stuck = append `#blocked` + an indented note. Done cards STAY in the file (transition diff + history) but the SPA board hides the Done column. **A Yousef comment on an Under-Review card's session re-opens it**: the protocol stanza + dispatch envelope instruct the assignee to move the card back to In Progress while addressing the comment, then back to Under Review — the board is Yousef's attention queue, Under Review always means "ready for review". Under Review wakes NOBODY (Yousef reviews via the live board + rail markers; SyncBus broadcast only); #blocked raises the assignee session's attention marker (`flagAttention("Blocked: <text>")` — red badge + push + Inbox attention band, ^mild-ibis) and wakes its fork parent, else Al (actionable). **Done winds the ticket-fork down** (`buildWindDownEnvelope` + the `t.done` branch in index.ts `onTransition`) — **silently when it can (2026-09-30, ^spry-bear)**: `kanban/winddown.ts` `probeSilentWindDown(cwd, blockId)` lists the repo's worktrees (`<cwd>/repo` realpath, else cwd's toplevel) and takes every one whose path basename or branch carries the card's ^id (autowt slugs are card-derived by convention); if there are none, or each is `git status --porcelain`-clean AND its branch is an ancestor of the main branch (`symbolic-ref origin/HEAD`, else main/master), the hub removes them (`git worktree remove` + `branch -d` — both refuse on their own if a check was wrong) and calls `mergeIntoParent(…, { absorb: 'queue', summary: summaryFromCardLines(text, lines) })` — the card's approved `- ` bullets ARE the digest — so the fork is folded in and killed **without a single wake** (log: `wound down silently (…)`). Any dirty/unmerged/unremovable worktree, a `deploy_gate` board, a fork mid-turn, or a failed merge falls through to the old wake path (log says why). Live pref `boards.silentWindDown: false` forces the always-wake behaviour. Why: 85 wind-down wakes/wk resumed parked 300–600k forks to delete a worktree and restate the card — $594/wk, 92% of it the rewrite. Otherwise the fork gets `[CARD APPROVED — wind down]` (gated boards: approval means the PARENT merges — the fork never does, it pushes the gated head and leaves PR number + gate run on the card and in its summary (Astera rule 48; ^teal-yak/^lean-owl, 2026-09-15); ungated: verify folded + remove its own worktree with `git worktree remove <path> && git branch -d <branch>` — NEVER `autowt cleanup --mode …`, which sweeps every merged worktree including siblings' live ones (bit wise-hawk and mild-ibis), and `autowt cleanup <slug> -y` needs a TTY forks don't have), and when its wind-down turn ends the hub runs `mergeIntoParent` — summary folded into the parent, fork killed. Non-fork sessions moved to Done just carry on. The watcher diffs in-flight (stamped) cards per poll and fires `onTransition` on any state CHANGE (any state CHANGE, not only open→closed — review→done must fire the wind-down). Board-file edits from scripts: always match whole LINES, never `line + '\n'` string-replace — the file's last line often has no trailing newline, so such replaces miss silently. Stale in-progress cards get nudged max 2× (`onStale`, 30-min steps). **Moving a stamped card BACK into a dispatch column re-activates it** (`onReopen`): findDispatchable skips stamped cards, so review/done→In Progress, un-`#blocked`-in-place, and stamped-Backlog→In Progress all fire the reopen path instead — live assignee gets a `[BOARD TASK — reopened]` nudge; a DEAD assignee (fork killed by wind-down or hub restart) gets a full re-dispatch: the fork-key convention (`<source>-<blockId>-fork`) peels back to the source role (else board `default_owner`), a fresh ticket-fork spawns with the original envelope + accumulated notes, and the board line is reassigned to it (string return, same contract as onDispatch). The stale watchdog re-arms either way. Manual escape hatch when no column move happened: `con board <p> redispatch "^id"` (`POST /board/:project/redispatch` → `BoardWatcher.redispatch`; refuses Done cards and unstamped cards). **Editing an OPEN in-flight card's content re-pings the assignee** (`onCardEdited` — `InFlightCard.content` diffed per poll, column moves/transitions/boot excluded): working sessions don't re-read their card unprompted, so added instructions were invisible until this. Self-echo suppressed via `BoardOps.lastActor` (board-actors.json, written on every `/board/*` mutation carrying `X-Console-Agent`, now with `op` + move `column`): an assignee's own report within `SELF_ECHO_WINDOW_MS` (60 s — the watcher polls every 10 s) doesn't ping it back. **The same guard covers `onReopen`**: an assignee that correctly moves its own card back to In Progress on feedback must not receive its own `[BOARD TASK — reopened]` nudge. GOTCHA: the SPA's whole-file writes record NO actor, so a stale ledger entry from the agent's earlier `move … "Under Review"` would mask Yousef's SPA drag-back — the reopen guard therefore requires `op === 'move'` AND `column` === the dispatch column it landed in, never the actor alone.
- **Protocol preamble** (`agents/org-protocol.ts` `buildBoardProtocol()`): the hub-owned "how work flows" stanza appended to every keyed session's `--append-system-prompt` at the `createSession` choke point and to Al's persona, plus a one-line `@key` self-identity and the project board path. (The org roster/position injection died with durable roles.)
- **Watchdog semantics**: nudges are per-card via the poll loop (no more separate 5-min `runTaskWatchdog` interval); a worker with a pending AskUserQuestion/plan approval (`Session.approvalPending`) is waiting on YOUSEF, so `isWorkerWaitingOnUser` skips it without burning a nudge AND restarts the card's stale clock — the first nudge comes 30 min after his answer, never the instant he answers (^snug-seal skipped but left the clock running, so both nudges fired 10 s apart the moment an overnight question was answered — ^red-loon). Hub-side: `tsx` without `watch`, so a watcher fix is live only after `con hub restart`.
- **Ticket-forks are FRESH-context by default** — a new session pinned with `--session-id` at the parent's cwd (CLAUDE.md + auto-memory arrive natively) plus a digest of the parent's conversation in the envelope; the `--fork-session` transcript copy re-read the parent's whole history every message. Opt back in per card with a trailing `#inherit` (`con spaces board <p> inherit|fresh "^id"`) or board-wide with frontmatter `fork_context: inherit`; a reopen honours the same choice; only inherited forks get the `/compact`-first wake. `con agent fork-cost` compares cache_read per turn by mode. Mechanics (spawn options, digest template, ledger, identity rules): skill `ticket-forks`.
- **Fork branch-point seed**: a UI fork (`fork_session` with `seed:true`, set by the SPA `forkSession`) gets a `[FORK — branched from <parent>]` marker (`buildForkSeed` in `merge.ts`) so it knows where its own branch begins vs the inherited history — makes a later merge summary crisp. `con agent chat` forks pass no `seed` (they inject their own side-conversation seed). **GOTCHA (load-bearing): `claude --fork-session` emits NO `system` init — and writes no transcript — until it receives a first message.** The seed is therefore sent **immediately** after the fork spawns, NOT on `session_init` (which would deadlock: silent fork → no message → no init → a dead, unusable fork — exactly the symptom when forking a large session). This is why `con agent chat` forks work (they message immediately) and why a `seed:false` UI fork would hang. **Fork csids are hub-minted and pinned:** `Session` generates the fork's `claudeSessionId` in its constructor and spawns `--resume <parent> --fork-session --session-id <own>` (the CLI honours the pin). So a fork is in the manifest/`list_sessions` from birth, its argv NAMES ITSELF (`--session-id` = own id, `--resume` = parent's), and the board envelope's IDENTITY stanza quotes that id so the reader can prove it is the addressee from `ps -o args= -p $PPID`. Before the pin, a fork's argv named only the parent — a fork that read its own args believed it WAS the parent, and nothing matching processes by `--resume` could tell parent from fork. If a CLI ever ignores the pin, init still wins (`rekeyedFrom` + a status line). Remaining edge: a hub restart in the seconds between fork spawn and its first init finds a manifest csid with no JSONL → the pruned-transcript fresh-respawn path runs (fork loses the inherited context).
- **Merge-back — forks** (`server/src/agents/merge.ts` pure builders + `mergeIntoParent`/`captureNextTurn` in `routes/agents.ts`; `mergeFork` is a back-compat alias): fold a fork's knowledge into its parent instead of discarding it. The fork self-summarises (`captureNextTurn` injects a merge-request and captures its next turn's text — INACTIVITY-based window, hard ceiling 10 min), the digest is injected into the parent, then the fork is closed. A SUMMARY, not the transcript — keeps the parent's context clean. **Parent = fork lineage only** (`parentClaudeSessionId` → the live source session); guards: child must be idle; must be a fork; parent must be live; no self-merge; if no summary is captured the child is left alive. **The parent also absorbs the child's live crons**: `mergeIntoParent` calls `ctx.reassignCron` to re-key every ACTIVE cron task onto the parent before the child is killed (skip counters reset; disabled tasks stay dead). Tests in `server/src/__tests__/cron-reassign.test.ts`. Surface: protocol `merge_session`/`session_merged`; CLI `con agent merge <id|conv|name>` + `con agent chat --id <conv> --merge`; UI "Merge into parent" in session context menus (forks only). The reversible alternative to killing.
- **Hand-off**: Al emits `@handoff(<agentKey>)` (sentinel parsed by `server/src/handoff.ts`, mirrors `attention.ts`; scanned on finalized text in `session.ts`, emits `session_handoff`). The SPA shows an opt-in "Talk to X" banner (store `pendingHandoff` → `acceptHandoff`) + a "↩ Back to Al" control (`handoffReturnTo`); the raw sentinel is stripped from Al's displayed text. **WhatsApp/voice hand-off is out of scope** — Al relays there.
- **Removed surface** (do not re-add): protocol `delegate`/`report`/`cancel_task`/`tasks_list`/`tasks`; HTTP `/agents/tasks*`; CLI `con agent delegate|report|tasks`; SPA `TasksPanel.tsx`, org-chart task badges + animated delegation edges, `useAgentStore.tasks/delegate/cancelTask`; `agents/{tasks,delegation,delegation-protocol}.ts`; `~/.config/console/agent-tasks.json` (file left on disk, no longer read). Ephemeral `--ephemeral` workers went with it — parallel throwaway work is `con agent create` or a fork.
- **`con spaces board` — the SOFTWARE mutation layer** (`kanban/board-ops.ts` + `routes/board.ts`): agents/CLI never hand-edit board markdown. `BoardOps` runs every mutation as parse→mutate→serialize inside a **per-board-path lock shared with the BoardWatcher** (`kanban/board-files.ts` `BoardFiles` — ONE instance in `index.ts` passed to both; fresh re-read within the lock) so concurrent writers serialize with no lost updates; a thrown op doesn't wedge the queue. **Board writes are safe by construction — four layers** (a non-atomic write once let the watcher persist a 64 KiB prefix as the whole astera board): (1) **`NoteStore.write`/`writeConditional`/`writeBinary` are atomic** (temp `.<name>.<pid>.<rand>.tmp` in the same dir + `rename`; readers see old or new, never a prefix; mtime semantics unchanged — stat after rename). (2) **One lock**: the watcher's stamp/reassign/reopen paths run inside `BoardFiles.mutate` against a FRESH read (classification stays lock-free); the reassign after `onDispatch` is its own locked section so a conflict retry can never fork a card twice. Writes are conditional on the read's mtime (`NoteConflictError` → one automatic retry with a fresh read). (3) **Size-verified reads + shrink guard**: `readWithMeta` now returns `size`; a read whose byte length ≠ stat size caught a third-party writer (Obsidian saves non-atomically) and is retried, never classified or written back; `BoardIntegrityGuard` keeps the last-good byte length per board and REFUSES a serialization under 60 % of it (`[boards] refusing suspicious write`, `BoardWriteRefused` → the CLI call fails loudly, and it surfaces on Home's alerts via `boardRefusals` in `gatherAlerts`). A legitimate shrink re-baselines once the smaller size has held steady across polls for `REBASELINE_MS` (15 s) — a write in flight changes size between polls, a halved board does not. Never add a trailing-newline heuristic — `serializeBoard` emits none. (4) **Hub-side journal** (`~/.config/console/board-journal/<path-with-__>/<epoch>.md`, last 100 per board, byte-identical copies deduped): every locked write first copies the CURRENT file; `con spaces board <p> history` lists them, `con spaces board <p> restore <ts> --confirm` (`POST /board/:project/restore {ts, confirm:true}`) is **HUMAN-ONLY** — overwrites the live board with a copy (the current file is journaled first, so it is reversible; the shrink guard is bypassed for this one deliberate shrink). Tests: `board-files.test.ts` (atomic 2 MB concurrent read, lock ordering CLI-vs-watcher, shrink refusal, size-mismatch retry, conflict retry, journal rotation/dedup, restore). Cards are addressed by `^id` or a UNIQUE text substring — ambiguity is an error listing candidates, never a guess. **An id always wins and an id-SHAPED argument (`<adj>-<noun>`) that matches no card id is an ERROR, never a text search**: a card whose own text quotes `^glad-wolf` is not that card, and a bare `note glad-wolf` used to append a whole hand-back to it and answer `success: true` (astera, 7 Oct 2026). A write that resolved by TEXT returns a `warning` naming the card it hit, and **`note "<card>" --undo` / `--remove-last <n>` (`POST /board/:project/unnote {card, count?}`) takes a note back** — undo removes exactly the lines the last recorded note added (the actor ledger carries `noteLines`), which is what repairing that misfire needed and had no verb for. HTTP: `GET /board/:project` + `POST /board/:project/{cards,move,assign,block,note,owner,model,nofork,edit,remove,redispatch}` (project = slug, board resolved like `listSpaces`, or a vault-relative .md path). CLI verbs: `show|add|move|assign|owner|model|nofork|forkok|block|unblock|note|attach|edit|remove|redispatch` (in the capabilities registry). The dispatch envelope + protocol stanza direct agents to report via the CLI (`… move "^id" "Under Review"`, `… block "^id" --note`). The SPA still writes whole files via `/notes/file/` — the watcher's duplicate-fork guard covers that residual race. **Flags are per-verb allow-listed (`BOARD_FLAGS` in `cli/src/commands/spaces.ts`) — an unknown flag is a JSON `USAGE` error, never a silent drop** (`--column` is an alias of `--to` on `add`). The CLI always emits JSON when piped (`isJsonMode` = `--json` | `--agent` | non-TTY stdout), so non-JSON on stdout in a script means the CLI never ran — usually **zsh not word-splitting an unquoted `$VAR` command string**; spell the command out or use a shell function, and capture raw stdout before piping to jq (memory: `project_board_note_cli_no_dashdash.md`).
- Unit tests: `kanban.test.ts` (parser round-trip + mutations), `kanban-dispatch.test.ts` (dispatchable/in-flight/watcher incl. restart-no-double-fire), `board-ops.test.ts` (query resolution, mutations, concurrency serialization), `merge-orch.test.ts` + `delegation-spawn.test.ts` (fork-only merge + spawn prompt injection).

### Hub-side agent cron (`/cron/*`)
Claude Code's built-in `/loop` / `CronCreate` is session-scoped and DOES NOT survive hub restarts under our SDK transport (`--input-format stream-json`) — empirically verified, matches Anthropic bugs [#40228](https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code/issues/40228) (`durable: true` doesn't persist) and [#40081](https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code/issues/40081) (sessions with cron tasks vanish from `/resume`). So the hub runs its own.
- **Scheduler**: `server/src/cron/scheduler.ts` (Croner + debounced JSON persist to `~/.config/console/agent-cron.json`). Tasks keyed by `claudeSessionId` (stable across restarts; hub session ids are rejected). Forks get a new claudeSessionId so they inherit zero tasks. **A csid is stable across hub restarts, hibernation wakes, `con agent reload <session>`, model/backend respawns — all `--resume`. Exactly two paths mint a NEW csid for an existing session, and both go through `followRekey` (`routes/agents.ts`) — as does `mergeIntoParent`, so a merged child's own live forks re-home onto the grandparent instead of keeping the dead csid: crons + listeners re-key via `reassignSession`, AND every live fork whose `parentClaudeSessionId` was the old csid is re-parented onto the new one (^plum-dove, 2026-09-20 — three al-* ticket forks were left pointing at a dead id after one `reload AL`, so their wind-down digests would have folded into nothing). (1) `con agent reload AL` (fresh persona spawn by design — `reloadAlSession` captures the old csid and follows on the new `session_init`); (2) the pruned-transcript fresh respawn (`session_init` carries `rekeyedFrom` when a pre-set resume csid differs from what the CLI minted; the `hub_message` listener in `createSession` follows).** Fires by mirroring the post-restart "Continue." nudge path: `broadcast(user_prompt) + session.logMessage + session.sendMessage` — **unless the session is mid-turn (`status === 'running'`), in which case the prompt goes through `session.queueMessage()` and lands at `result`** (a stdin write mid-turn interleaves a second user message into the running turn; same prompt already pending → not stacked). Auto-disables a task after 10 consecutive skips. One-shot tasks self-remove after firing. **Every attempt leaves a record** (^plum-goat, 2026-09-19, after a weekly fire vanished on 09-14 with no trace): `fire()` stamps `lastAttemptAt` before its first await and writes `lastOutcome` on every path (`fired` / `queued (…)` / `skipped: <reason>`); a throw inside `fire()` is a recorded skip (`fire threw: …`), not a lost promise; croner's `protect` is real now (the callback returns the promise — it used to `void` it, so protect never blocked) and an overlap is recorded as a skip; `nextFireAt` is persisted and a 60 s **missed-fire sweep** (`sweep()`) records any active task whose `nextFireAt` passed >90 s ago with no attempt as `missed fire due <iso> — scheduler never ran it` (or `hub was not running`, at `start()`) and re-arms its job. An overdue ISO one-shot fires 30 s after detection if <24 h late, else is recorded + disabled. Every fire/skip logs `[cron] <id> <outcome>` to the hub stdout (pm2 `console-server-out.log`). `con cron list` shows `lastAttemptAt`/`lastOutcome`/`nextFireAt` — "did it run", not just "is it scheduled". Tests: `__tests__/cron-outcome.test.ts`.
- **Guard scripts (token-free polling)**: a task may carry an optional `guard` — a shell command run (via `bash -c` in the session's cwd, 60 s cap) at **every** trigger, BEFORE any agent wake. `fire()` is async: it runs the guard first and only proceeds to `sendMessage` when the guard **exits 0**; **exit 1** is the normal "nothing to do" case — skipped silently, **no tokens spent**, task stays scheduled. **Anything else is "could not check"** (`classifyGuardExit`, db8ec2d7): another exit code, a crash recognised from stderr even when it exits 1 (a Python traceback, `command not found`, a syntax error), or the 60 s cap. That is recorded as `guard error: <last stderr line>` and counted in `guardErrorStreak`; at three in a row `noteGuardError` wakes the OWNER once with `[HUB CRON — GUARD FAILING]` (never with the task's prompt), then every 6 h while it lasts, and one good check ends the streak. Why: on 9 Oct 2026 a CLI bug (below) made every `con` call take over a minute, guards shelling out to it died on their own inner timeouts with exit 1, and for four hours every run was logged "guard: no change" — a watcher that cannot look must not look identical to one that saw nothing.
