Write guards to `exit 2` with a line on stderr when they cannot reach what they watch. **The CLI bug, because it will tempt again:** `cli/src/client.ts` probes `/health` to pick https or http and used only the status; `/health` returns the whole session list, and once that passed ~64 KiB (78 sessions, ~04:30) the unread body kept the probe's connection open and the process could not EXIT for 17-74 s after printing its answer. `probeHub` now cancels the body (`cli-probe-exit.test.ts` runs a real process to pin it). Two lessons: any fetch whose body you do not read must cancel it, and a slow CLI is diagnosed by timing its EXIT separately from its answer — `CONSOLE_HUB_URL=https://localhost:9877` was fast, which cleared the hostname and convicted the probe. The guard's stdout (trimmed, 4 KB cap) is appended to the wake prompt (`\n\n--- guard output (\`<cmd>\`) --- \n…`) so the agent knows *what* changed. This is how "wake only when X changed" works with zero cost between changes — e.g. a script that curls a URL, diffs against a saved copy, and `exit 0` only on change. **Guard skips do NOT count toward the 10-miss auto-disable budget** (a guard saying "no change" for months is working, not failing); only session-not-found/ended does. Guard stdout is transient (`guardOutput`, stripped from the persisted JSON); the guard command itself persists and survives restarts. Surface: `POST /cron {…, guard}`, CLI `con cron add … --guard "<cmd>"` or `--guard-file <path>`. **`--guard-file` INLINES the file's contents into the cron record** (the path is not referenced), so later edits to the script don't propagate — remove + re-add the task, or use `--guard "bash ~/exec/x.sh"` when you want the file to stay live. `runOnce`/`con cron run` also evaluates the guard (a manual run of a guarded task only wakes if the guard passes).
- **`--fork [--model <alias>]` (2026-09-30, ^spry-bear)**: a task with `fork: true` fires into a FRESH single-turn fork of the owning session instead of the session — `forkHooks.spawnFork` → `forkSessionForWake(ctx, source, 'Cron', task.id, task.model)` (`routes/agents.ts`; the same helper listeners' `--fork` uses, key `<ownerKey>-<taskId>-fork`, title `Cron <id> (fork)`), envelope = `buildCronForkIdentity` (agentKey, csid argv self-check, "closed when this turn ends — durable output goes on a card / emit / notification; never touch the owner's cron") + the prompt + guard output; `reapForkAfterTurn` (`agents/wake.ts`, shared with listeners) closes it 2 s after its first `result` unless it pinged Yousef or 30 min pass silent. The owner must still be live (cwd, CLAUDE.md, auto-memory, lineage come from it); its transcript is never touched. **Why**: six crons fired into 500k–1M hosts cost $917/wk, 85–95% of it the host's cold rewrite before the check even ran (`4E562Sg` $386/wk alone) — a fork is ~$1/fire. `lastOutcome` reads `forked → <name> [on <model>]`. Without hooks (tests) a fork task records a skip. `--model` without `--fork` is a CLI usage error. **Convert an existing task in place with `con cron edit <id> --fork [--model x] [--force]`** (`PATCH /cron/:id` → `scheduler.update`; same own-tasks-only rule as remove, but NO owner wake — remove + re-add would cost the 500k host exactly the rewrite `--fork` exists to avoid). `--no-fork` reverts and drops the model.
- **Removal is guarded, logged and announced** (2026-09-15, after Astera general bulk-removed every task whose prompt contained "merge" and silently took out the weekly mobile sweep). `con cron list` is FLEET-WIDE, so never select tasks to remove by a substring of your own prompt — filter on your csid. `DELETE /cron/:id` from an agent (`X-Console-Agent` set) 403s unless the task is bound to that agent's own session or `?force=1` / `con cron remove <id> --force` is passed; human clients (SPA, agent-key-less CLI) are unrestricted. Every removal logs `[cron] removed <id> (owner …) by <actor>`; a removal by anyone but the owner wakes the owning session with `[HUB CRON REMOVED]` + the full prompt (agent actor → "likely a mistake, re-register if needed" + a push; human actor → "intentional, do not re-add"). Tests: `__tests__/cron-remove-guard.test.ts`.
- **Routes** (`server/src/routes/cron.ts`): `GET /cron[?session=…]`, `POST /cron`, `DELETE /cron/:id[?force=1]`, `POST /cron/:id/run`, `GET /cron/upcoming?n=N`, `GET /cron/ics-token` (returns `{token, publicUrl}`), `GET /cron.ics?token=…` (constant-time compare; 403 on bad token; `Cache-Control: max-age=300`).
- **ICS**: `server/src/cron/ics.ts` expands each task into discrete VEVENTs (next 50 firings, 30-day window) via `Croner.nextRun()` — avoids the lossy cron→RRULE conversion. UTC timestamps, RFC5545 line folding + escaping. Calendar clients re-fetch periodically.
- **CLI**: `con cron list/add/remove/run/upcoming/ics-url`. `--trigger` accepts a 5-field cron, ISO datetime, or `+30m`/`+2h`/`+1d` (CLI resolves relative to ISO). `--once` flips recurring=false (implied for ISO/relative).
- **SPA**: `src/components/agent/CronPill.tsx` (status-bar chip) + `CronPanel.tsx` (side panel: list, run-now/delete, recurring/one-shot create form with live validation, ICS subscription URL with copy). Backed by `src/store/cron.ts` (polled, no WS sync in v1).
- **Public exposure for GCal**: Caddy serves the ICS at `https://con.amar.io/public/cron.ics?token=…` (the `/public/*` namespace is in the auth allow-list). Legacy `/cron.ics` is Caddy-rewritten to the new path so existing GCal subscriptions keep working. `getPublicIcsBase()` returns `CONSOLE_PUBLIC_ORIGIN` (default `https://con.amar.io`). Token query string is the only auth — don't share it.

### Glasses (G1)
**Native Kotlin BLE, APK-only; the hub is a dumb pipe** (`/glasses/*` → WS RPC over `/push` → `GlassesService`), the phone is the single source of truth for connection state. Wire protocol: `docs/g1-protocol.md` (the g1-reverse firmware decompile is the PRIMARY source, MentraOS second). **Never write to the SMP GATT service `8d53dc1d-…`** (firmware update; MCUboot is overwrite-only with no recovery). Every hub push also fans out as a native `0x4B` card (gated by `GlassesConfig` + DnD; needs the `0x04` app-whitelist sent on connect); head-tilt-up shows the hub-driven HUD; the SPA mirror re-asserts on head-down. **Everything else — mirror/stealth screen, HUD layout, notifications + `msgId`, teleprompter (`0x09`), countdown timer (`0x07`), the §19 primitives, mic → STT, the research log, battery/wear/case events — is the `glasses` skill** (`.claude/skills/glasses/SKILL.md`; auto-loads on `server/src/glasses/**`, `src/glasses/**`, the Android glasses code, `docs/g1-*.md`; otherwise `Read` it). Several encoders (teleprompter, timer, dismiss/status/unpair) have NOT been on the wire yet — the first real invocation is the verification, `con glasses research tail` right after.

### Pebble Index 01 ring (`con ring`) — webhook archive + voice-command tree
A smart ring with one button + a mic (Core Devices; single click-hold = the ring's own note agent, double click-hold = configurable). Its app can POST every recording to ONE webhook **in addition to** its own agent run, and **never shows the HTTP response** — so Console's feedback is a push notification, never the reply body. Docs: `https://help.repebble.com/en/articles/15724406-index-advanced-features-mcp-webhook`. Yousef's intent: a `con`-like tree of short commands, spoken to **projects and lists, not agents** — `log dream …`, `add movies …`, `add console …`, `message mum …` — AL as the final fallback. **There is deliberately NO `agent <name>` verb** (Yousef's rule — an early version had one and was rejected) — `add <project> …` files a card and the board forks the agent; AL is reached only as the unclaimed-text fallback.
- **Contract in one paragraph** (full detail in the **`ring` skill** — `.claude/skills/ring/SKILL.md`; auto-loads on `server/src/ring/**`, `server/src/lists/**`, `cli/src/commands/ring.ts`; otherwise `Read` it): every delivery is archived append-only (`~/.config/console/ring/recordings/`) behind the normal bearer wall (`ring` scope, `con ring setup`); the command tree is DATA in the vault note `projects/console/ring-schema.md` (`canonical: [ways to say it]`, hot-reloaded, last-good on a broken edit; `con ring schema --check`); routing is deterministic first (verb → target → payload, fuzzy ≤1 edit, head punctuation stripped ONCE by `headWords` — extend `HEAD_PUNCT`, never a per-rule regex; a target inside the opening SENTENCE is a `weak` rescue), then a hub-STT re-hearing of the audio's first 10 s when the rules did not firmly match (the ring mis-hears the HEAD — "Look," for "Log dream."), LLM classifier last — and it only CLASSIFIES (`lead_in`); the payload is always the transcript's own words, a paraphrase is refused — AL only as the unclaimed-text fallback via the `AL ↔ ring` conversation fork. **Rules that were corrections — do not regress**: no `agent <name>` verb; `message <person>` sends AS YOUSEF via his Matrix account (never relayed through AL; `message al` is a WhatsApp send to AL's DM — do NOT reroute it to `al.direct`); `voice <person> <speech>` sends the RECORDING ITSELF as a WhatsApp voice note from his account, command head cut off (whisper word timestamps snapped to the signal's own pause — `ring/voice.ts`), never the transcript; `draft <person> <text>` (^odd-loon) is `message` that does NOT send — the text lands in that room's hub-owned composer draft (`matrixSync.setDraft`, the `con chat draft` seam; an open draft is appended to, never overwritten) for him to edit and send himself; `al <text>` is the escape hatch straight to the fork; lists and logs are ONE verb (`add`/`log`) over `scratch/lists/`, `dated: true` makes a log; enrichment is a property of the FILE (`server/src/lists/` `ListWatcher`, `con notes enrich`) — the groceries list is a QUEUE that drains into the open Sainsbury's order and never books one; `con ring say` EXECUTES — `--dry` is the schema loop.
- **Execution feedback**: result → `pushServer.broadcast` (`type:'generic'`, id `ring:<recId>`). A dead target is reported ("X is not live"), never silently swallowed. **Failures also land in the Home alerts log** (Yousef's rule) — `RingStore.failures(sinceMs)` (no transcript, or `route.ok === false`) feeds `gatherAlerts` via the dashboard ctx's `ringFailures` as `kind:'error', source:'ring'` rows, 24 h window. **And a failed route files a board card the moment it happens** (Yousef's rule): `fileMissCard` (index.ts) adds `Ring miss: "<transcript>" → <detail>` to the console board in `on_failure.column` from the schema note (default **In Progress**, so the board dispatches a Console-general fork immediately; `Backlog` = triage; `null` = off), with detail lines from `buildMissCard` — the recording id + `con ring show`, then **step 1: interpret the transcript and DO what Yousef asked** (check the sidecar first so a partly-done action isn't doubled), **step 2: fix the cause** (schema note phrasing / target, or `server/src/ring/` / `server/src/lists/`), verify with `con ring say --dry` + `con ring schema --check`. Deduped on the board itself: an OPEN card (any column but Done) whose text starts with `Ring miss: "<same transcript>"` means no new card (`route.card` in the sidecar records `filed|exists|failed`). `kind:'unknown'` (no fallback configured) is a choice, not a miss — no card. Unclaimed text still goes to the AL fork, whose seed asks IT to judge whether a `Ring schema gap` card is warranted — **flagged `LIKELY TRUNCATED` when the capture opened mid-speech** (`ring/capture.ts`, ^shy-orca: the Index has no pre-roll — hub-STT word timestamps hear a clipped opener the ring's STT dropped, or the classifier reads a mid-clause fragment; sidecar `truncated`, push `· cut off?`), in which case the fork asks Yousef for the whole thought instead of acting on or filing the fragment. Latency: rules are µs; hub STT 1–3 s only when the ring sent no transcript, or to re-hear a 10 s head when the rules did not firmly match; the LLM classifier ~10–15 s only on a rule miss (`llm_fallback: false` skips it).
- **Recommended ring config**: Send = both, Trigger = the button combo reserved for Console (double click-hold) so plain notes never hit the fallback agent. Not built: an MCP-sandbox server (the ring's cloud agent calling Console tools — Yousef wants string parsing, so the webhook is the primary path); SPA/APK surfaces for the archive (`con ring list/show/audio` is the current reader).

### Project webhooks (`con webhook`) — any third party → the project's owner
Generic inbound webhooks routed by project slug (^jade-finch, 2026-09-16). Wire: **ANY `https://con.amar.io/hub/hook/<project>[/<subpath>]`** — `/hook/*` is the one exempt path in the auth wall (`isWebhookInboundPath`), because it authenticates ITSELF: a `webhook`-scoped hub token named `webhook:<project>` (`con webhook setup <project>`; `--rotate` revokes the old one; one active token per project), presented as `Authorization: Bearer …` **or `?token=`** (GitHub/Vercel/Stripe-style providers can't set headers). The name binds the token to the slug — a token for one project 401s on another; the token is checked BEFORE the body is read. The hub never interprets payloads: `server/src/webhooks/pipeline.ts` `processInbound` archives the delivery FIRST (`WebhookStore` → `~/.config/console/webhooks/deliveries/<id>.json`, append-only; text bodies verbatim, binary as base64, 1 MB cap → 413), then resolves the owner with **the same resolver the board dispatcher uses for an unassigned card** (`ownerForProject` in index.ts: board frontmatter `default_owner:` via `findProjectBoard`+`boardDefaultOwner`, else `conventionOwnerForProject` = `resolveDefaultOwner` over the project's bound non-fork sessions) and injects a `[WEBHOOK — project <slug>]` envelope (`buildWebhookEnvelope`: source + user-agent, method/path/query minus `token`, headers minus credentials/transport, body pretty-printed JSON clipped at 6 KB, then `con webhook show <id>` / `con webhook list <project>`) via `injectToSession`. **No live owner → 202 + `route.delivered:false` with the reason** (`no owner` / `@key is not live`), never dropped; `con webhook redeliver <id>` replays to the CURRENT owner and appends to `redeliveries[]`. Routes `server/src/routes/webhooks.ts`: inbound `/hook/<slug>`; management (normal wall) `GET /webhooks` (per project: URL, token, owner + live?, counts), `POST /webhooks/<slug>/setup {rotate?}` (409 when a token exists and `rotate` is false; 404 for a slug that is no vault project), `POST /webhooks/<slug>/test {body?, contentType?, headers?}` (full pipeline, no token — `via:'cli'`), `GET /webhooks/<slug>/deliveries[?limit]`, `GET /webhooks/deliveries/<id>`, `POST /webhooks/deliveries/<id>/redeliver`. Ctx wired in index.ts after `configDir` (TDZ), beside the ring. CLI `con webhook {status,setup,list,show,test,redeliver}`. Tests: `server/src/__tests__/webhooks.test.ts` (pure pipeline + the route handler over real HTTP with a stub token store). **Since ^hazy-fawn every delivery also emits `webhook.received` on the event bus, and a matching listener OWNS it (no owner-wake, `handledBy` on the record) — the owner-wake is the fallback for deliveries no rule claimed; see Events + listeners below.** Not built: SPA/APK surface (agent-facing; `con webhook status` is the reader), HMAC signature verification (the owner can verify `x-hub-signature-256` etc. itself — the headers ride in the envelope and the event), per-provider tokens within one project.

### Events + listeners (`con event`, `con listen`) — webhooks over polling
The hub's one in-process pub/sub and the rules agents hang off it (^hazy-fawn, 2026-09-20; design + restart analysis in the vault at `projects/console/research/webhooks-over-polling.md`). **Cron = time, listen = something happened.** Before you `con cron add` a guard that polls a channel the hub already streams (a Matrix room, Gmail, the calendar, a board, inbound webhooks, geofences), register a listener instead — same guard script, seconds instead of minutes, no process spawned every 5 min. `server/src/events/`: `types.ts` (`HubEvent {id, topic, at, source, key?, hops, data, ref?}` — a SUMMARY plus a `ref` command that fetches the full thing, never the payload; `topicMatches` glob `chat.*`), `topics.ts` (the built-in taxonomy + field docs; custom topics register on first emit), `store.ts` (`EventStore`: one JSONL per UTC day in `~/.config/console/events/`, appended synchronously BEFORE dispatch so an event can be logged-but-undispatched but never the reverse; 90-day prune at boot; `alive.json` heartbeat every 30 s → `hub.started` carries `downSince`/`downMs`), `bus.ts` (`EventBus.emit`: validate topic (`^[a-z][a-z0-9]*(\.[a-z0-9_-]+)+$`) → `hops` ≤ 5 → dedup `(topic, key)` within 24 h, the set REBUILT FROM THE LOG on construction because a restart is exactly when Matrix `since` redelivery and IMAP re-reads happen → persist (or the 500-entry ring for `logged:false` topics — `location.fix`) → in-process `subscribe(pattern, fn)` (a throwing subscriber is logged, the rest still run) → WS tails (`/events/tail?topic=`)). Adapters emit through an optional trailing `emit` seam so tests construct them without a bus: **Matrix** (`matrix/sync.ts` — one `chat.message` per conversation event in every non-initial delta, BEFORE the push mute/low-priority filters, muted rooms and own sends included (`isSelf`); the cursorless initial sync is backfill and emits nothing, a resume from a saved `since` after a restart IS the downtime traffic and does), **Gmail** (`mail/sync.ts` `pushAddedMessages` — `mail.received` per thread arrival with `from/fromEmail/subject/snippet/labels`, only on the incremental path), **calendar** (`cal/sync.ts` — `cal.event.created|updated` from the persisted fingerprint diff, only for a calendar that already had a baseline; `cal.event.starting` beside the reminder push), **webhooks** (`webhooks/pipeline.ts` `webhookEvent` — `webhook.received` with provider headers + parsed JSON clipped field-by-field at 8 KB; **listeners take over**: `ctx.matchedListeners(ev)` non-empty → `handledBy` on the record, `route.detail: handled by listener(s) …`, counted as landed, NO owner-wake; empty → the legacy owner-wake runs unchanged, so a project without rules loses nothing), **board** (`board.card.dispatched|moved|edited` from the `BoardWatcher` hooks in index.ts), **sessions** (`agent.session.ended` via `AgentContext.onSessionEnded`), **location** (`location/watcher.ts` — `location.fix` per new fix (live WebSocket frame or history replay, `data.confidence`), `geo.enter|geo.leave` per transition ALONGSIDE the fence's own `wake`/`url`; fences keep their direct path, listeners are the general one), **IMAP IDLE** (`imap/watcher.ts` `ImapIdleWatcher` + `imap/accounts.ts`, ^ripe-loon 2026-09-20 — the agent mailboxes: every `~/.config/<name>-mail/.env` with `MAIL_HOST/USER/PASS` is discovered at boot (al@, ceo@, Mai's Yahoo — the SAME files `~/exec/al-mail.py` reads; credentials never enter the repo), one `imapflow` connection per account held open on INBOX (EXAMINE, read-only), `mail.received` per arrival with `source: imap:<name>`, **`data.account = <name>` (al | ceo | mai), `data.address` = the mailbox**, `id`/`uid`, `messageId`, `from/fromName/fromEmail/to/subject/date`, `snippet` (first 240 chars of the text/plain part, html stripped, from a 32 KB partial FETCH), `hasAttachments`, `unread`, `ref: python3 ~/exec/al-mail.py --account <name> read <uid>`; key `imap:<name>:<uidvalidity>:<uid>`. Cursor `(UIDVALIDITY, lastUid)` per account in `~/.config/console/imap-cursors.json` — its OWN cursor, not `~/.cache/<name>-mail/state.json`, so the `al-mail.py watch` crons keep firing independently during the §7 run-in-parallel week; no cursor or a changed UIDVALIDITY → baseline silently at UIDNEXT-1 (backfill is not news), otherwise `UID SEARCH <last+1>:*` on connect emits exactly the downtime traffic (the `N:*` above-max quirk is filtered). imapflow owns IDLE (auto-IDLE, re-issued every 25 min, NOOP keepalive on socket timeout, NOOP polling where a server lacks IDLE — `mode: idle|poll` in status); the watcher owns reconnect (5 s → 5 min backoff), the cursor and catch-up (an EXISTS burst during a catch-up coalesces into one re-run). Started beside the bus AFTER `listenerEngine.start()` so the post-restart catch-up reaches listeners; `void imapWatcher.stop()` in shutdown. Health: `con event status` → `adapters.imap.<name>` `{connected, mode, cursor, lastError, emitted, reconnects}`. Tests `__tests__/imap-idle.test.ts` incl. §10.8 (F) kill-and-restart over the persisted cursor. Migration: `con listen add --on mail.received --where data.account=al --wake "…"` beside cron `uUmVGIU` (al@), `-IcLuSc` (ceo@), `11s8s9w` (Mai daily digest — `--coalesce 24h` or keep the cron); retire by their owners after a week), **custom** (`POST /events` / `con event emit <topic> --data --key --ref` — a release script emitting `astera.release` replaces a cron tailing its log). `server/src/listeners/`: `types.ts`, `matcher.ts` (`--where <path><op><value>`, ops `= != ~ ^= > < >= <= in`, regex `/…/i` for flags, paths dotted into the event incl. dashed header names; NOT an expression language — anything cleverer is a guard script; `inWindow`/`nextWindowStart` in Europe/London, `22:00-06:00` wraps), `shell.ts` (`bash -c` with the batch JSON `{listener, events}` on stdin + `LISTENER_ID EVENT_TOPIC EVENT_ID EVENT_IDS EVENT_COUNT`, 60 s cap, injectable), `store.ts` (`~/.config/console/listeners.json`, atomic, debounced), `engine.ts` (`ListenerEngine` — **the cost ladder**: topic + where (in-process) → `dropOutside` → batch into `pending` with `dueAt = max(now + coalesce, lastFired + cooldown, window opening)` → timer → ceiling check (`maxPerHour` hit → `pausedAt` + push + `listener.paused`, batch KEPT) → journal `firing` → guard (exit ≠ 0 = `guard-skipped`, a deliberate no-op; timeout/spawn failure = `error`) → action → `fired` + `listener.fired`. Actions: `wake` (envelope `[EVENT — <topics> ×N coalesced] Listener <id> …` + the prompt, `{{data.x}}` templated from the first event, through `agents/wake.ts` `wakeOrQueue` — the SAME path cron now uses: idle → send, mid-turn → `queueMessage`, identical pending prompt not stacked; `--as <agentKey>` targets another live session), `run`, `post` (3 retries, `X-Console-Event-Id`/`X-Console-Listener`), `notify`, `emit` (`hops+1`, `source: listener:<id>`; a listener never matches its own emits), `card`. Defaults: `--wake` coalesces 60 s and is capped at 12/h; everything else 0 s and 60/h. **Lifetimes**: `times`/`--once`/`--times N` (self-removes after N *successful* fires — a guard-skip or dead target does not count; logged `removed <id> … fired N/N — self-removed`, no owner notice) and `expiresAt`/`--expires 2h|<iso>` (self-removes at the deadline whether or not it fired; a 60 s `sweepExpired()` plus a check on every event and in `start()`, pending events dropped with an `expired` log line). **Agent-to-agent waiting goes through events** (Yousef, 20 Sept): the waiter `con listen add --once --expires 6h --on <project>.<noun>.<verb> --wake "…"`, the doer `con event emit <topic> --data … --key …` when done — never a polling cron, never `con agent send`; topics are `<project>.<noun>.<verb>` (`astera.release.landed`, `console.card.hazy-fawn.done`), and any agent whose result someone may be waiting on emits it. **Restart contract** (§10 of the design): `pending` batches persist and flush 1 s after boot (dropped with a `dropped … stale after downtime` outcome when > 24 h old); a `firing` outcome with no successor is re-run on start (at-least-once — actions must be idempotent on the event key); a `wake` into a dead session is a skip → warn at 3, auto-disable at 10, `con listen resume` clears; `run/post/notify/emit/card` need no session and outlive their owner (that IS the software listener). Listeners are keyed by `owner.claudeSessionId` like crons — `reassignCron` in index.ts re-keys BOTH on merge/re-key; a ticket-fork's listeners die with the card, register long-lived rules on the parent. `con listen list` is FLEET-WIDE; `DELETE /listeners/:id` 403s an agent removing another session's rule without `?force=1` and the owner is woken with `[HUB LISTENER REMOVED]`. Routes: `routes/events.ts` (`GET /events?topic&since&until&source&limit`, `GET /events/topics|status`, `GET /events/:id`, `POST /events`, `POST /events/:id/redeliver {listener}`) and `routes/listeners.ts` (`GET/POST /listeners`, `GET/DELETE /listeners/:id`, `POST /listeners/:id/pause|resume|flush|test`); both behind the normal wall. Boot order in index.ts: bus constructed right after `feedsConfigDir` (every adapter takes it), `eventBus.start()` + `listenerEngine.start()` + `eventBus.emitStarted()` run AFTER the session-restore loop so wake targets resolve; shutdown flushes both. Sessions now carry `CONSOLE_CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` in their env — `con listen add` / `con cron add` default `--session` to it. CLI `con event topics|log|tail|show|emit|redeliver|status` (tail is poll-based, the CLI has no ws dep) and `con listen add|list|show|log|test|pause|resume|flush|remove` (`test` = dry run reporting the rung — `no-match / where / window / cooldown / paused / guard / would-fire` — runs the guard, never the action). **`--wake … --fork [--model haiku]`** (^loud-kite, 2026-09-20): the wake goes into a FRESH single-turn fork of the target instead of the target — `ctx.spawnFork` → `forkSessionForWake(ctx, source, 'Listener', id, model)` (`routes/agents.ts`, shared with cron `--fork`: the ticket-fork's fresh mode minus the board — `pinSessionId`, `parentClaudeSessionId = target`, same cwd/project, key `<targetKey>-<listenerId>-fork`, title `Listener <id> (fork)`, `modelOverride` from `--model`, no cache pin), envelope = `buildForkIdentity` (agentKey, csid argv self-check, "closed when your turn ends — route anything durable through a card / emit / notification") + the normal `[EVENT]` envelope + prompt. The target must still be live (it lends cwd/lineage), so skip semantics equal a plain wake. `reapForkAfterTurn` closes the fork (`closeSession`) 2 s after its first `result` — unless `needsAttention` is set (it pinged Yousef → left alive, logged) or 30 min pass with no result (left alive, logged, never killed mid-work). The owner's context is never touched: that is the point — use it for chatty topics whose handling is stateless. `validateAction` rejects `--model` without `--fork`. **SPA**: `src/components/agent/ListenerPill.tsx` (`listen: N (M paused)` chip beside the cron pill in the session status bar) + `ListenerPanel.tsx` (right drawer: per-listener rule/action/fired/last outcome, pause · resume · flush · remove; no create form — rules are registered from the session with `con listen add`), backed by `src/store/listeners.ts` (polled like cron, 30 s); the two drawers share one slot (`sidePanel` state in `AgentSessionView`). Tests: `__tests__/events-bus.test.ts`, `__tests__/listeners.test.ts` (incl. kill-and-restart: pending flush, stale drop, interrupted `firing` re-run; `--fork` spawn/close/ping-keeps-alive), webhook takeover in `webhooks.test.ts`. **Expectations (`con listen expect`, ^deft-hawk, 2026-09-20)** — the "did NOT happen in time" half, so cron = time, listen = event, expect = both. A `Listener` with an `expect` field (`listeners/types.ts` `Expectation`; deadline maths in `listeners/expect.ts`, croner with `timezone: Europe/London`); its `action` is the `--else`, `expect.then` the optional on-satisfaction action, and `matching()` excludes expectations so they never claim a webhook delivery. Two flavours: **absolute** `--by <cron|iso|+dur> [--window 3h]` keeps exactly one `pending` entry (the next tick); at the tick it is satisfied iff `expect.matches` (every `on`+`where` hit, persisted, pruned past the window) has one in `[tick − window, tick]` (default window = since the previous tick), else `miss()`; a one-shot ISO/`+dur` self-removes after its tick is judged. **Relative** `--within 90m [--after <topic> [--where …]]`: each `after` hit pushes `{armedAt, deadlineAt, triggerEventId}` (cap 50; `--hours/--days` gate arming), a matching `on` hit disarms ALL pending (+ `then`, + `expect.satisfied`); **no `--after` = armed once at creation and self-removed on satisfied OR missed** — the replacement for `--once --expires 6h` when you also need to hear that it never came. `miss()` emits `expect.missed` (`deadlineAt, armedAt, reason: deadline, trigger{…}, lateMs, confidence` — geo topics get `stale` when `ctx.lastFixAt()` (the geofence store's last fix) is > 30 min old, so the woken agent can say "phone dead?") and then runs the `--else` through `runBatch(l, [missed, trigger], 'expect')` — same journal (`firing` → `fired`), guard (stdin = the miss + trigger; exit 1 = "no yoga booked, don't nudge"), ceiling (`ceilingHit`, shared with `flushPending`) and `times` countdown as any listener; the envelope header is `[EXPECTATION MISSED — expect <rule>] … deadline <when> passed with no matching event` and `{{data.trigger.<field>}}` templates from the trigger. Timers: ONE per listener (the earliest deadline), re-derived from `pending` — `armExpect`/`evaluateDue`; the 60 s sweep also runs `sweepDeadlines()` so a dead timer chain cannot silently skip a tick (the cron 2026-09-14 lesson). **Restart**: `resumeExpect()` — a deadline < 24 h overdue is judged now (`--else` fires late, envelope says how late), one > 24 h overdue is logged `missed … not acted on` + `expect.missed{reason:'hub down', acted:false}` with no action (the cron one-shot policy); absolute matches are persisted so a tick that passed during downtime is judged from what actually arrived. `con listen test` on an expectation reports `would-arm / would-satisfy / would-count / ignored`; `con listen flush` = `judgeNow()` (pull the nearest deadline to now). CLI grammar is position-aware (`parseExpectArgs` in `cli/src/commands/listen.ts`): `--where` binds to the `--on`/`--after` before it, action options (`--as --body --method --header --data --to --assign`) to the `--else`/`--then` before them — `--else notify "…" --body "…" --then wake "…"`. Tests: `engine: expectations` in `listeners.test.ts` (both flavours, then/guard/envelope, one-shot wait, §10.8 (E) restart <24 h / >24 h, absolute judged from persisted matches, test/flush, validation). Card D (^lean-vole) shipped the OwnTracks live WS + history replay (see the Location stanza). Not built (card G on the console board): provider onboarding (GitHub org webhook + `github-catchup.sh` on `hub.started`, Vercel, Xero HMAC).

### Agent mailboxes (`con agent inbox`) — an agent's own email address, provisioned

Al (al@amar.io) and the CEO (ceo@amar.io) were wired by hand; `con agent inbox create <name> [--for <agentKey> | --project <slug>] [--from-name "…"] [--signature "…\\n…"] [--domain] [--quota MB] [--password] [--quiet]` (^red-bear, 2026-09-20) does the same for any agent in one shot, hub-side (`server/src/agents/inbox.ts` `provisionInbox`, route `routes/agent-inbox.ts` `POST/GET /agents/inbox`, `DELETE /agents/inbox/<name>[?keep=1]`): (1) mailbox `<name>@amar.io` on mxroute through its REST API (`server/src/mxroute/client.ts` — `https://api.mxroute.com`, OpenAPI at `/openapi.yaml`, three headers `X-Server` (the DirectAdmin server hostname, `blizzard.mxrouting.net`) / `X-Username` (DA username) / `X-API-Key` (minted at panel.mxroute.com → Advanced → API Keys); `POST /domains/{domain}/email-accounts {username, password, quota, limit}`; 20 writes/min; a service-management API, never mail itself). Credentials live in **`~/.config/console/mxroute.env`** (`MXROUTE_SERVER`, `MXROUTE_USERNAME`, `MXROUTE_API_KEY`, optional `MXROUTE_DOMAIN`, `MXROUTE_API_URL`), re-read per request — adding the file needs no restart; without it the routes answer 503 `UNCONFIGURED` with the instructions. Password = 28 random alphanumerics (mxroute wants upper+lower+digit; alphanumeric so it survives the single-quoted `.env` and any shell). (2) **`~/.config/<name>-mail/.env`** (dir 0700, file 0600: `MAIL_HOST/USER/PASS/FROM_NAME/SIGNATURE`) — exactly what `~/exec/al-mail.py --account <name>` and the IDLE watcher read, so the existing script is the agent's mail client with zero changes. (3) IMAP login verified with a throwaway imapflow connection, retried 6× at 3 s (a fresh DirectAdmin mailbox can lag) — a persistent failure is a `warning`, not a rollback (the .env stays, the watcher backs off). (4) **Watcher hot-add** — `ImapIdleWatcher.addAccount` (+ `removeAccount`, `has`) start/stop a per-account loop at runtime, so `mail.received` flows without a hub restart. (5) **The education**: `renderInboxSkill` (`agents/inbox-skill.ts`) writes `<cwd>/.claude/skills/<name>-email/SKILL.md` into the agent's session cwd (or `~/sync/brain/root/projects/<slug>/` with `--project`) — commands, inbound handling, and Yousef's standing rules generalised from the al-email skill + the CEO section of amar-systems/CLAUDE.md (never send unprompted; new thread / substance → draft and wait for "send"; cc a human from our side; `--reply-to-id` doesn't quote; signature is CONFIG, never typed; no em dashes; introduce yourself as an AI). Default signature `"<From Name>\n\n<From Name> is an AI agent acting for Yousef Amar."` — override with `--signature`. (6) With `--for` (NOT `--agent` — that is the CLI's global agent-mode flag and gets eaten before the verb sees it), a `mail.received --where data.account=<name>` wake listener is registered on that session (skipped if one exists) and (7) one `[MAILBOX PROVISIONED]` wake (`renderInboxOnboarding`, via `wakeOrQueue`) tells it to read the skill and confirm in one line (`--quiet` skips). `con agent inbox list` = local `*-mail` configs ⋈ mxroute accounts (usage, sent today, suspended) + watched? + listener ids; `con agent inbox remove <name> [--keep-mailbox]` undoes all of it (the mxroute mailbox and its mail go unless kept). Name rule `^[a-z][a-z0-9._-]{0,31}$` — it is the local part AND the config key AND `data.account`. An existing local `.env` is a 409; an existing mxroute mailbox with no local config is a 409 that tells you to adopt it with `--password`. Tests: `__tests__/agent-inbox.test.ts`.

### Location + geofences (`con location`) — OwnTracks fix → server-side fences → agent wakes
The interpreted layer over the `/owntracks/*` proxy (^odd-goat, 2026-09-20). `server/src/location/`: `geofence.ts` (pure — haversine, `evaluate(fences, state, fix)` with hysteresis: enter at `radius`, leave at `radius + max(30 m, 15 %)`; a fix whose `acc > max(2r, 150)` cannot flip a fence, and a transition needs the whole ±acc circle past its line (`resolveInside`: enter when `d + acc ≤ r`, leave when `d − acc > r + margin` — a ±200 m network fix 186 m out flipped home 2 min after an enter on 2026-09-24, ^busy-finch); a fence with no prior state initialises SILENTLY — an event is a transition, never "here is where he already is"), `store.ts` (`GeofenceStore`: fences + per-fence state + last fix in `~/.config/console/geofences.json`, transitions appended to `geofence-events.jsonl`, never pruned; geometry change on upsert drops that fence's state so it re-initialises quietly), `watcher.ts` (`LocationWatcher`: holds the Recorder's live WebSocket `wss://…/recorder/ws/last` open (`recorder.ts` `makeRecorderLiveFeed`, same `auth.json owntracks{}` creds — unconfigured → `live.state:'polling'`, feed retried every 60 s; the protocol is: send `LAST` → one JSON per device then a literal `LAST` sentinel, then every stored fix as it arrives; the snapshot rows name the device only in `topic: owntracks/<user>/<device>`; ws ping every 30 s, no frame/pong for 90 s = dead → reconnect with 1 s→60 s backoff), and **replays the gap on every (re)connect — including hub start**: `fetchHistory` (`/api/0/locations?from=<lastFix.tst>&to=now`, UTC ISO, rows stamped with the query's device/user since the Recorder omits them) is fed through the engine in fix order BEFORE any live frame, so an enter+leave that both happened while the hub was down still fire, flagged `replayed: true` (envelope says `[GEOFENCE REPLAYED — …]`, bus `data.confidence: 'replayed'|'live'|'test'`); fixes apply strictly in arrival order through one promise queue (`settled()` awaits it), a frame with `tst ≤ lastFix.tst` is dropped, a gap > 14 d is not replayed (state re-derives silently); `tick()`/`fetchLast` remain only for `POST /location/refresh` and a first `GET /location` with no fix; `dryReplay(from, to)` runs the engine over history WITHOUT state/dispatch (`POST /location/replay`, `con location replay`) — the way to tune a radius against a known week; `onChange` seam → SyncBus service `'location'` (`fix` / `fences` / `feed` ops + `snapshot` RPC) so the Map tab follows live; prunes `expiresAt` fences per fix; dispatch = wake every `wake` agentKey with a `[GEOFENCE — Yousef ENTERED/LEFT "<name>"]` envelope through the SAME `deliverToAgent` seam as project webhooks, then POST the event JSON (`type:'geofence'`, `delivered` stripped, `fence{}` added) to `url` with `Authorization: Bearer <urlToken>`; outcome per target recorded on the event and logged `[location] enter "home" → @al, https://… ✗ HTTP 500`), `routes/location.ts` (`GET /location` (polls first if no fix yet), `POST /location/refresh`, `GET/POST /location/geofences` (upsert body `{id?, name, lat, lon, radius, wake?: 'al,ceo'|[], url?, urlToken?, private?, on?: enter|leave|both, expiresAt?, note?}`; id slugified from name; 201 create / 200 replace — a replace is WHOLE, omitted fields reset), `DELETE /location/geofences/<id>`, `POST …/<id>/test {event}` (synthetic transition through the real pipeline, state untouched, envelope says TEST), `GET /location/events?limit&fence`, `GET /location/map` (fix + fences with state + live-feed health — what the Map draws), `POST /location/replay {from?,to?,fence?,device?}` (dry run, default last 7 d, ≤92 d)). Wired in index.ts beside the webhook ctx; `locationWatcher.stop()` in shutdown. **Disclosure is hub-side too** (`location/disclose.ts`, `GET /location/for/<slug>`, CLI `con location for <user>`): the policy is the frontmatter of AL's `users/<slug>.md` (`WORKSPACE_DIR`, parsed with `al/users.ts` `parseFrontmatter`) — `trust: owner` → exact; `location: exact|area|city|country|none`; legacy `allow: [location]` → exact (`location:city` inside allow works); nothing → none. `levelFor` picks the level, `disclose` words it from a Nominatim reverse-geocode at that level's zoom (`location/revgeo.ts`, cached per 100 m cell + zoom in `~/.config/console/revgeo-cache.json`) and returns `{level, why, say|null, note}` — `say: null` = refuse. **`private: true` = a privacy zone** (home): inside one, `exact`/`area` collapse to "At home (Reading)" (no pin, no address); backstop with no fence: a reverse-geocode containing a WhatsApp-censor term (`al/whatsapp.ts` `blockedTerms()`) collapses the same way. The point of doing this in software: a non-owner AL fork only ever runs `con location for <user>` and never has the raw fix in context. **ETA + lateness** (`GET /location/eta?to&mode`, `GET /location/late-check?threshold&window&mode`; CLI `con location eta`, `con location late-check [--guard]`): `location/late.ts` `lateCheck` takes every upcoming event HE IS GOING TO with a physical `location` (`listAllEvents(…, { attending: true })` from routes/calendar.ts — his ticked calendars + invites he has not declined, see Calendar → `cal/visibility.ts`; `VIRTUAL_RE` skips home/zoom/meet/teams/URLs; declined/cancelled/all-day/past skipped), geocodes it via the gmaps client biased to the current fix, gets a traffic-aware ETA (`computeRoutes` with `departureTime` now), and reports events he would reach more than `threshold` min after the start — one report per event, re-report only when lateness grew by another threshold (state `~/.config/console/location-late-alerts.json`), already within 250 m = arrived (state cleared), fix older than 30 min = `skipped:'stale fix'`; state for ids no longer listed is pruned each run. **Travel blocks are judged on LEAVING, not arriving** (^rare-vole, 2026-09-25 — "Drive to Wilding Camp (Reading → Halstead)" 08:00–11:00 fired "13 min late" at 06:25 because its `location` is the destination and its start the departure time): a title matching `TRAVEL_RE` (`^(drive|train|flight|fly|cycle|walk|bus|…)\b.*\bto\b`, or an `A → B` / `A -> B` arrow) is travel without any geocode; so is a solo event (no attendees besides him) whose duration is within `TRAVEL_DURATION_TOL` (20 %) of the route ETA — that classification sticks in state (`travel: true`). For a travel block the fix at every pre-start tick is remembered as `origin`; once the start has passed by more than the threshold with him still within 250 m of it (and not at the destination — he may have driven up the night before) the report is `kind:'not-left'` (`NOT LEFT: "…" was due to leave 08:00, 13 min ago; still where he was at departure time, heading for <dest>` + the ETA/`block ends` line), re-alerting per threshold like lateness; moving > 250 m from the origin marks `departed` and ends the check for that event. Hub-down before the start = the first post-start fix becomes the origin, judged from the next tick. `LateReport.kind` is `'late' | 'not-left'`; `venue`/`etaMin`/`distanceKm`/`arriveIso` are optional (a `not-left` whose destination failed to geocode). CLI `formatLate` in `cli/src/commands/location.ts` mirrors `formatLateReport` — keep both in sync. `--guard` gives hub-cron guard semantics (report + exit 0 only when late; else silent exit 1) — AL main's cron `TtoM7_0` runs it every 5 min (its prompt says "late"; a NOT LEFT report is the same shape, the al `location` skill explains it). **`expiresAt` = one-shot fences** (a meeting venue, `--expires +4h`) — the "am I there yet" primitive. CLI `con location [now|refresh|history|geofence list|add|remove|events|replay|test|for|eta|late-check]` (`now` prints `Feed: live WebSocket, up 2.1 h, last frame 40 s ago` / `RECONNECTING (closed 1006)` / `no live feed — OwnTracks not configured`, and the last replay; `events` marks `REPLAYED` rows) (`cli/src/commands/location.ts`; `add --at "<address>"` geocodes through `/gmaps/search` biased to the current fix). **Map tab** (`src/map/location-subscribe.ts`, wired in GatedBoot; store `fences`/`locationFeed` in `src/store/map.ts`): built-in layer `fences` — each fence a geodesic 64-gon (`circleRing`; MapLibre circles are pixel-sized) as `geo-fences-fill`/`-line` tinted green inside, neutral outside, amber outline for `private`, dashed while state is unknown, name label just above the north edge (`geo-fence-labels`, minzoom 10), click → dark popup (state + since, radius, wake, private, note, expiry); the live `fix` op moves the `ot-current` pin and extends the drawn track when its range reaches now, `fences` re-tints on transitions, `feed` drives the dot on the centre-on-me button (green connected / amber pulsing reconnecting / grey none). Not built: phone-side OwnTracks regions (the Recorder stores `_type:transition` but `/last` never returns them — server-side fences are the source of truth, and they work for venues AL invents on the fly). The raw fix is OWNER-GRADE: anything that relays it to a third party goes through AL's policy, never straight from this route. Tests `server/src/__tests__/location.test.ts` (pure + store + watcher with stubbed IO + routes over real HTTP).

### Push-to-talk (PTT) — system-wide hold-to-talk → STT → mic owner
A hardware/hotkey hold-to-talk that streams mic → hub `/stt` (OpenAI realtime, `{type:'audio',data:b64}` → `{interim|final,text}`) and on release routes the transcript to the **mic owner** session. Owner model in `server/src/mic.ts` (`MicState`: `ownerSessionId` persisted to `mic-owner.json`, transient `hot`); routes in `server/src/routes/mic.ts` (`/mic/status|owner|hot|say|compose`); SyncBus `mic` service in `index.ts` broadcasts `state` (owner/hot) + `compose` (transient, NOT in the subscribe snapshot — and the SyncBus never replays missed events). Default owner = explicit-if-live-else-Al. CLI `con mic status|give|pass|release|say|ptt`. SPA mirror in `src/store/mic.ts` + the `MicButton` in Spaces.
- **Two delivery modes**: `/mic/say` = inject + **auto-send** to the owner; `/mic/compose` = drop into the owner's composer **unsent** for review (`AgentPromptInput` reacts to `composeSeq`). A composed draft **lingers** in the textarea until sent/cleared — by design (it's a review draft), so an earlier foreground compose can still be sitting there later; it is NOT a duplicate of a backgrounded `say`.
- **Desktop** (Sway) PTT is REMOVED (^lean-duck) — the bindings, `~/exec/con-ptt*`, the `foot` popup and the waybar indicator are gone; never re-add them. Hub `/stt` + `/mic/*` stay (SPA, APK, CLI use them).
- **Phone** (APK v26+): the rugged phone's Custom-key **"Open Zello"** Easy-shuttle mode is the ONLY one that emits the firmware-level `com.zello.ptt.down/.up` broadcasts (Zello PTT API action strings — independent of any app). `PushService.pttReceiver` records on `.down` (AudioRecord VOICE_RECOGNITION 24kHz mono PCM16 → `/stt`), and on `.up` routes to `/mic/compose` if `MainActivity.foreground` else `/mic/say`. Needs FGS type `microphone` + RECORD_AUDIO. The native service requires the **apk bearer** (pair the APK) — see [Known Issues] + `memory/project_android_js_bridge_bind.md`.
- **Two STT models, deliberately NOT one constant** (`server/src/stt.ts`). The realtime endpoint (`wss://…/v1/realtime?intent=transcription`) and the batch endpoint (`POST /v1/audio/transcriptions`) reject each other's model ids, so they need separate constants — `STT_MODEL = 'gpt-realtime-whisper'` (streaming: PTT, dictation, glasses mic) and `STT_BATCH_MODEL = 'gpt-transcribe'` (whole-file: `/stt` upload endpoint + Al's WhatsApp voice notes). A test in `__tests__/stt.test.ts` pins `STT_BATCH_MODEL !== STT_MODEL` so a "consolidate these" refactor fails loudly. **`gpt-live-transcribe` is REJECTED** (A/B-tested: measurably worse transcripts; and `server_vad` breaks the explicit `input_audio_buffer.commit`) — don't retry it. **When probing the realtime endpoint, stream at true realtime pace (4800-byte chunks / 100 ms at 24 kHz mono PCM16)** — faster starves the streaming decoder and garbles every model, invalidating the comparison.
- **A turn only ends because the CLIENT says so — `{type:'done'}` on mic release** (`server/src/index.ts` `/stt` handler; `STT_FLUSH_IDLE_MS` / `STT_DONE_TIMEOUT_MS` in `stt.ts`). `gpt-realtime-whisper` **rejects `turn_detection`**, so nothing closes a turn on its own: it streams deltas lazily and the tail sits unemitted for 5–8 s. Two commit triggers, and **neither is a wall-clock timer** — that distinction is load-bearing, since periodic commits chop mid-sentence and were vetoed for wrecking accuracy (`memory/feedback_stt_no_periodic_commits.md`): (1) **idle** — every `append` resets a `STT_FLUSH_IDLE_MS`=600 ms timer, so a commit only lands in a genuine gap; (2) **`{type:'done'}`** — commits immediately, then the relay holds the socket open until the `final` lands and **closes it itself** (capped by `STT_DONE_TIMEOUT_MS`=4 s so a stuck turn can't strand the mic UI). Measured commit→`final` ≈ 600–700 ms.
  - **The START of the turn is guarded too: no audio is dropped during either handshake.** Two rules: (1) the hub relay silently discarded frames arriving while the OpenAI WS was still CONNECTING (~0.5–1.5 s TLS+WS) — it buffers them (`pushCapped` in `stt.ts`, oldest-evicted at `STT_PREBUFFER_MAX_BYTES` ≈ 30 s) and flushes on open, with a `done`-during-handshake committing right after the flush; (2) `useDictation` serialized getUserMedia → WS open → only then built the capture graph, so the browser wasn't even recording until the client WS opened — capture now starts immediately with its own pre-open frame queue, and the WS + getUserMedia open concurrently. The Kotlin paths need nothing (OkHttp queues `send()` before the connection opens). Also: a browser whose SpeechRecognition fails with `network`/`service-not-allowed` (Brave) burns seconds discovering it per dictation — `console:dictation:hubOnly` localStorage remembers the failure and goes straight to hub STT forever after.
  - **Three independent implementations, all of which must send `done` and wait** — a fix to one is a fix to none of the others: `src/hooks/useDictation.ts` (SPA + APK WebView), `android/.../core/Dictation.kt` (native composer mic), `android/.../PushService.kt` `pttUp()` (hardware PTT key).
  - **Android dictation is a process singleton with an OWNER**: `Dictation.start(owner?)` tags `State.owner`, and `committed` emits `Commit(text, owner)`. The on-screen `Composer` collects `committed` unconditionally (that's how the hardware PTT key lands text), so any OTHER field that dictates — `ui/components/DictatedTextField` (approval-card answer + plan-comment) — must start with its own owner id; the Composer masks owned state (`owner != null` → plain Mic, no transcript, commit skipped). Without the tag a dictated answer lands in BOTH the field and the composer draft. A new dictating surface = a `DictatedTextField`, never a raw `Dictation.start()`.
  - **The `final` carries the WHOLE turn, so it supersedes the accumulated deltas** — appending it duplicates the utterance. The Kotlin paths replace rather than append; the SPA instead *suppresses* the final when deltas already covered it (`sawDeltaRef`), and that flag must **not** be reset in the teardown path — the final arrives *after* `stop()`.
- **`android/zello-shim/`** — a tiny invisible stub app with applicationId **`com.loudtalks`** (Zello's REAL package — `com.zello` is only the broadcast prefix). "Open Zello" deep-links to the Play Store when `com.loudtalks` is absent; installing this no-op (a translucent activity that finishes instantly) makes the firmware's launch resolve to nothing visible while the PTT broadcasts keep firing. Build `./gradlew :zello-shim:assembleDebug`, served at `/public/apk/loudtalks-shim.apk`.

## Keybindings
- **Global**: `\` = command bar (jump to anything — see below; also fires from an EMPTY input/textarea, because chat/agent composers re-focus themselves after a send — a field with text keeps its backslash, ^teal-eel), `Ctrl+Tab`/`Ctrl+Shift+Tab` = switch pane, `Tab` = next pane, `?` = help, `Shift+T` = dark mode
- **Mail/Chat**: `j/k` = navigate, `e` = archive/read, `b` = snooze, `r/R/f` = reply/all/forward, `c` = compose, `/` = search, `u` = undo
- **Inbox**: `j/k`, `e`, `b`, `p` = promote/demote source, `r/R/f` on mail, `o` = open agent in Spaces, `/` = command bar (the pane spans every source, so its search IS the bar)
- **Calendar**: `h/l` = prev/next week, `t` = today, `w/d` = week/day view, `c` = create
- **Spaces (agents)**: `y/n/a` = approve/deny/allow-all, `Enter` = focus prompt / send, `Ctrl+Enter` = queue until the turn ends, `Shift+Enter` = newline, `Esc` = interrupt, `/` = command bar (same as `\`)
- **Notes**: vim mode in editor, `Ctrl+P` = find file, `Ctrl+S` = save, `:w/:q/:wq` ex commands
- **Map**: `j/k` = adjacent cache, `f` = fetch caches in view, `g` = my location, `Esc` = deselect

### Command bar (`\` anywhere, `/` on Spaces, Search glyph in the header)
`src/components/CommandBar.tsx` (mounted in `App.tsx` on `useUiStore.commandBarOpen`) is the ONE jump-to-anything surface — it absorbed the Spaces-only `/` switcher (deleted 2026-09-11; `SpacesQuickSwitcher.tsx` + `switcherOpen` are gone). Sources: every pane + Money sub-tab, a few actions (compose / new event / add bookmark / add feed / dark mode / shortcuts), spaces, live agent sessions, the whole vault, chat rooms (`db.chatRooms`), mail threads (recent 200 preloaded + a debounced whole-table `db.threads.filter` for queries ≥ 2 chars), feed subscriptions, bookmarks, and still-running/upcoming calendar events (60 days, `endTime > now`). Ranking is pure + unit-tested in `src/commandbar/rank.ts`: empty query = launcher (Recent, per-kind capped so mail can't flood it → Upcoming → Go to); a query = one flat fuzzy list, structure (pane/action/space) before content on an equal score, then most recent. Every pick goes through the existing deep-link primitives — `focusSessionInSpaces`, `openVaultFile` (lands in Spaces Docs), `chat.selectRoom`, `inbox.selectThread`, `cal.navigateToDate` + `selectEvent` — never a bespoke path. Opening primes the lazy stores (`reconnectVault` / `fetchBookmarks` / `fetchFeeds`) so it is complete from a cold boot on any pane. `\` is bare-key (not while typing — Esc blurs first, the app's vim model); no modifier chord yet because `Ctrl+K` is the editor's and Yousef names his own chords (`memory/feedback_keyboard_layout.md`).

## Commands
- `pm2 start "npm run dev" --name console-dev` — dev server (Vite, plain HTTP; Caddy in front does TLS)
- `cd server && pm2 start "npm run dev" --name console-server` — hub server (binds `0.0.0.0:9877`, HTTPS when certs available). `npm run dev` no longer wraps `tsx watch` — use `npm run dev:watch` if you want auto-restart on source changes.
- `con hub restart` — **canonical way** to restart the hub after `server/src/*` changes. Wraps `pm2 restart console-server`, waits for `/health` to come back, and nudges any agent sessions that were mid-turn with a "hub was restarted, continue" prompt (via `wasRunning` in the session manifest). Idle sessions resume silently. **Protocol (Yousef, 2026-09-08): ask him whether it's OK to restart, then run `con hub restart` YOURSELF once he says yes** — don't wait for him to do it. **If YOU are a hub-managed session, the call kills your own process mid-tool-call and the SDK reports it as "The user doesn't want to proceed with this tool use … [Request interrupted by user]" — that is the restart SUCCEEDING, not a veto.** Verify with `pm2 jlist` uptime after the resume nudge; never re-issue for the same deploy.
- `pm2 save` after any add/delete of a pm2 process. One-time `pm2 startup` install (sudo) sets up `pm2-amar.service` so both processes survive reboot — see `memory/reference_pm2_systemd_boot.md`.
- `npm test` / `cd server && npm test` — tests
- `npx tsc --noEmit` — type check SPA
- `cd server && npx tsc --noEmit` — type check server
- `cd cli && npx tsc --noEmit` — type check CLI

### Android APK
Requires Android SDK at `$ANDROID_HOME` (default `~/app/Android/Sdk`), minSdk 26, targetSdk 35. Applicationid `io.amar.console`.
- **Parity is owned by the Mobile agent, not by SPA forks.** The "New mobile app" session (agentKey `new-mobile-app`, cwd `android/`) runs a nightly parity sweep — hub cron `gBQ18EE`, 04:00 every night (weekly until 2026-09-19), guarded by `~/exec/mobile-parity-guard.sh` so a quiet week costs nothing — that diffs the SPA against the app, files gaps as `android/BACKLOG.md` Open entries with their phone equivalents, implements them via board cards, and cuts the release. A SPA fork shipping a phone-relevant feature still files the BACKLOG Open entry (that's the sweep's input); it does not port it. Procedure + charter: `android/CLAUDE.md`.
- **Always build release, never debug.** Single-user software — the in-app updater (`/apk/latest.json`) only serves the release channel, debug builds can't auto-update, and there's no QA/staging lane that benefits from a separate debug variant.
- **Batch APK releases via `android/BACKLOG.md`.** This rule is about the Android APK ONLY — rebuilding/reinstalling the app for every little thing is a hassle (Yousef's rule). Hub/SPA changes deploy instantly (`con hub restart` / Vite HMR) and are NOT batched — ship those immediately as always. For app-side items: add to the backlog's "Open" section, implement + commit promptly (moving to "Built, awaiting release"), but only cut a versionCode bump + `build-release.sh` when there's significant accumulated meat or he asks. The file also records what shipped in which APK version — check it before claiming something is deployed vs merely committed.
- `android/scripts/generate-keystore.sh` — one-time, create `~/.config/console/console-release.jks` (back it up!)
- `android/scripts/build-release.sh` — build signed release, copy to `~/.config/console/apk/`, write `latest.json` (reads `~/.config/console/apk-release.env` for passwords). Phone picks up the update on next launch via the in-app banner.
- `android/scripts/build-debug.sh` exists but should not be used.
- The APK loads `https://con.amar.io/` — Caddy serves SPA + hub + public surface from one origin; HMR works through Caddy's reverse_proxy to Vite.
- Web side: `isNative()` (from `src/platform.ts`) detects `window.__isConsoleAPK`. Used by `src/gmail/auth.ts` (passes `?callback=app` so hub redirects OAuth to `console://auth/done`) and `src/main.tsx` (requests `navigator.storage.persist()`).
- Remote debugging: `chrome://inspect` on the laptop while phone is USB-connected (debug builds only; `setWebContentsDebuggingEnabled(BuildConfig.DEBUG)`). The embedded debug agent also works — APK events show in `/debug/log` with UA containing `ConsoleAPK/...`.
- Push notifications: `PushService` opens a persistent WebSocket to `/push` and posts system notifications even when the WebView is backgrounded. Server sources call `pushServer.broadcast({ type, title, body, pane, id, ...chatFields })`. Wired today: Monzo webhook (`money` channel), agent `AskUserQuestion` / `ExitPlanMode` (`agent` channel), Gmail new mail (`mail/sync.ts` — fires on `messagesAdded` deltas), Matrix room messages (`matrix/sync.ts` — see below), Calendar per-event reminders (`cal/sync.ts` — 30s ticker scans upcoming events, fires on `reminders.overrides` minutes-before-start with a 60s slack window, dedupes via persisted `fired` map), property listing alerts (`property/sync.ts`, see below), hub health alerts (`server/src/health.ts` — sync-loop failure escalation for mail/cal/matrix/flights + cron skip/disable pushes; runbook in `docs/hub-health.md`). CLI/webhooks can emit via `POST /push/send`. Check connected clients: `curl -sk https://localhost:9877/push/status`.
  - **`PushMessage.url` bypasses pane navigation entirely.** `navigateDeepLink` (MainActivity.kt) only maps `itemId` to a detail route for Chat/Mail/Agents/Feeds/Notes — any pane without an item-level route means `console://pane/$pane` with no query params, so a tap just opens the bare pane with zero indication of *which* item the notification was about. For a notification about one specific external thing with an existing external page (a property listing, a flight, anything with its own URL) and no in-app detail route, set `url` on the `PushMessage` instead of relying on `pane`+`id` — `handleGenericPush` (PushService.kt) opens it directly via `ACTION_VIEW`, skipping `MainActivity`/`navigateDeepLink` altogether. Building an actual in-app detail route (map camera-jump to a specific pin, etc.) is the more complete fix but a much bigger lift; `url` is the cheap universal fallback whenever the thing being notified about already has its own page.
  - Chat push specifics (`server/src/matrix/sync.ts`):
    - **Mute filtering**: skip rooms where the delta's `unread_notifications.notification_count === 0` (server-side push rules muted it) OR where the room is tagged `m.lowpriority` / `m.archive` with no highlight. Mirrors the browser's unread logic.
    - **Enrichment**: `PushMessage` carries `roomId`, `roomName`, `senderName`, `senderId`, `senderAvatarMxc`, `roomAvatarMxc`, `isDirect`, `timestamp`. **`roomName`/`isDirect`/`roomAvatarMxc` are read from the canonical, persisted `ChatRoomsStore` snapshot (`chat-rooms.json`), NOT the local in-memory `roomState` cache.** Why: the local cache only learns a room's name from `m.room.name` state events, which Matrix replays on initial sync (or when the name changes). After a restart an incremental sync carries no state events, so the local cache is empty and a group push would be attributed to the sender. The `ChatRoomsStore` resolves names via heroes/bridge-member fallbacks (`computeRoomState`/`getRoomName` preserve the prior resolved name across nameless deltas) and survives restarts on disk, so group titles are always correct. The local `roomState` cache (name, avatar mxc, members with displayname+avatar) + `directRooms` set (from global `m.direct` account_data) are still maintained per sync tick + from the `snapshot` RPC, and still source per-event `senderName`/`senderAvatarMxc` from `members`; they're just the fallback for name/isDirect/avatar now.
  - APK rendering (`PushService.kt`):
    - Chat notifications use `NotificationCompat.MessagingStyle` with `Person` + circular avatar (fetched once via hub's `/matrix/media/thumbnail/...` proxy, cached in-memory). History keeps the last 8 messages per room.
    - Grouping: `setGroup(CHAT_GROUP_KEY)` + a single summary notification (id 100). Per-room notifId is `roomId.hashCode()` so follow-up messages update the same card.
    - Vibration debounce: max one vibration per room per 60s — subsequent messages build with `setSilent(true)` + `setOnlyAlertOnce(true)`.
    - Deep link: `console://pane/chat?roomId=<id>`. `MainActivity.handleDeepLink` parses the query param and dispatches `console:navigate` with `{ pane, itemId }`; `src/notifications.ts` listens for that event and calls `handleNotificationClick` (same path as browser notifications), so tapping a chat notification lands in the right room.
- APK update check: runs once in `onCreate` (cold start) comparing `/apk/latest.json` versionCode against `BuildConfig.VERSION_CODE`. The refresh button in `src/components/Layout.tsx` also triggers a re-check by calling `window.ConsoleNative.checkForUpdate()` — bridge exposed via `addJavascriptInterface(ConsoleBridge(), "ConsoleNative")` in `MainActivity.buildWebView`. No-op in the browser. `updateBanner` tracked so repeated calls don't stack banners.

## Setup
1. Google Cloud project with Gmail + People + Calendar APIs enabled
2. OAuth 2.0 Web Application credentials
3. Add redirect URIs:
   - `https://con.amar.io/hub/auth/google/callback` — the production path (browsers + WebView via the OTT cookie-jar handoff).
   - `http://localhost:9877/auth/google/callback` — direct loopback, kept for back-compat with curl flows.
4. `npm install && npm run dev`
5. Hub: `cd server && npm install && npm run dev`
6. Set Google credentials: `curl -X POST localhost:9877/auth/google/credentials -d '{"clientId":"...","clientSecret":"..."}'`
7. Sign in via the app UI (or hit `https://con.amar.io/hub/auth/google/start?return=…` directly).
8. **Auth enforcement**: `CONSOLE_AUTH_ENABLED=1` in the pm2 env turns on the auth wall. **Break-glass**: `CONSOLE_AUTH_DISABLED=1` forces allow even with `ENABLED=1`. Both vars live in pm2's environment — `pm2 restart console-server --update-env` after editing.

## Hub Routes
External clients reach all of these under `${origin}/hub/<path>` via Caddy (the prefix is stripped before the upstream sees it). Local-host clients (CLI, Al) hit `https://127.0.0.1:9877/<path>` directly with a bearer. The `/public/*` and `/auth/google/*` paths are the only ones the auth middleware lets through unauthenticated.
- `/auth/*` — Google + Monzo + Matrix OAuth flows.
  - `/auth/session` (GET) + `/auth/session/logout` (POST) — current hub session (cookie-derived).
  - `/auth/hub/sessions` (GET) — list active browser sessions (id-prefixes only).
  - `/auth/hub/tokens` (GET/POST), `/auth/hub/tokens/:id` (DELETE) — name + scope-keyed bearer tokens for non-browser clients. POST returns the plaintext once.
  - `/auth/hub/allowed-emails` (GET/POST), `/auth/hub/allowed-emails/:email` (DELETE) — Google allow-list. Default: `['yousefamar@gmail.com']`.
- `/mail/*`, `/cal/*`, `/matrix/*`, `/money/*`, `/bookmarks/*`, `/feeds/*`, `/notes/*` — service proxies / CRUD. `POST /notes/open` is the odd one out: a UI remote-control relay, 409 when no SPA client is subscribed (see Notes → Remote-open).
- `/location`, `/location/{refresh,geofences,geofences/<id>,geofences/<id>/test,events,map,replay,for/<user>,eta,late-check}` — interpreted location layer: geofences, per-user disclosure, ETA, lateness (see the Location stanza). - `/owntracks/*` — proxy to the self-hosted OwnTracks Recorder (`maps.amar.io`, basic auth injected), plus `GET /owntracks/status` (configured-ness, never the password) and `POST /owntracks/credentials` (`{url?,username?,password?}`, merged). `/geocaching/*` — gc.com scraper (status/credentials/fetch-area/caches/cache). (Map basemap is on-demand OpenFreeMap vector tiles loaded directly by the client — no hub route.)
- `/debug/*` — debug agent log, eval, state, screenshot, toggle. `/debug/eval` is loopback-only (Phase 9+).
- `/push` (WebSocket), `POST /push/send`, `GET /push/status` — push notification channel consumed by the APK's PushService. Carries glasses WS RPC framing (see Glasses section). PushService attaches `Authorization: Bearer <apk-scoped-token>`.
- `/glasses/*` — low-level dumb pipe proxied to the APK's GlassesService over the `/push` WS.
- `/dashboard/snapshot`, `/dashboard/alerts`, `/dashboard/servers`, `/dashboard/costs` — Home pane data sources.
- `/dashboard/canvas/{islands,tabs}` — CRUD + per-slug `POST/GET/DELETE /publish` for the public surface.
- `/canvas/*` — static file serve from `~/.config/console/canvas/` (the AI agent canvas dir). `DELETE /canvas` wipes + re-seeds the placeholder. fs.watch broadcasts `dashboard.canvas_changed` on the SyncBus.
- `/public/*` — unauthenticated surface. Three things live here:
  - `/public/<slug>/` — published tab/island standalone page (short form, tab-first-then-island; explicit `/public/canvas/<tab|island>/<slug>/` disambiguates a dual-published slug; NO token — publish = the slug URL is public); `/<slug>/<asset>` serves tab assets (`path.resolve` guard). Published slugs persist at `~/.config/console/canvas-public.json` (sibling of `canvas/`, survives `con dashboard canvas reset`).
  - `/public/cron.ics?token=…` — Google Calendar subscription; the cron token in the query is its own auth. Legacy `/cron.ics` is Caddy-rewritten to this.
  - `/public/apk/latest.json` + `/public/apk/console-<n>.apk` — APK update channel. Legacy `/apk/*` is Caddy-rewritten to this (so pre-v20 stuck installs can still pull v20+).

## AL (absorbed into Console hub)

**His name is AL — all caps, everywhere the hub names him (Yousef's rule).** Session name `AL` (`AL_NAME` in `server/src/al/identity.ts`), forks `AL ↔ <person>`, every UI string. The agentKey slug stays lowercase `al` (board `@al`, mic owner `'al'`, ring `fallbackAgent`). `isAlName()` also accepts the legacy `Al` so a pre-rename manifest still resolves; `ensureAlSession` normalises a resumed/adopted session's name on boot, so the restart that deploys this IS the rename. CLI name matching is case-insensitive (`con agent reload al|Al|AL` all work).

Al's WhatsApp + voice + persona runtime lives in-process at `server/src/al/` and is owned by Console hub. Al himself is a permanent Console-managed Claude Code session (no more `pi-agent-core` loop). Persona (`AL.md`, `mistakes.md`, workflows summary) is loaded as the system prompt **only on a fresh spawn** (`--append-system-prompt`, applied just once — never re-appended on resume or model-respawn; see `session.ts:195`). To re-derive the persona after editing `AL.md`/`persona.ts`/etc., run **`con agent reload AL`** — no hub restart needed. It maps to the `reload_al` client message → `reloadAlSession()` (`al/al-session.ts`): kills the current Al, clears `al-session.json`, re-runs `ensureAlSession` → fresh spawn with a new `claudeSessionId` and freshly-derived persona. Works whether Al is up or already down. (A plain hub restart only *resumes* Al with his old baked-in prompt; and editing `server/src/al/*` source still needs `con hub restart` to deploy — but the persona itself then applies via `con agent reload AL`.) For non-Al sessions, **`con agent reload <id|name>`** → `reload_session` → `Session.reload()` respawns the subprocess via `--resume` (history preserved) — the lever to recover a stuck/dead session without bouncing the hub. No fs.watch / no live reload. (There is no `con agent restart`; reload is the verb.)

### Layout
- `server/src/al/identity.ts` — `AL_HOME`, `WORKSPACE_DIR`, `AUTH_WHATSAPP_DIR` (`~/.config/console/auth_whatsapp/`), `AL_SESSION_FILE` (`~/.config/console/al-session.json`). (No `OWNER_PHONE`/recipient guard — removed as dead code; `/whatsapp/send` is bearer-gated and Al's job is to message contacts, so the old non-owner restriction doesn't apply.)
- `server/src/al/persona.ts` — `buildAlSystemPrompt()`: verbatim `AL.md`, then mistakes log under `# Past mistakes`, then auto-built workflow one-liners.
- `server/src/al/users.ts` — frontmatter resolver + `normalize()` that strips `@s.whatsapp.net|@lid|@c.us|@g.us`. Yousef's `users/yousef.md` carries both his SIM (`447XXXXXXXXX`) and iPad lid identifier under `whatsapp:`; without that his iPad-sent messages get treated as non-owner (footgun #1). **Resolving `@lid` (linked-device) identifiers**: WhatsApp Web/Desktop/iPad messages arrive under a 15-digit `@lid` JID, not the phone number, so auto-discovery creates *dup* contact files (some historically mis-flagged "IMPERSONATOR"). **The lid↔phone map is the hub's own since ^calm-ram (2026-09-30): `server/src/al/wa-identity.ts`** — seeded at boot from Baileys' store (`~/.config/console/auth_whatsapp/lid-mapping-<pn>.json` → lid, `lid-mapping-<lid>_reverse.json` → pn; ~480 pairs) AND wa-voice's SQLite (`~/.config/console/wa-voice/whatsapp.db` `lid_pn_mapping`, read-only via `node:sqlite`; history sync fills it), fed live by Baileys' `lid-mapping.update`, persisted to `~/.config/console/wa-identity.json` (Baileys wipes its store on logout; this survives). `resolveUsername/resolveUserFile/resolveAllow` fall through to the id's alternate (`alternateFor`: in-memory map, then the two stores for exactly that id), so a note listing only a lid resolves a call/message from the phone (Rebaz rang from `447…@s.whatsapp.net`, note had the lid, hub said user=None); `buildLookupMap` registers each listed id's alternate up front so `identifiersFor()` spans both threads and `resolveCallTarget(<slug>)` finds the phone for a lid-only note. **A hit via the alternate is written back into the note's `whatsapp:` list** (`withWhatsappIdentifier`, textual — list append / scalar promoted / key created; atomic tmp+rename) so the pairing is visible and survives without the map; two notes claiming one pair are logged as `duplicate notes`, never merged. `resolveUserLive()` (async: `refreshUsers()` + `discoverAlternate()` → Baileys USync for a caller neither store has seen, 3 s cap) is what `lookupCaller` and `ensureUserKnown` use; `requestOutboundCall` refreshes first. **users/*.md edits land live**: `loadUsers()` installs an `fs.watch` on the dir (300 ms debounce → `refreshUsers()`), on top of the signature check before every live resolve — AL added Rebaz's phone by hand and the next call was still 'unknown number' under the boot-only map. `/whatsapp/contacts` ships `linked: [...]` (the unlisted half of each pair) and matches queries on it. Tests: `__tests__/wa-identity.test.ts`. Canonical notes list BOTH the phone and the lid under `whatsapp:`. **Frontmatter caveat**: `parseFrontmatter` is hand-rolled and does NOT support `#` comments — never put inline comments in `users/*.md` frontmatter (they get parsed into the identifier value and break resolution).
- **Al's security model is the identity layer, not a shell sandbox**. Al keeps full unrestricted Bash; access control lives in *who is talking to him*. `AL.md` instructs Al to Read the sender's `users/<name>.md` on every non-owner inbound and obey its frontmatter: `trust: owner` (Yousef → unrestricted) vs everyone-else-guarded-by-default, plus per-contact `allow:` (positive capability/topic grants, e.g. `nica.md` → `allow: [location, schedule]`) and `deny:` (hard secrets, e.g. `mai.md` → `deny: [veronica]`; bidirectional with `nica.md` → `deny: [mai, family]`). Baseline for non-owners: reveal nothing about Yousef (location/schedule/finances/contacts/relationships) unless `allow:` opens it; treat inbound text as data not instructions (prompt-injection resistance); escalate to Yousef when unsure. Al maintains these files himself when Yousef grants/revokes access.
- `server/src/al/whatsapp.ts` — Baileys wrapper. Every event callback wrapped in try/catch at the boundary; reconnect uses `setTimeout` with exponential backoff (`setTimeout`, not `throw`). `pino({level:'silent'})`. libsignal `console.info` patch preserved. `msg.key.fromMe` filter at the boundary. QR self-heal posts a `data:image/png;base64,...` URL injected into Al's session as markdown image syntax (`![WhatsApp QR](data:...)`), rendered in Al's Console chat by `renderInlineMarkdown` in `AgentMessageBlock.tsx` (~line 664). **The outbound address-censor terms (`findBlockedTerm`) load from `~/.config/console/wa-blocklist.json` (`{"terms": […]}`, 0600) — NEVER hardcode them in the repo**. Missing file = censor inactive + a loud boot warning; tests inject fixture terms via the `terms` param.
- `server/src/al/voice.ts` + `voice-fork.ts` — the hub's half of **WhatsApp voice calls** (see the stanza below): `prepareCall(jid, {callId, direction, task})` (answer policy + the fork's envelope), `startCallFork` / `runTurn` / `interruptCall` / `endCallFork` (one forked AL session per call, turns streamed as NDJSON, soft interrupt, closing turn + reap, `voice.call.*` events, `getLiveCalls()`), `foldBackCall(payload, alJid, fork?)` (transcript file + wa-history line + the `[WHATSAPP CALL …]` envelope), `requestOutboundCall(to, task)` / `requestHangup(callId)` (→ the pipeline), `startSidecarRelay(cb)` (QR/status client on the sidecar socket).
- `server/src/al/al-session.ts` — `ensureAlSession()` either resumes Al from manifest or spawns fresh with persona; persists `claudeSessionId` to `al-session.json` on first `session_init`. `injectToAl(envelope, broadcast)` is the canonical inbound surface — broadcasts a `user_prompt` so the SPA renders it AND writes to Claude's stdin.
- Env: `server/.env` carries `NOTIFY_JID` (loaded via `--env-file-if-exists=.env` in `server/package.json` scripts; `pm2 restart console-server --update-env` after editing). `ATOMS_*` is dead config — delete it if you see it. The Caddyfile `al.amar.io` block still reverse-proxies `/voice/*` to `https://localhost:9877` (`tls_insecure_skip_verify`); nothing external calls it any more (every voice caller is loopback), so it can be removed from the Caddyfile.

### Sending WhatsApp from a sibling agent
Use `con whatsapp send <to> --body "..."`. The CLI carries an `al`-scoped bearer when enforcement lands.

- `con whatsapp send <to> [--body "..." | --file <path> | --stdin]` — outbound text. `<to>` is bare phone (auto-suffixed `@s.whatsapp.net`) or fully-qualified JID (`<n>@s.whatsapp.net`, `<lid>@lid`, `<id>@g.us`).
- `con whatsapp send <to> --speak "…" [--lang ar] [--speed 0.6–1.5]` — **voice note in Yousef's cloned voice**: hub `al/tts.ts` calls Cartesia (`/tts/bytes`, sonic-3.6, the same clone the Astera demo videos use; config `~/.config/console/cartesia.env` → symlink to `~/.config/astera/cartesia.env`: `CARTESIA_API_KEY`, `CARTESIA_VOICE_ID`, optional `CARTESIA_MODEL`/`CARTESIA_SPEED`), `whatsapp.ts toVoiceNote` transcodes with ffmpeg to mono ogg/opus and computes the 64-bucket waveform + `seconds` itself (Baileys would need the optional `audio-decode` dep), then sends `ptt: true`. `--lang` is the language of the TEXT (`ar` for Arabic — dialect comes from the words; verified Egyptian Arabic round-trips through Whisper). `--audio <file>` sends any ffmpeg-readable file as a voice note instead. Both go through the address censor and land in `wa-history` as `(voice note, Ns) <text>`. Body shapes: `{to, speak, language?, speed?}` / `{to, audio: base64}` — exactly one of `text|speak|audio`.
- `con whatsapp edit <to> [<message_id>|last] --body "…"` (^dry-moth, 2026-09-24 — AL opened a message to Nic with "Al here"; only delete existed) → `POST /whatsapp/edit {to, messageId|'last', text}` → `wa.editText`: Baileys `sendMessage(jid, {text, edit: key})`, which wraps the text as a `protocolMessage` of type MESSAGE_EDIT keyed on the original (the original id stays the message's identity). `last` = the newest `dir: 'out'` entry with an id in wa-history for that thread (`lastOutbound`; 404 when none — never a blind send); a raw id is sent as-is even if history never recorded it. On success `amend` rewrites the history line so the envelope's `Recent thread` shows what the recipient now sees; the reply is `{ ok, id, jid, user, previousText, warning? }`. **WhatsApp applies edits only within ~15 min** (`EDIT_WINDOW_MS`) and silently ignores later ones — no error surfaces, so the route warns from the history timestamp when the original is older, and `send` now returns `editableUntil` beside `id`. Text messages only (a voice note cannot be edited); the address censor runs on the replacement text. Tests: `__tests__/whatsapp-edit.test.ts`.
- `con whatsapp delete <message_id> --to <jid>` — revoke for everyone; WhatsApp silently ignores past ~48 h.
- `con whatsapp contacts [--query <text>]` — workspace lookup; source = `~/.local/share/al/workspace/users/*.md`.
- `con whatsapp qr [--out <path.png>]` — fetch current pairing QR; 404 when paired.
- `con whatsapp status` — `{connected, hasQr}`.

The CLI is the preferred path. If a sibling agent needs Al's interpretation / context, `con agent chat "AL" "please WA Rowan saying X"` — Al sends, holds the thread, forwards replies.

### HTTP routes (hub :9877; voice paths are also reachable via `https://al.amar.io/*`)
- `GET /whatsapp/status` / `GET /whatsapp/qr` / `POST /whatsapp/send` / `POST /whatsapp/delete` / `GET /whatsapp/contacts` — bearer-gated.
- `GET /voice/health` (the only open one) / `GET /voice/status` / `GET /voice/qr` / `POST /voice/session` / `POST /voice/turn` / `POST /voice/interrupt` / `POST /voice/hangup` / `POST /voice/transcript` / `POST /voice/call` / `GET /voice/calls` — **all bearer-gated except `/voice/health`**. The caller is the local voice pipeline carrying the `voice`-scoped token from `local-tokens.json` (`HubTokenScope 'voice'`, minted at boot as `voice-pipeline`); nothing under `/voice` is exempt (opsec rem #63, ^gold-hare: the Atoms-era exemption made `/voice/delegate` an unauthenticated write primitive into Al's session for 86 days). `callerPhone` must be phone-shaped (`normalisePhone`) before it names the caller or seeds a `users/<phone>.md`; `callId` must pass `isSafeCallId` before it names a file. Tests: `server/src/__tests__/voice-auth.test.ts`, `voice-calls.test.ts`.

### WhatsApp voice calls (^wise-lark → ^ripe-elk, 2026-09-20)
AL makes and takes **WhatsApp voice calls from his own account** (+447897073727 — the identity people chat with), speaking in Yousef's Cartesia clone. The call's brain is a **real fork of the AL session** (AL's persona as system prompt, AL's cwd → skills + memory files, the full toolset), one per call, driven turn by turn by the pipeline — Yousef on the first live call: "this delegate thing is just too many layers... make it closer to a normal forked session where you have direct access to all the tools you need... tell people to hold on while you do stuff." Three processes besides the hub:

```
WhatsApp relay ⇄ wa-voice (Rust, voice/wa-voice/, pm2 `wa-voice`, linked device #2, store ~/.config/console/wa-voice/)
                    ⇄ ws://127.0.0.1:9878  JSON control (text frames) + PCM (binary frames)
                 al-voice-pipeline (Python 3.12 + uv + Pipecat, voice/pipeline/, pm2 `al-voice-pipeline`, http://127.0.0.1:9879)
                    Silero VAD → Cartesia STT (`ink-whisper`, language omitted = auto) → ForkLLMService (hub /voice/turn, NDJSON deltas)
