                    → LanguageRouter (per-sentence `language` for Cartesia) → Cartesia TTS (clone, TOKEN mode, 16 kHz raw) → slot
                    ⇄ hub: POST /voice/session · POST /voice/turn · POST /voice/interrupt · POST /voice/transcript
                 hub ⇄ AL voice fork (al/voice-fork.ts: `Call with <name> (fork)`, key `al-call-<id8>-fork`, nested under AL in the Agent tab)
                 hub → parent AL session ([WHATSAPP CALL …] envelope after the call)
```

- **Socket contract** (`voice/wa-voice/src/proto.rs`): binary frame = `[slot: u8][s16le mono 16 kHz PCM, 960 samples = 60 ms]` both ways. Sidecar → client: `{"ev":"status",connected,paired,jid,calls}` + `{"ev":"ready",jid}` on connect and on change, `{"ev":"qr",code,dataUrl,timeoutSecs}`, `{"ev":"incoming",callId,from,video,slot}`, `{"ev":"ringing"|"accepted"|"ended",callId,slot,reason?,durationMs}`, `{"ev":"ack",cmd,callId,slot,id}`, `{"ev":"error",callId?,message,id?}`, `disconnected`, `loggedout`. Client → sidecar: `{"cmd":"call",to,id?}` (`to` = JID or bare phone), `answer`/`reject`/`hangup {callId}`, `flush {callId}` (drop queued outbound PCM — barge-in), `status`, `repair` (unpaired: reconnect for a fresh QR batch now — the sidecar otherwise pauses 3 min after an unscanned batch; `GET /voice/qr` sends it when no QR is cached), `loopback {pcm: base64 s16le 16 kHz}` → `{"ev":"loopback", frames, speechFrames, encodedFrames, encodedBytes, meanSpeechPacket, meanIdlePacket, encodeMsMean, encodeMsP90, encodeMsMax, encodeMsMaxFrame, encodeSlowFrames, inputDbfs, id}` (the PCM through `MicSource` with the live levers + a fresh `MlowEncoder`, no call — `calls.rs loopback()`; the encode-time distribution is per frame, `encodeSlowFrames` = frames ≥ 60 ms), `ping`. `id` on a command is echoed on its ack/error. **The sidecar's 60 ms ticker is the RTP clock**: whatsapp-rust's engine emits one packet per mic frame it is handed, so the sidecar feeds exactly one frame per tick — queued PCM from the socket, else **comfort noise at -60 dBFS** (`IdleNoise`, `WA_VOICE_IDLE_NOISE_DB`; `off` = ±1 LSB dither), NEVER exact zeros. Two lessons from the live calls: wacore's `encode_mlow_frame` treats an all-zero frame as mic-mute and sends a 1-byte DTX comfort-noise packet, and ±1 LSB dither (-90 dBFS, coded as ordinary speech frames, TOC 0x50) STILL lost the first word on the phone ("count to five" → "never heard one") — the receive path adapts to an idle level no microphone produces, so give it a real room-noise floor — and never stalls; the pipeline paces its sends at real time with a 0.3 s lead and `flush`es on interruption. Peer audio is re-chunked to 960 samples. MLOW PCM path (`AudioFormat::MLOW_16KHZ_60MS`), with the libopus fallback decoder for `CallEvent::ForeignAudio` (peer outside the MLow rollout); video offers are rejected; group calls ignored. Built on `oxidezap/whatsapp-rust` pinned by git rev in `Cargo.toml` (needs the nightly in `rust-toolchain.toml`; `opus` builds libopus from source via cmake); `cargo build --release` through `scripts/heavy.sh`.
- **First-word cut-off — ROOT CAUSE FOUND AND FIXED 4 Oct 2026 (^jade-gull): a bug in OUR sidecar, not the phone, not the codec.** `push_audio` re-chunked with `q.pop_back().filter(|f| f.len() < FRAME_SAMPLES)`, which pops the back frame and DROPS it when it is complete; the pipeline sends whole 60 ms frames and bursts ~5 at the start of every sentence (its 0.3 s lead), so each push deleted the previous one — ~240 ms gone from the start of every sentence since the first call, and every lead-in made it worse (speech queued behind it was deleted the same way). Now `enqueue_pcm` (only a PARTIAL back frame is taken back), with burst regression tests that fail on the old code. Proven offline with the real pipeline + real TTS in a queue-emulating harness: deployed logic sent "it's" / "to" / "Let me" where the fix sends "Hi Youssef!" / "One." / "Hold on.". Yousef said "It's in the audio" on 21 Sept and "the way you're constructing this audio is stupid" on 4 Oct; both right. Every phone-side theory below (level gate, DTX, receive AGC, activity flags, lead-ins) is SUPERSEDED — kept only as history. Lesson: the sender was "exonerated" by synthetic encoder loopbacks that never went through `push_audio`; the evidence (a 30–100 ms stub of "one", or "two" first, in our own `-tx.wav`) was in the 3 Oct data and was misread. HISTORY: the phone loses the first ~0.3–0.5 s of every AL utterance. **The codec and our framing are exonerated** — wacore's MLow encoder → WhatsApp's *shipped* WASM decoder (the library's `tools/oracle-cli` over `JgwtTQVeWPm.wasm` from static.whatsapp.net) plays the onset intact (−0.3 dB over the first 100 ms) for both the DTX/SID framing and the continuous-noise framing; encode is ~1 ms/frame; the engine paces regularly. The loss is phone-side and looks level-gated (output-path/BT power-save or a receive-side gate). Plan, evidence and the one-lever-per-call experiment table: `research/voice-cutoff-investigation.md`. Tooling: `voice/wa-voice/examples/onset.rs` (idle→speech loopback + packet dump) and `voice/wa-voice/tools/mlow-oracle/` (specs for the real decoder/encoder). **Every call now writes `~/.cache/console/voice-wire/<callId>.log`** (`wire.rs`: the library's `PacketTap` around the native relay dialler — RTP header/size/cadence both ways, ticker onsets, engine events, final `media_stats`; `WA_VOICE_WIRE_LOG=0` = library default dialler). **Update 21 Sept**: earpiece and speaker cut identically (Yousef) — output-device gates ruled out; it's the phone's common receive path. **Pre-roll incident the same day (fixed 113213ec, read before touching the ticker)**: the first call with the pre-roll on was SILENT for the callee for 56 s — the pre-roll re-armed every time it drained while the queue was non-empty, so only noise went out and the whole utterance queue overflowed into the bin. The mic clock's frame source is now `MicSource`, an `Idle → Preroll → Speaking` machine that arms the pre-roll only on the idle→speech transition (8-tick hangover so a lagging pipeline can't splice noise mid-sentence), with six tests pinning "every queued frame reaches the wire exactly once after exactly one pre-roll"; `MAX_QUEUE_FRAMES` overflow is now logged instead of silent. **Triage any "they heard nothing" report from the wire log first**: `idle after N speech frames` lines (one per talkspurt; N × 60 ms = how much of AL's speech left the wire — with the pre-roll ON `onset` lines are NEVER written, `MicSource` goes Preroll → Speaking without one, so `onset=0` proves nothing) and no `queue overflow` line means our speech went out; the peer's RTCP `fraction_lost`/`cumulative_lost` says whether their phone accepted it; `WA_VOICE_CAPTURE=1` (ON since 24 Sept) adds `<callId>-tx.wav` = the audio itself. **Pre-roll OFF since 24 Sept (^lime-tern)**: it was ON 21–23 Sept (`WA_VOICE_ONSET_PREROLL_MS=300`, noise) and every call with it on was inaudible to the callee — Mai 21 Sept (the re-arm bug) and Yousef 23 Sept 23:29 (00276e96: 65 contiguous speech frames on the wire, 0 % loss at his phone, he heard nothing — not the transcript-only pipecat `Dropping buffered word` warnings, which fire AFTER the audio has gone out) — while every audible call ran without it. Last-known-good = off; the first-word cut-off is the lesser evil. Re-enable only for a deliberate A/B call. Levers in `~/.config/console/voice.env` — **the sidecar reads it at startup** (`load_voice_env`, process env wins; no inline comments on value lines; startup log line `levers at startup:` proves what loaded), so set one and `pm2 restart wa-voice`: `WA_VOICE_CAPTURE=1` (tx/rx wavs), `WA_VOICE_IDLE_NOISE_DB`, `WA_VOICE_DTX=1` (zeros → SID + marker), `WA_VOICE_ONSET_PREROLL_MS` + `WA_VOICE_ONSET_PREROLL=tone|noise` [+ `_DB`] (pre-roll before each utterance; the tone is the diagnostic for a level gate). **Sixth confirmation 24 Sept 12:19 (0074df98, all levers off, ^jade-gull) — `-tx.wav` has "One" intact (40 ms in, 180 ms long, −24 dBFS), the phone accepted every packet (332 RTCP reports, 0 loss), the sender's clock and cadence are clean: the loss is a ≥ 220 ms window inside the phone's playout and no remaining lever is safe to ship blind.** So the next call is a MEASUREMENT, not a guess: `WA_VOICE_ROOM_CAPTURE=<pipewire source>` (`wire.rs` runs `pw-record` on this machine's mic for the call → `<callId>-room.wav`; the desk C615 is `alsa_input.usb-046d_HD_Webcam_C615_965F0360-00.mono-fallback`; phone on speaker at the desk = what the phone PLAYED) and `WA_VOICE_SWEEP=base,dtx,floor:-45,tone:300@-30,…` (`calls.rs` `Levers::parse` / `MicSource::with_sweep`: one lever set per talkspurt, advancing at utterance end so it governs the idle gap AND the onset; every `onset`/`preroll start` wire line carries `lever=<label> #k/n`; atoms `base|dtx|floor:<dB>|tone:<ms>[@<dB>]|noise:<ms>[@<dB>]` joined by `+`); then `python3 voice/wa-voice/tools/onset_loss.py <callId>` → per onset: loss in ms, attack curve in 100 ms bins, how much of a tone pre-roll was heard, grouped by lever — the result→fix table is in `research/voice-cutoff-investigation.md` ("24 Sept"). Both flags stay COMMENTED OUT between diagnostic calls (the tone is audible to whoever AL calls; the desk mic must not record every call); a single-entry/absent sweep leaves production behaviour byte-identical. **3 Oct outcome (two sweep calls, 20 counts graded by Yousef, room mic on): every lever cut — idle floor −60/−45, SID-every-frame DTX, AND 300 ms lead-ins (tone −30, noise −40, breath clips via the new `clip:<name>[@dB]` pre-roll — `~/.config/console/voice-clips/breath*.wav`), which the phone plays (boosted ~+15 dB) and then still fades the first speech word in from ~20 dB down over ~100 ms after a click (raw waveform; he confirmed the cut on the recording itself). Lead-ins are DEAD; production stays without one. The untested ingredient is a real packet GAP before speech (wacore sends a packet every 60 ms whatever the content; phones send a SID per ~400 ms–2 s and nothing between) — needs a `[patch]` of whatsapp-rust so the RTP clock advances while idle. Room-mic metrics from `onset_loss.py` are indicative only (±5 dB); the arbiter is the raw waveform + Yousef listening to the room clip.** **3 Oct, later: the third silence shape is now IMPLEMENTED and deployed.** wacore's encoder was config-0/active-only ("R9"), so every frame AL ever sent — including the quiet floor between utterances — carried TOC `0x50`, "active voice"; the shipped encoder codes that same silence as `0x10`/`0x12` (inactive / DTX hangover) frames. Filed upstream as oxidezap/whatsapp-rust#1615 and fixed in PR #1616 (4 files in `wacore/src/voip/mlow/`: the VAD reports `speech_detected` so the TOC's three states follow it, `encode_smpl_lsf` omits the two symbols `decode_smpl_lsf` already skips, the pulse-geometry flag `p4` becomes the activity flag — it is the frame-type row `[80,160,160]` shared with `smpl_perc`'s cap, and hard-coding it to 1 concealed ~3/4 of inactive frames at the reader, and the LSF interpolation search is pinned to index 0 when inactive). Verified by 140 wacore mlow tests (2 new) AND the shipped engine via `oracle derive` (inactive frames decode to −66.2 dBFS against −64.8 in; the talkspurt after 36 inactive frames returns at −13.8 dBFS vs −13.7 for the one before, corr 0.97 — no desync). **Console's sidecar now builds against `yousefamar/whatsapp-rust` rev `8b78151c`** (= the old pinned `908e62b6` + that one commit, so the encoder is the only delta; #1616 was MERGED upstream 3 Oct as `99a9a118`, so any upstream rev at or after it carries the fix and the next upstream bump drops the fork; docs follow-up PR #1620) — rebuilt, deployed and loopback-green on 3 Oct 21:16. The remaining test is one live call: the wire log's idle packets should drop from 42–77 B to the reference's ~13–27 B, and the question is whether Yousef hears the first word whole. Still untested on the wire: the packet-GAP idea (idle cadence is still one packet per 60 ms, because `RtpStream` only advances the RTP clock when it sends; that is question 3 on #1615).
- **Line-health events (ff54c3f0, ^teal-carp)**: the sidecar emits `ev:health {callId, slot, kind, issue}` when the line is provably degraded — `outbound-lost` (the `MAX_QUEUE_FRAMES` overflow guard: AL's speech is being discarded), `inbound-silent` / `inbound-stalled` (the engine's own `AudioSilent`/`AudioReceptionStalled` alarms — the CALLER's audio stopped; it fires when they simply listen for 4 s, so it is a cue, never a hangup trigger). The pipeline (`main.py` → `CallSession.notify_line_problem`) kicks the fork ONCE per kind with a `(Line problem — …)` cue so AL tells the caller and hangs up instead of talking into static (call 00357d4b ran 11 turns against a dead outbound path with every layer convinced the call was healthy); `outbound-lost` goes straight to the fail-loud path below (nothing the fork says can be heard); kinds land in the transcript payload as `lineProblems`.
- **Fail loud (^lime-tern, 24 Sept — call 008048b0: Pipecat's setup timed out after Yousef picked up and he heard 33 s of comfort noise, 0 turns, an EMPTY transcript envelope)**: `CallSession.fail(why)` plays the pre-rendered apology straight to the slot (`clips.py`: `~/.config/console/voice-clips/<name>.<lang>.<hash>.pcm`, Yousef's clone via Cartesia REST, rendered once at pipeline boot for every voice language, English first, fallback en; `transport.play_pcm` paces it — no live TTS involved), hangs up, and the transcript carries `outcome: failed`, `reason: PIPELINE SETUP FAILED: <why>`, the clip as an assistant turn, plus a `setup` block (per-processor setup seconds, `stillConnecting`). The hub's `callEnvelope` for `failed` tells AL what the caller heard and to text them ("you promised a message"); `endCallFork` already skips the merge for non-completed outcomes so the parent gets the envelope. Triggers: Pipecat `on_setup_timeout` (`VOICE_SETUP_TIMEOUT`, 20 s; the log names the processor still connecting — `CartesiaTTSService (cancelled after 20 s)`), the StartFrame `on_pipeline_timeout`, a Cartesia STT/TTS `on_connection_error` before ready, a fatal `on_pipeline_error`, the pipeline finishing on its own mid-call, a `prepare()` exception, `outbound-lost`, **the pickup watchdog** (`go()`: pipeline not ready `VOICE_HOLD_ON_AFTER`=2 s after pickup → the hold-on clip; not ready at `VOICE_SETUP_GRACE`=12 s → fail), and **the mute-turn alarm** (`_expect_audio`: the fork produced text but no TTS audio frame reached the slot within `VOICE_TTS_AUDIO_TIMEOUT`=8 s — the case the sidecar's health events cannot see). Still ringing when it fails → hangup without speaking (a pickup in the race gets the apology from `go()`). Root cause of 008048b0's late start: `_build()` ran the Silero ONNX load synchronously on the event loop — 26 s on a cold, saturated disk (load 15, first call in 13 h), which also delayed the `accepted` event 8 s — so the pipeline only began connecting at pickup; the VAD is now built in a thread and warmed at boot (`CallManager._warm`), and every processor's `setup` is timed (`pipeline ready 0.2 s after prepare (setup: CartesiaSTTService 0.2 s, …)`). **Double build (^wise-finch, call 006B2AD9, 1 Oct 2026 — Rebaz picked up 0.5 s into the inbound build; `go()` saw no `_pipeline_task` yet and started a second `prepare()`)**: two pipelines were built on one slot, both resuming into the same `self._transport`, so both StartFrames ran down one chain (`pipeline ready` logged twice with doubled STT/TTS), the orphan `PipelineTask` hit its 20 s StartFrame timeout and its `_cleanup()` tore down the shared transports — reproduced: the live pipeline is mute from that point. `prepare()` is now idempotent from its FIRST call (`build_started_at`, set before the VAD await), `_build()` keeps every processor in locals, and the `accepted` log says `N s into the build` instead of `not yet prepared`; `test_a_pickup_during_the_build_does_not_build_a_second_pipeline` pins it. A log with two `CartesiaSTTService#N: Connected` lines for one call is this bug. `GET /health` (= `con whatsapp voice` → `pipeline`) now exercises what a call needs — `hub`, the sidecar socket round-tripped (`rtt_ms`), `cartesia {ok, ms}` (voice record GET, cached 15 s), **`loopback {ok, speechFrames, meanSpeechPacket, meanIdlePacket, …}`** (the pre-call end-to-end audio loopback: the `hold_on.en` clip — Yousef's clone, the exact PCM a call sends — through the sidecar socket, its mic clock and the MLow encoder, packet report back; `main.py loopback_verdict` → `(ok, why, check)`: every clip frame must come out of the mic clock as signal (`check: mic_clock`), every tick must yield a packet (`packets`), and encoding must be real-time SUSTAINED (`encode_speed`: the **p90** per-frame encode time < `ENCODE_BUDGET_MS` 60 — NOT the max: call 00AB3AFC, 30 Sept 2026, was refused for one 80 ms frame in 47 at load 3.4 while the other 46 took ~1 ms; a single pre-empted frame is jitter the call rides out, five-plus late frames of 47 is a codec that cannot keep up); `why` quotes the threshold and the measurement (`p90 72 ms per frame against the 60 ms budget (mean …, worst … at frame N, K of 47 frames over budget)`), and a pass with one stalled frame logs `passed with one stalled frame`. An `encode_speed` failure is **re-run once after `LOOPBACK_RETRY_AFTER` 1 s** (`attempts`, `firstAttempt` in the health JSON) — the other checks are deterministic and are not. Packet SIZE is deliberately not judged: MLow is content-adaptive, a pure tone codes ~80 B while the -60 dBFS noise floor codes ~127 B; cached 60 s), `clips {ready, missing}` — and `ok` is the AND; it said all green at 23:22 on 23 Sept with Cartesia never probed. **Both `place_call` and `_on_incoming` run the loopback fresh and refuse the call (503 / reject → `failed: PIPELINE SETUP FAILED: pre-call audio loopback [<check>]: …`) when it fails** — everything short of the network is proven before anyone hears a ring. Deploy order when the socket protocol grows: the sidecar binary first, then the pipeline (an old sidecar answers `loopback` with an error → every call refused). Tests: `voice/pipeline/tests/test_failsafe.py` (fake sidecar + clips, no network) and `tests/harness.py --setup-fail` (a tarpit TTS URL via `CARTESIA_TTS_URL` + `VOICE_SETUP_TIMEOUT=5`: hold-on clip, apology, hangup, `PIPELINE SETUP FAILED`, all inside the grace window); hub side `voice-calls.test.ts` (`failed` envelopes). Known cosmetic: pipecat's word-timestamp sequencer drops TTSTextFrames it cannot match to a slot (`Dropping buffered word … not recognised by any slot`), so an assistant turn can be missing/truncated in the pipeline's `turns` — audio is unaffected (pushed independently as `TTSAudioRawFrame`), and the fork session has the full text.
- **Call lifecycle**: outbound `POST /voice/call` → pipeline dials (the sidecar mints the id) → `POST /voice/session` (answer policy + `startCallFork`; the envelope is the warm turn and, outbound, asks the fork for its OPENING LINE — the exact words for pickup) → the pipeline BUILDS AND STARTS the Pipecat pipeline during the ring (`CallSession.prepare`: STT/TTS sockets open, VAD loaded) → `accepted` → `go()` sends the `(The call was answered.)` cue → the hub speaks the pre-generated line instantly (`runCue`: 0 ms fork latency; still streaming if the warm turn is mid-way) and tells the fork what it opened with on the first real utterance. The Nica call (20 Sept) showed why: a post-pickup turn is ~2 s TTFT + a sentence of buffering, and by then she had said "Hello?", which barged in on the greeting. Inbound: `incoming` → `/voice/session` → answer + prepare in parallel; the caller's first words hit a live pipeline; `(The caller has said nothing for two seconds.)` only if VAD saw no speech. Call 2 on 20 Sept doubled its greeting because the pipeline used to start on `accepted` and a 7 s Cartesia connect queued the greeting behind Yousef's "Hello?". **Turns**: each finished utterance → `POST /voice/turn {callId, text, cue?, interruptedAfter?}` → the hub sends it into the fork (logged as a `user_prompt`, so the fork session IS the live transcript) and streams back `{type: text|tool|result|error}` lines; text → TTS as it arrives; `tool` with nothing spoken yet → a filler in the conversation's language; the fork is told to say a holding phrase itself before any tool. **The `tool` line is sent on the hub's `tool_use_start` message** (emitted by `Session.handleStreamEvent` at the tool block's `content_block_start`, i.e. the instant the model stops writing text — ephemeral, never logged), NOT on the finalized `tool_use`, which only lands once the arguments have streamed in (~0.5–1 s for a Bash call) and by then only records the tool summary on the call. The pipeline closes the spoken segment on `tool` so the holding phrase plays during the tool call; on the 24 Sept confirming call it sat unspoken through the whole calendar lookup and came out glued to the answer as "calendar.Nothing" (^kind-crow, ^glad-swan). **Barge-in** (^loud-fawn, 24 Sept): while AL is talking the caller cuts him off with `VOICE_INTERRUPT_MIN_SECS` (0.7) of continuous speech (`al_voice/barge_in.py` `SustainedSpeechUserTurnStartStrategy`, VAD-driven) OR `VOICE_INTERRUPT_MIN_WORDS` (2) in the final transcript (`MinWordsUserTurnStartStrategy`) — a bare "Hello?"/"yeah" over AL cancels nothing (the Nica call); any speech starts a turn when AL is quiet; `MIN_WORDS=1` = pipecat's plain VAD start. Words alone never worked mid-sentence: **Cartesia ink-whisper sends NO interim transcripts** — one final ~0.4 s after the caller stops (replay of Yousef's seven "hello"s from the 24 Sept call: single final at 2.9 s; `ink-2` only chunks finals, first at 1.4 s, English-only) — so the old word-only start fired after he had finished and AL had too. Harness `--barge` now uses q6 (continuous "hello ×9") and fails unless the flush lands ≤ 1.2 s after the caller starts (main before the fix: 2.6 s; after: 0.99 s) → pipecat cancels the streaming task → `POST /voice/interrupt` → `Session.softInterrupt()` (stream-json `interrupt` control request; SIGINT only as fallback) → the next utterance carries `(you were interrupted after: "<what was actually heard>")`. **Hang-up**: the fork runs `con whatsapp hangup <callId>` once after its goodbye (`POST /voice/hangup` → pipeline `POST /hangup/<id>` → graceful: waits for the current playout, 8 s grace cap); safety net in `call.py` `HANGUP_RE` ("hang up", "end the call", "leg auf", "اقفل" …) hangs up after the reply if the fork did not. **End**: `POST /voice/transcript` → `call-transcripts/<id>.json` (+ `fork` block) and a wa-history line; then the fork hands back **exactly like a chat fork** (Yousef, 20 Sept: "the parent should not get the transcript when the fork already has it… hand back like a chat fork"): `endCallFork` runs `mergeIntoParent(request = closingTurn)` — the fork finishes what it promised, updates memory, writes a digest as its final message, and AL receives `[MERGE — fork "Call with X (fork)" folded in and closed]` + digest; the fork is closed by the merge. The parent gets the `[WHATSAPP CALL …]` transcript envelope ONLY when no fork ran the call (rejected/missed/no-answer). If the merge cannot run (AL not live, fork ended) the fork is closed and the transcript file is the record. Events on the bus: `voice.call.started|turn|ended` (`con event tail voice.call.turn` = a live transcript in any terminal).
- **Visibility**: `GET /voice/calls` → `{live: [...], calls: [...]}` and `GET /voice/status` carry the in-progress calls (elapsed, turns so far, last line, fork session id); `con whatsapp calls [--live]`, `con whatsapp voice`, `con whatsapp hangup <id>`. The fork session in the Agent tab shows every utterance (user_prompt) and reply live.
- **Pipeline** (`voice/pipeline/al_voice/`): `sidecar.py` (one reconnecting socket; audio routed per slot; commands correlated by `id`; **events are handed to the handler through a queue on their own task** — awaiting the handler inside the read loop deadlocked every inbound call, because `answer()` waits for an ack only the read loop can deliver), `transport.py` (`WhatsAppCallTransport`; 60 ms output chunks; end-of-speech → first audio per turn + TTFB metrics), `fork_llm.py` (`ForkLLMService`: the pipecat LLM whose "model" is the hub fork; cue detection = a parenthesised message; `_cut_off()` on CancelledError), `language.py` (`LanguageRouter`: sentence re-chunking with pipecat's own aggregator, script-first detection then `py3langid` restricted to the voice's languages, sticky unless confident; `voice_languages()` reads the clone's `accents` at startup; fillers/apologies per language), `call.py` (`CallSession.prepare/go`, `TranscriptCollector` from `TTSTextFrame`s in playout order, the graceful hang-up state machine), `main.py` (FastAPI `POST /call`, `GET /health`, `GET /calls`, `POST /hangup/<id>[?now=1]`; `ended` → `POST /voice/transcript` for EVERY outcome, spooled to `~/.config/console/voice-calls/<id>.unsent.json` if the hub is down). Config: `~/.config/console/voice.env` (`WA_VOICE_PORT`, `VOICE_PIPELINE_PORT`, `VOICE_STT=cartesia|openai`, `VOICE_STT_MODEL`, `VOICE_OPENAI_STT_MODEL`, `VOICE_STT_LANGUAGE` (start language, default en), `VOICE_TTS_LANGUAGES`, `VOICE_EXTRA_LANGUAGES`, `VOICE_INTERRUPT_MIN_SECS` (0.7), `VOICE_INTERRUPT_MIN_WORDS` (2), `VOICE_TURN_STOP=timeout|smart`, `VOICE_USER_SPEECH_TIMEOUT` (0.4), `VOICE_HANGUP_GRACE` (8), `VOICE_TURN_TIMEOUT` (200), `VOICE_SETUP_TIMEOUT` (20), `VOICE_HOLD_ON_AFTER` (2), `VOICE_SETUP_GRACE` (12), `VOICE_TTS_AUDIO_TIMEOUT` (8), `VOICE_CLIPS_DIR`, `CARTESIA_TTS_URL` (harness fault injection only), `VOICE_INBOUND=0` to reject all inbound; hub-side `VOICE_FORK_MODEL` (default `claude-sonnet-5`) and `VOICE_FORK_CONTEXT=fresh|inherited`) + `cartesia.env`; `voice` bearer from `local-tokens.json`. No Bedrock config any more. **Harness without WhatsApp or the hub**: `uv run python tests/make_wavs.py` once, then `uv run python tests/harness.py [--inbound] [--barge] [--setup-fail]` (fake sidecar + fake hub with a scripted fork, real Cartesia; PASS/FAIL checks: one greeting, first audio ≤ 3 s after accept, hangup after the caller asks and only after playout, barge-in → flush ≤ 1.2 s after the caller starts talking over AL + interrupt + interruptedAfter; NB the spawned pipeline imports the CWD's `al_voice` whatever `PYTHONPATH` says (`-m` puts cwd first) — to run main's code against a worktree's harness use `--no-spawn` with a pipeline started from main; `--setup-fail` = the fail-loud path). The harness's STT occasionally hears q3 as "Okay, thanks. up." — the three hangup checks then fail for that run alone; rerun. `tests/bench_fork_ttft.py` times real Claude Code fork turns per model.
- **Hub** (`al/voice.ts`, `al/voice-fork.ts`, `routes/al.ts`): `prepareCall(jid, {callId, direction, task})` → answer policy + `buildCallEnvelope` (caller's users body, **every OTHER `users/*.md` body** (`loadContacts`, ~10k tokens — a fresh fork has none of the address book a text fork inherits from AL's transcript, and a Read mid-call is dead air; 24 Sept 2026 Yousef had to be asked who "Yasin" was because the fact sat in his siblings' files, not his own — ^odd-yak), last 30 wa-history lines, open threads, task, "Reply with exactly the word: ready"). **Answer policy** (`answerPolicy`): `trust: owner` always; a known contact unless their `users/<slug>.md` has `calls: false` or `calls` under `deny:`; unknown numbers never (→ `[MISSED WHATSAPP CALL …]` envelope). Fresh mode (default) = `createSession` at AL's cwd with `systemPrompt = buildAlSystemPrompt() + voiceForkRules(callId)`; `inherited` = `--fork-session` of AL's transcript (rules travel in the envelope; ~130k tokens re-read per turn — slower). `POST /voice/call {to, task}` still **refuses a JID with no prior `wa-history` thread**. `startSidecarRelay` relays the sidecar's `qr` into AL's session and serves `/voice/status` + `/voice/qr`. Tests: `voice-calls.test.ts`, `voice-fork.test.ts` (stub session: serialised turns, deltas, tool split, barge-in, closing turn + reap), `voice-auth.test.ts`.
- **Languages**: Cartesia phonemises in the `language` it is given and pipecat sent `en` on every request — the first call's Arabic and German "with a strong English accent". The clone is single-language by construction (instant clones are built from one recording; `GET /voices/<id>` showed `language: en`, no `accents`); the documented path is `PATCH /voices/<id> {accent}` to label the native accent, then `PATCH /voices/<id>/accents` (Add Voice Accents) — done 20 Sept: `british` (native) + `modern-standard-arabic` + `standard-german` + `standard-italian` + `parisian` + `castilian` (6 of 10). Egyptian Arabic is not a localizable accent (only MSA is); languages without an accent fall back to British with correct normalisation. The router detects across the voice's accents ∪ `VOICE_EXTRA_LANGUAGES` (it/fr/es/pt/nl/tr) — restricting to en/de gave Italian English phonemes (fifth call). **STT has no auto-detect**: Cartesia `ink-whisper`'s `language` "defaults to en" (the code's `auto` was a no-op — Yousef's Arabic came back as English gibberish), so the router pushes `STTUpdateSettingsFrame` upstream on every switch and Cartesia reconnects in the new language: the STT follows whatever AL last spoke, a call starts in the caller's `users/<slug>.md` `language:` (default en), and the fork is told to ask "which language?" when a transcript is nonsense right after a switch. `VOICE_STT=openai` swaps in `OpenAIRealtimeSTTService` (`gpt-transcribe`, language omitted = per-utterance auto-detect, Arabic dialects) for true code-switching at ~+0.8 s per turn (harness: 2.2-2.5 s vs 1.4-1.7 s speech-end → reply). The fork's rules: Arabic ALWAYS in Arabic script — Franco-Arabic digits (3ayez, 7aga) are read aloud as English numbers.
- **Latency, measured 2026-09-20**: the old direct-Bedrock brain did end-of-speech → first audio 1.26-1.82 s (median 1.42; Haiku TTFB 1.12, STT 0.29, TTS 0.10) on the live call. A Claude Code fork turn (AL persona at AL's cwd, warm cache, `tests/bench_fork_ttft.py`) reaches its first word in **~1.8-2.5 s on Sonnet 5 ≈ 2.0-2.4 s on Haiku 4.5, ~2.9 s on Fable 5.1**; a turn that uses a tool adds the tool's duration after the holding phrase. So the honest budget with the fork is **~2.5 s to first audio**, not the spec's 1.2 s — the price of direct tools, which Yousef chose. Sonnet 5 is the default (`VOICE_FORK_MODEL`). Live (calls 3-4, Sonnet 5): end-of-speech → first audio 2.6-3.7 s (fork first text 2.0-3.2 s). Harness: greeting 0.3 s after pickup (pre-generated), ~0.9 s of pipeline overhead per turn on top of the fork's TTFT (turn-stop 0.4 + STT final 0.3 + TTS 0.1).
- **Deploy**: both run from the MAIN checkout under pm2 — `wa-voice` executes `voice/wa-voice/target/release/wa-voice` (a build artefact, gitignored: after a sidecar change run `cargo build --release` in main, `pm2 stop wa-voice` BEFORE overwriting the binary — a running binary is "Text file busy" — then `pm2 start wa-voice`); `al-voice-pipeline` runs `voice/pipeline/.venv/bin/al-voice-pipeline`, an editable `uv sync` install, so a source change on main applies on `pm2 restart al-voice-pipeline` (re-run `uv sync` only when `pyproject.toml` changed). `pm2 save` after either. Neither needs a hub restart; hub-side `al/voice.ts` changes do.
- **Guardrails**: never dial a number AL has not chatted with; test on Yousef's numbers only, and NO test call to him unless he asks (20 Sept: "Are you wasting time?") — verify with the harness/unit tests and ask in the session first when a real call is genuinely needed; every utterance is Yousef's voice (voice-note policy); heavy steps (`cargo build`, `uv sync`, tests) through `scripts/heavy.sh`.
- **CPU priority (^tidy-deer, 30 Sept 2026 — two calls unusable at load 16 from forks' test runs: 00AB3AFC refused on one 80 ms encode frame, 00817e79 answered but the pipeline's 2 s pickup watchdog fired at 10.5 s and no TTS audio reached the wire in 28 s)**. Three levers, all default-on: (1) **the hub spawns every agent session under `nice -n 10`** (`session.ts agentNice`, `CONSOLE_AGENT_NICE=0` disables; `nice` execs claude in place so the pid/argv are the claude process) — every test run, build and dev server a fork starts inherits it, so the hub, wa-voice and al-voice-pipeline (nice 0) and Yousef's desktop win contention; among themselves agents share as before; (2) `scripts/heavy.sh` additionally runs its step at `nice 10` + `ionice -c2 -n7` (`CONSOLE_HEAVY_NICE`); (3) **wa-voice and al-voice-pipeline try `nice -10` at startup** (`WA_VOICE_NICE` / `VOICE_PIPELINE_NICE`, default -10, `off` to skip; set before the tokio runtime / event loop so every thread inherits) — this needs RLIMIT_NICE headroom and **pm2-amar.service runs with `LimitNICE=0`, so today both log one `nice -10 refused` warning and run at nice 0**; to unlock: drop-in `/etc/systemd/system/pm2-amar.service.d/nice.conf` with `[Service]\nLimitNICE=-15` + `sudo systemctl daemon-reload` (takes effect at the next pm2 daemon start), and live without restarting pm2: `sudo prlimit --pid $(cat ~/.pm2/pm2.pid) --nice=35` then `pm2 restart wa-voice al-voice-pipeline` (children inherit the daemon's rlimit; `--nice=35` = ceiling nice -15). Not done: SCHED_RR for the encoder (it lives inside whatsapp-rust's engine task, not a thread of ours) and pausing heavy jobs on `voice.call.started` (nice is one-way without privilege — a +10 process cannot renice itself back to 0).
### Inbound flow
Baileys `onMessage` (after `fromMe` filter) → `ensureUserKnown` → `inboundEnvelope(msg, resolvedUser)` → `injectToAl(envelope, broadcast)`. The envelope is **task-framed** (not a chat message) so Claude doesn't default to a silent in-session reply. Shape:
```
[INBOUND WhatsApp — action required]
From: <senderName> (<sender-jid>) — resolved user: <name or "unknown">
Thread: <jid>
Message ID: <wamid.HBgL...>

Message:                                   ← for a ptt voice note this slot is instead
<body>                                       "Message (voice note, transcribed — treat exactly like typed text):" + transcript,
                                             or "(voice note — transcription FAILED; …)" — never "attachment only"
[Attached image(s) (Read these paths): /tmp/console-al-inbound/<id>-0.jpg]   ← only when an image is attached
[INBOUND WhatsApp] Attached file (audio, audio/ogg): /tmp/console-al-inbound/<id>-0.ogg   ← shared audio/video/document, any type
(voice note audio: /tmp/console-al-inbound/<id>-0.ogg)   ← the ptt file itself, for the record

---
ACTION ... con whatsapp send <jid> --body "..."  (a session-only text reply is invisible to the sender)
```
**Read text AND media only through `inboundContent(msg.message)`** (`whatsapp.ts`): it unwraps via Baileys' `normalizeMessageContent` — newer clients send a captioned document as `documentWithCaptionMessage.message.documentMessage`, and ephemeral/view-once/edited messages wrap everything the same way — so reading `msg.message.documentMessage` directly finds neither the caption nor the file. A caption on an image/video/document IS the message and fills the `Message:` slot (^hazy-bear, 2026-09-15: a 1,023-char brief on a zip arrived as "(no text — attachment only)" and Al worked the file blind). Tests in `__tests__/whatsapp-caption-text.test.ts`. Inbound images/audio/video/documents are all downloaded by `whatsapp.ts`, written to `os.tmpdir()/console-al-inbound/` (documents keep their real filename), and their paths injected into the envelope so Al can `Read` them (the text-only inject path can't carry bytes). Voice notes (audio + `ptt`, `WhatsAppFile.voiceNote`) are auto-transcribed via `al/transcribe.ts` (`STT_BATCH_MODEL` on OpenAI, Gemini fallback) and the transcript IS the `Message:` slot — Al handles it exactly like typed text; a failed transcription says so in the slot so he asks the sender to type it. No OPENAI_API_KEY/GEMINI_API_KEY configured just skips transcription (never blocks delivery of the audio file itself). **End-to-end test recipe (^lime-boar, 2026-09-14)**: `espeak-ng -w x.wav "…" && ffmpeg -i x.wav -c:a libopus -ar 48000 -ac 1 x.ogg`, then `con chat send-file '!0VApYxoYBKX9U407pbEe:beeper.local' x.ogg --voice` (Yousef's Beeper DM with AL; `--voice` adds the MSC3245 key, without which the bridge sends a plain audio file and Baileys sees `ptt: false`) — the transcript shows up in `~/.config/console/wa-history.json` and in AL's envelope within ~20 s. Voice calls no longer inject `[Voice delegate …]` envelopes into AL mid-call (^ripe-elk): the call runs in its own fork; AL only receives the `[WHATSAPP CALL …]` fold-back afterwards.

### Conversation forks (per-thread routing — `al/conversation-forks.ts`)
**Non-owner WhatsApp threads no longer inject into Al's main session.** `routeInbound()` (wired in the `onInbound` handler before `injectToAl`) forks AL per thread JID (`AL ↔ <person>`, `--fork-session` off Al's claudeSessionId) and routes all later messages from that thread to the fork; **Yousef's threads always go to the parent** (`resolvedUser === 'yousef'` bypass — routing the owner through a digest-delayed fork would hollow out parent-Al). Why: privacy (concurrent conversations — e.g. Mai + Nica — never share a context window, so per-contact deny-walls become a physical boundary) + parent context hygiene. Mechanics: routing table persisted at `~/.config/console/al-conversation-forks.json` (atomic write; restored on boot by `startConversationForks`, which re-points hub ids the restore loop re-minted via claudeSessionId match and drops dead records); messages arriving during the fork's spawn gap queue in `pendingSpawns` and flush on `session_init` (the `--fork-session` no-init-until-first-input gotcha applies — seed + first envelope are sent immediately); fork spawn failure falls back to the parent (never drops a message). **Idle wind-down** (1 min sweep, 1 h idle, measured from `lastMovedAt` = the later of the last inbound message and the end of the fork's last turn): **the default is a digest-merge into the parent** via `mergeIntoParent`; a fork is reaped silently only when it is PROVABLY trivial (`mergeReason` returns null): the hub watched every tool call of its life (`observed`), every one was a bare `con whatsapp send` to its own thread (`isReplySend` — a send to anyone else, a chained command, or any other tool is work), and it had ≤2 inbound messages. `watchFork` gathers that evidence from the fork's own `hub_message` stream from before its first wake, and is re-attached on boot. A mid-turn fork gets its deadline pushed, never interrupted. **Why merge-by-default (9 Oct 2026):** the old rule promised "any tool call beyond the reply makes it substantive" but `markSubstantive` had no caller, so message count alone decided — a fork that did 95 minutes and 245 tool calls for one request (asked Yousef, wrote 17 files, published a canvas tab) had 2 inbound messages and was removed as "trivial (2 msg)" with no merge, 15 minutes after finishing, because the clock ran from her last message. Yousef: "we can't lose fork information like this." A fork-reaping rule must reap on evidence and merge on doubt; a record from before tracking existed is merged, never guessed. Unit-tested in `__tests__/conversation-forks.test.ts` (stubbed createSession/wakeSession/merge; forks file via `CONSOLE_AL_FORKS_FILE`).
- **A contact is one PERSON across two JIDs, and the thread alone can't show what they're replying to.** Al's sends go to whichever JID he picks (usually the phone `447…@s.whatsapp.net`); the reply often arrives from the linked-device `…@lid`. Label the IDENTITY at both ends, never restate history (quoting Al's last sends into every envelope was vetoed as heavy/redundant — the model already has the transcript, it just needs to know two names are one person): (1) the envelope's From line reads `resolved user: nica (same person as 447776912442)` (`identifiersFor(user)` minus the sender's own id, via `users.ts`); (2) `POST /whatsapp/send` returns `user: <resolved>` beside `jid`, so the send's tool result carries the same canonical name the inbound will; (3) `forkSeed` names the other identifiers as "the SAME PERSON". ~10 tokens per event. Tests in `__tests__/al-contact-identity.test.ts`.
- **The envelope DOES carry the recent thread (supersedes the "never restate history" veto above, 2026-09-11, ^zany-crow).** The veto assumed the reader already had the transcript; it doesn't when a conversation fork did the sending — the nica fork relayed Nica's messages to Yousef's DM, Yousef's owner-thread reply "Tell her yeet" landed in the PARENT, whose envelope held one message and no antecedent ("who is her?"). `al/wa-history.ts` is a persisted ring buffer (`~/.config/console/wa-history.json`, last 50 per thread, text capped at 300 chars; `CONSOLE_WA_HISTORY_FILE` for tests) — inbound recorded in the `onInbound` handler before routing, outbound in `POST /whatsapp/send` after `sendText` succeeds with `via` = the caller's `X-Console-Agent` (else `al`). `inboundEnvelope(..., { history, forks })` renders `Recent thread (oldest first):` between Message ID and Message — last 6 entries across ALL of the contact's identifiers (phone + @lid = one thread), current message excluded, one line each: `19:17 Nica: …` / `19:18 AL→Nica: …` / `19:39 AL(nica fork)→Yousef: …`; omitted when empty. Owner thread only: `Active conversation forks: nica (last message 19:39)` from `forkSummaries()`, so the parent knows a relay is mid-flight even before any history exists. Conversation forks now get an `agentKey` (`al-<contact>`, minted via `mintAgentKey`) purely so their `con whatsapp send` calls self-identify — that is what makes the `AL(nica fork)→` attribution possible. Tests in `__tests__/wa-history.test.ts`.

### Read-only session peek (`con agent peek`, `GET /agents/peek`)
`con agent peek <session-id|conv-id|name> [--n 20]` — look at ANY session's conversation **without forking, injecting, or disturbing it** (safe mid-turn). Hub route resolves hub id → claudeSessionId → unique name (409 on ambiguity) and reads the transcript off disk via `loadSessionHistory` (the full record — `messageLog` is capped/replay-limited). Built so Al can introspect his conversation-forks ("is there a convo I'm not aware of?" — his AL.md points him at `con agent list` + `peek`); generally useful for auditing any agent conversation.

### Past-session recall (`con agent search` / `con agent read`, `server/src/recall/`) — ^jade-bat
Every JSONL transcript under `~/.claude/projects/` (hub AND terminal sessions, all projects) is FTS5-indexed into `~/.config/console/recall.db` (node:sqlite, ~470 MB for 790 sessions; content ~40 %, FTS the rest). **Why**: the CLI deletes transcripts after `cleanupPeriodDays` (was the 30-day default; set to 90 in `~/.claude/settings.json` 2026-09-11) and the hub had no copy and no cross-session search — the pruned-transcript respawn only *works around* the loss. Design follows olly-styles/corroborator (write-up: `~/sync/brain/root/projects/console/research/corroborator-learnings.md`) but as `con` verbs, not MCP.
- **Indexing** runs in a **forked child process** (`recall/worker.ts`, NOT a worker_thread — tsx 4.x does not map `.js`→`.ts` inside worker threads), one file per IPC request so searches interleave with a backfill (full backfill ≈ 3 min). Boot scan + hourly catch-up (`RecallIndex.scanAll`); a hub session is re-parsed ~8 s after each `result`/`session_ended` (`AgentContext.recall.touch`, wired in `routes/agents.ts`). Unchanged files (size+mtime) are skipped. Worker dying 3× within 10 s of spawn disables recall until restart. `CONSOLE_RECALL=0` disables it.
- **Parser** (`recall/parse.ts`) is the only file that knows the record shapes: a turn = one human prompt (`isSidechain`/`isMeta`/`isCompactSummary`/tool-result/`<tag>`-prefixed records skipped, `<command-name>` → `/name args`) + every assistant text block + tool events paired by `tool_use_id`. Results capped 8k (20k for WebFetch/WebSearch/Agent). `recall/redact.ts` scrubs key/JWT/private-key/Authorization/URL-credential/`password=` shapes; `Read`s of `.env`/key/credential paths become a placeholder. Schema bump (`SCHEMA_VERSION` in `recall/db.ts`) drops and rebuilds from disk.
- **Routes**: `GET /agents/recall/search|read|stats` (`routes/recall.ts`). The caller's `X-Console-Agent` maps to its live session, which is excluded from results (`--self` to include). Every call is appended to `~/.config/console/recall-qlog.jsonl` (agentKey, session, cwd, query, hits, ms) — the evidence for the 3-week review (2026-10-02, verdict KEEP + IMPROVE on the console board: 238 calls from 53 sessions, 63% search→read follow-through, three verified cases where the result changed the action). **Never put FTS5 `snippet()`/`highlight()` in the ranking query**: SQLite evaluates them for every MATCH row before ORDER BY/LIMIT and re-tokenises the whole document each time — "board" matched 33,850 tool results and took 124 s (qlog median 12 s, p90 575 s). `runMatch` ranks on `bm25()` alone and builds excerpts in JS (`excerpt()`/`termsOf()` in `query.ts`) for the 600 survivors; the tools index is consulted only with `--tools` or when turn hits cannot fill the page (db828149: 538 s → 0.2 s). **Two worker processes, never one** (55460199): `WorkerSlot('index')` owns the writable connection (`index`/`needsIndex`), `WorkerSlot('query')` a read-only one (`openReadDb`, spawned lazily on the first search/read/stats) — a re-index rewrites a whole session in one transaction and on the 217 MB Astera general transcript that held the single worker 30–40 min, queueing every search behind it (median 21 min end to end after db828149). WAL lets the reader serve the last committed snapshot meanwhile. `--since` accepts `h` as well as d/w/m/y. **A changed transcript is APPENDED, not rebuilt** (cb753f8c): `upsertSession` compares the stored `(idx, uuid)` list against the parse — a verbatim prefix means the file only grew (the CLI only ever appends; a compaction appends a boundary + summary), so it deletes/rewrites from the last indexed turn onward (that turn is ALWAYS redone: it may have been mid-write when indexed) and rebuilds `files` whole, since those rows are per-path COUNTS that can't be appended without double-counting the redone turn. A uuid mismatch, a shorter parse, or a new `source_path` still rebuilds the session. Measured on a copy of the live 2.2 GB index with the 217 MB Astera general transcript: 70,157 ms → 58 ms of writes (parse is then the whole cost, ~6.5 s). `upsertSession`/`indexFile` return `{mode: 'append'|'full', kept, wrote}` and the scan line reports `(N appended, M rebuilt)`. NB a full `INSERT INTO turns_fts(turns_fts) VALUES('integrity-check')` kills the process on an index this size — check consistency with row counts (`turns` vs `turns_fts` per session) and a scoped `MATCH` probe instead.
- **Read ladder** (`recall/render.ts`): `read <s8>` clipped overview → `read <s8> --grep re` (lines as `t<idx> <uuid8> <date> <role>: …`) → `read <s8>/<uuid8>` full turn + `cite:` line → `read <s8>/t<idx>#<seq>` tool result. Turn uuids are the JSONL record uuids, so addresses survive re-indexing. Agent-facing method + quoting rule: `~/.claude/skills/recall/SKILL.md` and the "Recalling past sessions" section of `~/CLAUDE.md`.


## Known Issues
- Matrix E2EE: new device needs key backup restore (`POST /matrix/keys/restore-from-recovery-key`) before old messages decrypt
- Matrix E2EE: hub device must be cross-signed or Beeper bridges silently drop encrypted messages with `com.beeper.undecryptable_event`. Run `POST /matrix/keys/restore-cross-signing` after a hub re-login.
- `bootstrapCrossSigning(true)` overwrites server keys — always restore keys FIRST (the route above uses `reset:false`)
- WASM `UserId` objects consumed per call — create fresh instances each time

## Matrix recovery workflow (hub-side)
The browser "recovery key" flow is gone; recovery is hub-side (`server/src/matrix/backup-restore.ts` + `secret-storage.ts`): `POST /matrix/keys/restore-from-recovery-key`, `POST /matrix/keys/restore-cross-signing` (required after ANY hub re-login — bridges reject Olm from uncross-signed devices), `POST /matrix/keys/import-local-backup`, `POST /matrix/decrypt-event`. **"🔒 Encrypted message" placeholders in one room = a withheld Megolm session**; the fix needs NO user-supplied key (the hub's own seed in `matrix-crypto-snapshot.json` re-encodes to a valid `EsU…` key). The step-by-step runbook, the SSSS/keyId gotchas and the withheld-session recovery are the **`matrix-recovery` skill** (`/matrix-recovery`, user-invocable) — invoke it when messages won't decrypt or bridges drop sends.

## forge — running forks on a remote box (`server/src/forge/`)

Agent forks can run their `claude`, worktree, dev server and builds on an EC2
box instead of this machine. Built 6 Oct 2026 because the desktop was starving:
9 forks on 8 cores / 23 GiB with every worktree on a 7200 rpm disk at 95% full
(44% iowait, 35% CPU *idle* — it was disk and RAM, not CPU). Moving work to the
local SSD was tried first and did not help.

- **Mechanism**: the hub spawns `ssh -T forge … claude`. Its transport to an
  agent is stream-json on stdin/stdout, so ssh carries it verbatim and nothing
  downstream (SPA, message log, cost ledger, interrupts) can tell. The ONE
  spawn point is `session.ts` — one more arm on the `nice` ternary.
- **The agent still makes its own worktree.** The hub never ran `autowt` and
  still doesn't: forge mirrors this machine's paths exactly (an `amar` user,
  `~/proj/code/<repo>`, `~/sync/brain/...`), so the vault's repo symlink
  resolves and no agent-facing instruction changes. Never "translate" paths —
  mirroring them is what makes this invisible.
- **Routing is opt-in and local-first**: board frontmatter `remote: forge` opts
  a project in, a `#local` card tag opts one card out, `#forge` opts one in.
  **Local is also the FALLBACK** — unreachable box, non-fast-forwardable repo,
  refused tunnel all run locally with the reason logged. The board must never
  stall on a sleeping cloud box. The card detail's cloud pill sets the tag
  (board default / local / forge); CLI: `con board <project> local|forge|here "<card>"`.
- **A Console fork on forge has nothing to borrow `node_modules` from.** The
  box's primary clone carries only `cli/node_modules` (`ensureConCli`, for
  `con`); root, `server/` and `src/` have none, so the desktop's "symlink all
  four from main" trick does not exist there. Install what the suite needs in
  your worktree (`npm install` in root, `server/`, `cli/`) before trusting a
  full run: without it 8-9 server tests fail on import or spawn, identically on
  unchanged main, and read as pre-existing breakage (^keen-eel, 9 Oct 2026).
- **Readiness is prepared AHEAD of dispatch** (`prewarmCwd`), because the board
  watcher stamps the card before the *synchronous* dispatch callback runs. A
  cold box used to mean "runs locally this once" — which was EVERY time (five
  cards on 7 Oct), since each dispatch woke the box, never used it, and let it
  idle-stop. Since 840a0e01 a cold box DEFERS instead: `decidePlacement` returns
  `defer`, the session is created with `deferSpawn` (no process, so the card is
  satisfied and its wake is buffered), the prewarm runs, and
  `Session.startDeferred()` spawns on forge ~60-90 s later. Local is still the
  fallback, but only for a box that genuinely FAILED, and the card is noted.
- **Placement is no longer fixed for life**: `con agent forge move <session> |
  --project <slug>` (`forge/move.ts`) moves a running session — prepare, push
  the transcript desktop → forge (`pushTranscript`; without it a `--resume`
  there silently starts a NEW conversation), rebuild its card worktree at the
  same absolute path with its branch and uncommitted changes
  (`ensureWorktreeOnForge`, plain push so an un-folded-back forge branch refuses
  rather than being overwritten), forward a dev port, then `applyPlacement()`
  puts the local process down so the next message resumes on the box. Mid-turn
  sessions move at turn end (`afterTurn`). `--project` takes In Progress card
  forks only: Under Review forks are waiting on Yousef and generals are his own
  conversations, and neither belongs on a box that stops when it goes idle.
  A move's verdict always lands somewhere: the deferred case runs long after the
  CLI call returned, so it logs and emits **`console.forge.moved`**
  (`{session, ok, reason, devPort, worktrees}`). Wait on that event rather than
  assuming — `con listen expect --on console.forge.moved --within 10m`. That
  holds across a restart too: a deferred move is one in-memory callback, so
  `shutdown()` drains the pending set and reports each as a failure rather than
  letting it simply not happen.
- **Adopting a newer model generation on Bedrock is four hand-maintained
  surfaces and two CLIs** (^trim-duck, 8 Oct 2026; the fleet had sat a
  generation behind for two days because nothing watched). Access is rarely the
  blocker: the 5-5 models already answered for `claude-code-amar`; what was
  missing was an owner-tagged application inference profile (`amar-cc-<label>`,
  tags `owner=amar app=claude-code`), the only route to per-person cost
  attribution. Then: (1) `server/src/model-chains.ts` `BEDROCK_CHAIN`; (2)
  `server/src/bedrock-profiles.ts` `STATIC_PROFILES` (a miss bills untagged
  forever); (3) the same file's `aliasProfileEnv` map, which is what subagents,
  compaction and `--model haiku` callers resolve through; (4)
  `src/utils/fleet-models.ts` `BEDROCK_MODELS`, the Spaces picker.
  `bedrock-profiles.test.ts` and `fleet-models.test.ts` derive from the chain
  and fail on a missed surface. **Bedrock serving a model is not enough: the
  `claude` CLI gates on its own bundled catalog first**, so probe with the real
  CLI, with the Bedrock environment set EXPLICITLY, `</dev/null` on stdin, and
  a control model beside it — and probe BOTH CLIs, because forge runs its own,
  usually older `claude` and a forge fork takes the Bedrock aliases whenever it
  is not on a Max login of its own (see `forgeSpawnPlan` below). Haiku 5.5 on 9 Oct: served and profiled
  (`amar-cc-haiku-5-5`), hangs on desktop 2.1.295, `unrecognized_model` on
  forge 2.1.288 — not adopted. The daily guard
  (`~/exec/console-bedrock-model-guard.sh`, cron `ahHcHQs`) scrapes
  `BEDROCK_CHAIN` out of the source with a regex, so renaming or moving it
  breaks the guard silently; and it only counts a model as usable when the
  model ANSWERS (until 9 Oct any output that was not a known rejection passed,
  which woke me for Haiku 5.5 while the fleet was on Max). Live only after a
  hub restart (`reconcilePreset` + `syncBackendSettings` at boot).
- **Test counts in the main checkout were inflated until 9 Oct 2026.** Vitest 4
  no longer excludes `dist/` by default, and `server/dist` (gitignored, built
  30 Aug, run by nothing) held 47 compiled test files: every run in the main
  checkout collected them, tested six-week-old code and passed. "168 files /
  2533 tests" was really 121 / 1948. Both configs now exclude `**/dist/**`.
  If a suite is ever larger in the main checkout than in a worktree, that is
  the question to ask. And **editing `vite.config.ts` restarts the live dev
  server and reloads Yousef's open Console tab** — put test-only settings
  somewhere that is not the SPA's own config, or do it when he is not in it.
- **A remote agent must die with its connection, because every hub-side stop
  is only a signal to the local `ssh` client.** `interrupt()`, `kill()`,
  hibernation, a model / login / backend respawn and shutdown all call
  `this.process.kill(…)`, and for a forge session `this.process` is `ssh`.
  Killing it closes the channel; with no pty sshd signals nothing, so the remote
  `claude` only saw stdin reach EOF and, mid-turn, went on working with nobody
  attached while the hub respawned a second copy. 9 Oct 2026: two restarts, a
  login switch and a backend switch in twelve minutes left 3-5 live copies of
  each session on the box; stale ones re-sent messages, wrote duplicate card
  notes, one landed a PR, and Astera general stopped 38 by hand. Even the stop
  button left a twin. Three defences now, the same three the local side has
  had since 4 Sept (`agents/process-reaper.ts`):
  **(1) the watchdog** (`forge/ssh.ts`, `dieWithConnection`): the agent's stdin
  reaches it THROUGH a small relay process; when the channel closes, the relay
  SIGTERMs the agent and what it is running, and SIGKILLs after 5 s. Measured on
  the box OVER THE MASTER: the tree is gone ~0.4 s after the ssh client dies by
  KILL, TERM or INT; without it, it survives indefinitely. Never set
  `dieWithConnection` on a command whose stdin is closed up front — EOF is the
  signal. **The watchdog only acts when the box SEES the connection close, and
  on a connection that is not multiplexed that depends on HOW the client died**
  (next bullet).
  **(2) the boot reap** and **(3) the 2-min sweep** (`forge/reaper.ts`,
  `reapForgeTwins` in `index.ts`): list the box's agents over ssh, judge them
  with the local reaper's rules, end the stale trees. Stale = `CONSOLE_HUB_PID`
  names a hub that is not this one; the sweep also ends an OLDER copy of a
  session this hub started twice. An unmarked process on the box is never
  judged. Both run only over a master that is already up — a reaper must not
  wake the box or hold it awake.
  The kill is a **ppid walk, enumerated before anything is signalled** (a child
  whose parent just died reparents to init and cannot be found afterwards). It
  takes the agent's live tool calls and background tasks and SPARES anything
  daemonised earlier, so a `run_in_background` dev server dies with its agent
  and a double-forked one does not. Nothing but the agent carries the agent's
  argv (the relay re-execs as `… forge-watchdog <pid>`), so `pgrep -f` still
  sees ONE process per session. To check the box by hand:
  `pgrep -u amar -fa '[c]laude --output-format'` and compare each one's
  `CONSOLE_HUB_PID` (`tr '\0' '\n' </proc/<pid>/environ`) with the hub's node
  pid — and bracket the first letter of any pattern you count over ssh, or the
  counting shell counts itself.
- **A forge session's ssh client is ASKED to exit, never SIGKILLed first
  (`Session.stopProcessNow`), and the box drops a client that stops answering
  (`ClientAliveInterval 30` x 4).** When the master has no session to spare
  (`mux_client_request_session: Session open refused by peer`) ssh quietly dials
  its OWN SSM tunnel ("disabling multiplexing"), and on that connection nothing
  tells the box that a SIGKILLed client is gone. Measured 9 Oct 2026: after
  SIGTERM the remote stdin closed within the second; after SIGKILL it was still
  open 240 s later with every local process (ssh, aws, session-manager-plugin)
  already dead. Hibernate, reload, a model / login / backend respawn and a move
  all used SIGKILL ("instant, nothing to flush" — true of a local claude), so
  every fleet-wide respawn that evening left the old agent working beside its
  replacement: 7 pairs at 22:03, three for 7-13 min at 19:08 (Console
  treasurer's finding). Four things changed, in order of how early they act:
  (a) SIGTERM, with SIGKILL only if ssh has not exited after 2 s; (b) every forge
  spawn books a reap 20 s after the last spawn of its burst (`reapForgeTwins
  ('respawn')`, superseded once the replacement is 10 s old — the sweep's own
  rule is 90 s and its interval 2 min); (c) the box's sshd pings its clients
  (`/etc/ssh/sshd_config.d/98-forge-sessions.conf`, also in
  `scripts/forge/bootstrap.sh`), which covers what no signal handler can: the
  hub itself SIGKILLed, the desktop losing power — the same SIGKILL test after
  the change: dropped at 150 s; (d) `MaxSessions 64` on the
  box (treasurer, 98552659) so sessions fit on the master in the first place.
  **A connection keeps the sshd limits it was OPENED with** — the master too,
  and it outlives every hub restart (`ssh -M -N -f`, 42 h old that night, still
  on MaxSessions 10 three hours after the box said 64). After changing the
  box's sshd config: `sudo sshd -t && sudo systemctl reload ssh` there, then at
  a moment when no forge session is mid-turn `ssh -O exit forge` here and
  restart the hub (its boot reopens the master with both reverse forwards,
  re-forwards every restored session's dev port, and the sshfs mounts reconnect
  by themselves). Idle sessions that were on the old master come back with
  their next message. Open the new master BEFORE the restart
  (`con agent forge up`, then `ssh -O check forge` shows a new pid): a boot that
  cannot reach the box restores its forge sessions LOCALLY, into empty history.
- **Never signal a process on the box by NAME.** Every agent there has a
  watchdog whose blocking child is a plain `cat`; `pkill -f 'cat$'` (written
  into a probe's cleanup on 9 Oct and caught before it ran) would end one live
  agent's whole tree per match. Kill by a pid you recorded yourself
  (`echo $$ > /tmp/x.pid` in the remote command, then `pkill -P "$(cat …)"`),
  and when you count or match over ssh, bracket the first letter.
- **A failed forge exec always says why (`execFailure`, `forge/ssh.ts`).** A
  command its timeout killed rejects with `code: null` and an empty stderr, and
  `''.split('\n').pop()` is `''`, not undefined — so the sweep logged
  `listing failed: ` with nothing after it three times on 9 Oct. A timeout now
  reads `no answer within 45 s (ssh ended by SIGTERM)`; a refusal with nothing
  on stderr names its exit code. The listing's budget is 45 s, because on a
  saturated master it first has to dial its own tunnel.
- **Dev ports come from the sessions, not from a table (`forge/dev-ports.ts`).**
  The dispatch envelope tells a fork its number ("run your dev server on PORT
  5183"), so two forks on one number is two dev servers fighting on the box, or
  one fork's specs answered by the other's server. The old table only grew
  (nothing released a port: dry after 40 dispatches) and was EMPTY after a hub
  restart while restored sessions kept theirs — on 9 Oct, after a day of
  restarts, 7 ports were each held by 2-3 live forks (16 sessions). Now a port
  is taken while a session that has not ended carries it; the map holds only
  reservations (a number promised before a session carries it, lapsing after
  10 min). A restore settles the whole manifest first (`claimAll`, two passes:
  first holders keep, then the rest move) and a session that had to move is
  told with its NEXT message (`Session.nextMessageNote`, persisted in the
  manifest) instead of being woken for it.
- **A forge fork's identity is decided per spawn (`forgeSpawnPlan`), and a
  restart is what proves it.** Two identities exist on the box. (1) **The
  subscription**, when the fleet is on `first_party` AND the box has a login
  for the SAME account the fleet is on: `FORGE_MAX_LOGIN_DIRS` maps the fleet's
  login name to a dir on forge (`second` → `~/.claude-max`, `default` →
  `~/.claude-max-default`). Those logins were made by Yousef ON the box
  (`CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR=<dir> claude auth login`, 9 Oct 2026); a login is never
  copied there. Forge and the desktop then draw on ONE weekly window, so a
  spent window spills or rotates both. (2) **Bedrock through the instance
  role**, for everything else: fleet on Bedrock, no login for that account, or
  a login that just answered "Not logged in" (marked unusable for 30 min, the
  fork respawned). `syncForgeMaxLogins` runs in every prepare: it symlinks each
  account dir's `projects`, `skills`, `plugins`… to the box's `~/.claude`
  (a real dir the CLI made is moved aside as `*.pre-link.<ts>`), writes
  first-party settings, and records the usable ones in
  `~/.config/console/forge-max-logins.json`. **A dir is only ever used once
  its `projects` resolves to the canonical one** — that link is what keeps
  `--resume` working, and its absence is how 15 forks lost their context.
  `session.remoteBackend` says which identity a forge process actually got;
  never infer it from the fleet backend. Forge's `claude` is older than the
  desktop's (2.1.288 vs 2.1.295 on 9 Oct): a first-party id the desktop knows
  is not automatically known on the box. History: until 9 Oct the box's Bedrock
  wiring only existed as a MIRROR of the desktop's `settings.json`, so when the
  fleet moved to Max and the next restart re-mirrored, all 21 forge forks
  answered "Not logged in" for 43 minutes (fixed 30bb8700, which made every
  forge spawn Bedrock; the logins above came later that day). Two things that incident taught about ANY
  restart: it re-runs every prepare and respawns every session on the CURRENT
  backend, so it surfaces whatever changed since the last one — never only your
  own diff; and its check is a liveness probe (a forge fork and a local one
  each ANSWER), not a process count, scheduled before you restart because the
  hub may not nudge you afterwards (`con listen add --once --on hub.started`).
- **"Not logged in" is a failed spawn, not an answer** (`agents/auth-failure.ts`,
  4bc031e8). The CLI reports it as a synthetic assistant message with
  `error: "authentication_failed"`; the session flags itself (`authFailure` in
  `con agent list`, with the count of messages spent into the error — that is
  the re-send list) and the hub raises ONE alert per outage: a log line per
  session, a push, and **`agent.auth.failed`** on the bus. Belt, independent of
  the running hub's code: cron `g9ImVPw` (on Console general; find it by its
  guard if the id has changed) runs
  `~/exec/console-auth-failure-guard.py` every 5 min and wakes Console general
  when any session's latest answer is a login error. NOT built: re-delivering
  the spent messages (needs the manifest, since the cure usually arrives by
  restart). A forge transcript on the desktop is only as fresh as that fork's
  last finished turn, so a fork mid-turn can still LOOK like it is failing.
- **The box holds credentials, and its perimeter is IAM, not a port** (8 Oct 2026,
  Yousef: *"Copy ~/.config/astera/*.env to the box. Treat the box as an extension
  of my PC, tell console to make sure the security is hardened so nobody gets in
  but us, e.g. via tailscale."*). `syncProjectCredentials` mirrors a **NAMED
  allow-list of files** on every prepare (`CREDENTIAL_MIRRORS[].files` in
  `forge/agent-env.ts`; for astera `app.env`, `neon.env`, and since 9 Oct
  `stripe-test.env` — one line, the `sk_test_` key only, split out of
  `stripe.env` precisely so the live key and webhook secrets stay home) to
  `~/.config/<project>/` on the box — outside every checkout, per astera rules
  6/163 — at 0700/0600. **It was his glob until 8 Oct 2026, and he narrowed it
  himself** once the inventory was in front of him: `*.env` had put 41 files on
  the shared box (live Stripe, Xero, QuickBooks, Rippling payroll, Resend, D&B,
  OpenAI, Airtable, Google OAuth), when only two have any box-side consumer —
  `app.env` is every worktree's `.env` (`appEnv`), `neon.env` is read by astera's
  `worktree-db.sh` / `local-gate.sh`, and `land-gate.sh` reads nothing. What
  forced the question: `blob.env` held the REAL prod Vercel Blob write token and
  an Astera fork read it on the box. Three rules that cost something to learn:
  **(1) a credential reaches the box by VALUE, not by filename** — a hash sweep
  of the directory found the same token duplicated in `front-sync.staging.env`,
  so withholding the obvious name would have been a false all-clear; sweep by
  hash (`grep '^KEY=' f | cut -d= -f2- | sha256sum`, never print the value)
  before calling one closed. **(2) `--delete-excluded` is what makes the list
  retroactive** — plain `--delete` deliberately PROTECTS excluded files on the
  receiver, so dropping a name would stop that credential being updated while
  leaving the box's copy in place; with it the box's directory is exactly the
  list, which means **nothing running ON the box may write into a mirrored
  directory** (the next prepare removes it). **(3) A fix on `main` that no
  running process has loaded is not a fix** — the old-code hub re-copied all 41
  files on the next prepare and undid a hand-applied removal within three hours;
  remediation was only real after `con hub restart`. To give the box another
  file: add its name to `files`, restart the hub, `con agent forge up`. The gh
  token is deliberately NOT auto-synced
  (it lives in the desktop keyring, so re-pushing it would reinstall a revoked
  one); its absence is logged instead, and `scripts/forge/install-gh-token.sh`
  installs one — prefer a fine-grained token on stdin over the keyring's, which
  carries `admin:org`/`delete_repo` across every repo Yousef owns. **The repo is
  org-owned: `astera-platform/app`, NOT `yousefamar/astera-app`** — so the PAT's
  resource owner must be `astera-platform` (and the org must have fine-grained
  PATs enabled), access limited to `app`, permissions Contents + Pull requests
  read/write and nothing else. A GitHub App would retire this residual rather
  than shrink it (hub mints short-lived installation tokens, no long-lived
  credential on the box) and installs on the org too; sequence the PAT first
  anyway, because it cuts the blast radius today while the App is built. Minting
  is browser-only — GitHub exposes no API for creating a PAT.
  **There is no CloudTrail trail in this AWS account**, so there is no durable
  audit log of the one path that grants root on the box (`ssm:SendCommand`) —
  only the 90-day console event history, and IAM global events land in
  `us-east-1` regardless of where the box is.
  **"Credentials are ready" is NOT "remote dispatch is safe", and only the owner
  of a project's fork tooling can make the second claim.** Console told Astera
  general it could flip `remote: forge` on the strength of the credential sync
  alone; its own gate — a `worktree-db.sh` that symlinked `node_modules` at a
  desktop-only staging checkout, so every forge fork would have died at its
  first pnpm step on a dangling link — happened to land ten minutes earlier.
  That was luck. State what YOUR half guarantees and ask the tooling owner to
  confirm theirs (Astera general, 8 Oct 2026).
  Two things to understand before touching the box's security:
  - **There is no public port to close.** The security group has zero inbound
    rules and access rides SSM. `ssm:SendCommand` gives **root** with sshd
    nowhere in the path, so whoever holds that IAM action is who can get in —
    and it is also the recovery channel if you break sshd. An inline Deny,
    `forge-box-restricted` on the Admin group, scopes shell/snapshot/SG-change
    actions on this instance to Yousef's own `aws:userId`. Do NOT trust
    `iam simulate-principal-policy` here: it does not populate `aws:userId`, so
    it reports `explicitDeny` for Yousef too. Prove it with a real
    `ssm send-command` instead.
  - **`ListenAddress` in sshd_config does nothing on Ubuntu 24.04.** sshd is
    socket-activated, so systemd owns the socket; `sshd -T` will happily report
    loopback while `ss` shows `0.0.0.0:22`. The real control is a drop-in on
    `ssh.socket` (both are in `bootstrap.sh` so a rebuilt box stays hardened).
  Residual, known and not closed: the box mounts the desktop back as `amar`, and
  that key is `restrict,command="internal-sftp"` so it gets no shell — but SFTP
  is path-unrestricted, so box-root can read/write anything `amar` can. Scoping
  it needs a root-owned `ChrootDirectory` with bind-mounts. Read-only mounts are
  NOT the answer: remote forks write research docs and auto-memory by ordinary
  file writes, deliberately.
- **Path parity covers checkouts outside `~/proj/code` too.** Astera's is
  `/opt/code/astera-app` and the vault's `app` symlink stores that absolute
  string, so a remote Astera fork followed it into nothing. `ensureRepoOnForge`
  now symlinks the desktop's own path (and its `-worktrees` sibling) onto the
  mirror, so both spellings resolve on both machines.
- **`origin` on the box is PER-REPO, and a static mirror is not a valid base**
  (`githubOriginRepos` in `forge.json`; `isGithubOriginRepo`, `baseRemoteFor`).
  Default is `origin` → the local bare mirror, which is right for Console: it is
  trunk-based, does not always push, and its forks never talk to GitHub. For a
  repo whose forks land their own PRs that default is wrong *silently* — astera's
  `land.sh` merges `origin/staging` and takes its gate base from it, so against a
  mirror it gates **green on a stale base**, and its "did staging move under the
  gate?" re-check can never fire because the mirror never moves. Measured 8 Oct
  2026: the mirror's staging was 25 commits / 9 merged PRs / ~6h47m behind
  GitHub's in one working day, because the mirror only advances when a prepare
  pushes the DESKTOP's remote-tracking refs. For a named repo: `origin` → GitHub
  over **HTTPS** (the box has a token, not an SSH key — the URL is derived from
  the desktop remote and converted, never configured, which is why a wrong guess
  about the repo path cannot get baked in), mirror demotes to `mirror`, card
  worktrees base on `mirror/<branch>` because that is where the desktop pushes
  them, and fold-back pushes home not to GitHub. `refs/remotes/origin/*` are
  purged when the URL changes — left in place they keep resolving, so a failed
  GitHub fetch would leave `origin/staging` on the stale mirror commit instead of
  missing.
  `mirror` is DELIBERATE, not residue: it is the seeding remote AND the base
  every card worktree resolves (`baseRemoteFor`) and the target fold-back pushes
  to. Git worktrees share repo config, so it cannot exist on the primary and not
  in a worktree; don't prune it.
- **A box can land a PR and still be unable to show you anything.** The first
  remote Astera card merged a PR and could not take one screenshot: `~/.cache`
  was provisioned 0775 (the desktop's is 0700) and `@swc/core` refuses a group-
  or world-writable directory in its native-binding cache chain without sticky
  protection, so every Next dev server and Playwright run died on
  `ERR_SWC_NATIVE_CACHE`. A/B proven. The message blames *"a parent"* even when
  the cache root itself is the offender, which is what made it hard to place.
  Pinned 0700 in `bootstrap.sh` and re-asserted on every prepare
  (`ensureCacheDirPrivate`), because any tool with a lax umask can recreate the
  directory and the failure is silent and total. **The general lesson: "a fork
  can land work" and "a fork can verify work" are separate claims** — test the
  visual path explicitly, it has its own dependencies.
  It is also a **mode-parity bug class, not an SWC one** (Astera general's
  point): a provisioning step with a lax umask also left `~/.local/share` and
  `~/.local/state` at 0775 where the desktop has 0700, plus five group-writable
  caches. `bootstrap.sh` now sweeps the whole home instead of naming the
  directory that happened to bite. Two things the audit itself must get right,
  both learned the hard way: `-xdev`, or it re-modes the DESKTOP'S files
  through the sshfs mounts; and `! -type l`, because a symlink's own mode is a
  meaningless constant 0777 on Linux and including them reported 4237 false
  positives. Box is at zero real offenders.
  The sweep fixes what provisioning left behind; **`umask 077` in
  `/etc/profile.d/forge.sh` stops new files arriving the same way**, which is
  the same instance-vs-class choice one layer earlier. Found by checking
  Astera's deduction that a card worktree seeds its `.env` from the symlinked
  main checkout: true, and the seeded file landed 0664 — all 33 lines of
  credentials inside a checkout, readable by the box's second login, once per
  card. Agent shells pick the umask up because `remoteCommandArgv` sources that
  profile.
  **That profile covers ONLY the paths that source it, which is narrower than
  it looks** (Homelab, 8 Oct): `/etc/profile.d` is read by LOGIN shells, and
  `ssh forge <cmd>` is neither login nor interactive. `remoteCommandArgv` is
  fine because it dot-sources the file explicitly — so the agent spawn path
  always had 077 — but **`forgeExec` (plain `ssh host command`), `forgePut`
  (scp), `forgeGet` (rsync) and any ad-hoc `ssh forge '…'` an agent types do
  not**, and those are what create repos, worktrees and mounts. Measured:
  `ssh forge 'bash -lc umask'` → 0077 while `ssh forge umask` → 0002, files
  0664. The umask is therefore set in **PAM**, which covers every session type:
  `pam_umask.so umask=077 nousergroups` in BOTH `/etc/pam.d/common-session` and
  `common-session-noninteractive` (backups `*.bak-umask-20261008`). Two traps
  here — an argumentless `pam_umask.so` silently defers to `login.defs`
  (`UMASK 022`), and `umask=077` ALONE still yields 0007/mode 660, because
  `USERGROUPS_ENAB yes` makes pam_umask copy the owner bits onto the group bits
  for a user whose group is private; `nousergroups` is what pins it to 0077.
  **Verify on a FRESH connection** (`-o ControlPath=none`) and with the
  non-login form: a channel multiplexed over the existing ControlMaster
  inherits the master's umask and never re-runs PAM, so live sessions keep the
  old value until their master cycles — which is exactly what hid this.
  No credential was actually exposed (the bearer, `settings.json` and every
  `~/.config/astera/*.env` are 0600 — the transfers chmod explicitly) and
  `/home/amar` is 0750, so a 0664 file was still gated at the home dir. The
  general rule both boxes taught: **a secret's own mode is only the first line
  of defence once the file sits outside a 0750 home** — which is why
  `/opt/code` on the desktop was a real exposure and `~/proj/code` was not.
  **The class, stated once: a mode that is never stated is whatever the umask
  happened to be.** All three instances were provisioning lines that said
  "create this" without saying "at this mode" — `~/.cache` 0775, the
  profile.d-only umask, and `/srv/git` 0775, which let the box's `ubuntu` login
  read a private repo's full source (`sudo -u ubuntu cat
  /srv/git/astera-app.git/HEAD` printed the ref while the same user on
  `/home/amar` got `Permission denied`). `/srv` is the one to remember: it is
  OUTSIDE `$AMAR_HOME`, so the home sweep never walked it. When adding anything
  to `bootstrap.sh`, grep it for directory creation with no explicit `-m` —
  `install -d` and `mkdir` both inherit root's 022 there.
- **`appEnv` gives the toolchain its `.env` without a secret entering a tree.**
  Repo basename → a file outside every checkout, symlinked to `<code>/.env` by
  the prepare. Without it the first remote fork GENERATED dev secrets inside the
  checkout to make the type generator run, which astera's rules 6/163 forbid and
  which the next reader cannot tell from real ones. Inert with no mapping, and it
  never clobbers a regular `.env` (it says so instead — so a stray real file
  blocks the link until someone removes it, on purpose). The source must ALSO be
  named in `CREDENTIAL_MIRRORS[].files`, or it never reaches the box and the
  prepare logs `appEnv source … is not on the box yet` — the mirror is a named
  allow-list now, not a glob that would have carried it for free.
  **The source is left read-only (0400), and that is a safety property.** One
  shared file reached through N symlinks means any write through a link mutates
  every checkout at once, and an *appending* tool is the bad case: astera's
  `worktree-db.sh:96` tops a worktree's `.env` up with
  `grep -v '^DATABASE_URL' "$main_env" >> "$here/.env"`, and when both sides
